  15  
  16  > [!IMPORTANT]
  17  > This document describes **how** the software-level lifecycle will be implemented once its prerequisites are mature. It records no completed activity. Nothing here is evidence of ISO 26262-6 conformity, ASIL capability or safety. The drafts in [`05-software/`](../05-software/), and the safety-mechanism changes integrated under change records, are pre-entry engineering (§2.3), not Part 6 execution.
  18  
  19  ## 1. Purpose and scope
  20  
  21  LionDriver already has draft software work products WP-W-01…WP-W-11, written by back-filling from the inherited openpilot, opendbc and panda code ([WP-M-01 T-03](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring)). What it does not yet have is a controlled way to run the ISO 26262-6 lifecycle on them. That means records rather than prose, enforced trace links, evidence bound to exact builds and configurations, review controls, and a link into the Living Safety Case.
  22  
  23  This specification fixes that blueprint before work starts. It:
  24  
  25  1. sets the engineering principles that govern the software lifecycle (§2),
  26  2. defines entry criteria and records a preliminary readiness snapshot of the prerequisites (§3),
  27  3. specifies each Clause 5–11 activity against the existing repository (§4),
  28  4. defines interfaces to the supporting processes and other parts of the standard (§5),
  29  5. specifies reuse assessment (§6), configuration-specific assurance (§7), traceability (§8), Living Safety Case integration (§9), repository layout (§10), the register extension (§11), automation (§12) and the dashboard (§13),
  30  6. sets acceptance criteria (§14) and the phase sequence (§15),
  31  7. lists what may and may not be done before activation (§16).
  32  
  33  Out of scope: the content of software safety requirements, architecture, analyses or verification results. Those come from the activities this document plans. They are not produced by this document.
  34  
  35  The licensed text of ISO 26262 is authoritative. No standard text is reproduced here. Clause references are to the 2018 edition and must be checked against the licensed copy before use in an audit.
  36  
  37  ## 2. Engineering principles
  38  
  39  ### 2.1 Evidence, not documentation theatre
  40  
  41  | Rule | Consequence in this repository |
  42  |---|---|
  43  | A template is not evidence | Templates live in a template location (§10) and carry no status. A work product copied from a template starts at `Planned` or `Skeleton` |
  44  | A passing CI run is not approval | CI results are evidence inputs. They never change a `review_state` ([`case/README.md` §4](../case/README.md)) |
  45  | A test is not verification of a requirement | A requirement is verified only when a reviewed verification record links that requirement to a result on an identified build and configuration (§8) |
  46  | No invented numbers | Coverage, timing, resource figures and metrics appear only when measured, with the build, tool version and command recorded. Otherwise the record says `Not established` |
  47  | Drafts stay drafts | AI- or tool-generated content is marked as such and stays below `Approved` until a named human reviewer accepts it (§2.5) |
  48  
  49  ### 2.2 Status vocabulary
  50  
  51  The specification calls for more statuses than the current work product convention ([`assurance/README.md`](../README.md): Skeleton, Draft, In review, Approved, Baselined). The proposed mapping below is adopted in Phase B (§15). It is not applied to existing documents yet.
  52  
  53  | Specification status | Current convention | Requirement `status` ([WP-P-06](../07-supporting/WP-P-06-requirements-management-traceability.md)) | Case record ([schema](../case/schema/case-schema.json)) | Meaning |
  54  |---|---|---|---|---|
  55  | Planned | (none; propose adding) | — | — | Identified in the register; no content |
  56  | Draft | Skeleton / Draft | `proposed` | `draft` | Content exists; not reviewed |
  57  | In development | Draft | `agreed` → `implemented` | `in-progress` | Being worked against an approved input |
  58  | In review | In review | — | `review-required` / `in-review` | Verification review open |
  59  | Approved | Approved | `agreed` | `accepted` | Review passed at the independence level required by [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) |
  60  | Verified | (none; propose adding) | `verified` | evidence `available` + review `accepted` | Approved, and its verification records are accepted for a named baseline and configuration |
  61  | Superseded | (none; propose adding) | `withdrawn` (requirements) | baseline `superseded` | Replaced; kept for history |
  62  | Not applicable | N/A (tailored) | `withdrawn` | `withdrawn` | Tailored out, with rationale ([WP-M-01 §5](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring)) |
  63  
  64  Allowed transitions are checked by tooling (§12). Leaving out a state is not allowed. For example, a record cannot go from `Draft` to `Verified`.
  65  
  66  ### 2.3 Pre-entry engineering track
  67  
  68  Software-level engineering is already under way before activation. At `682f184`:
  69  
  70  - WP-W-01…WP-W-11 are written, and WP-W-02 holds 134 SWSRs that refine the WP-S-02 TSRs. They are mirrored in [`trace/items/swsr.yaml`](../trace/items/swsr.yaml) and checked in CI (K3, K4, X3; [`trace/README.md` §6](../trace/README.md#6-consistency-checks));
  71  - safety mechanisms derived from proposed TSRs and SWSRs (hardware watchdog, RX timeout, TX whitelist, safety-mode lock, heartbeat, IRQ priorities, EPS fault, cruise-main check, TX rate supervision) are integrated in the reference build. Each has a CT-1 change record in [`07-supporting/impact/`](../07-supporting/impact/README.md), and none is verified on hardware or independently reviewed;
  72  - a design note ([DN-01](../05-software/design-notes/DN-01-tx-rate-supervision.md)) and a HIL bench plan ([`hil-bench/`](../05-software/hil-bench/README.md), D-04) exist.
  73  
  74  This work is legitimate engineering, and it is what makes activation possible. It is governed by these rules until D-14. The project maintainer confirmed them as written on 2026-10-12 ([RR-2026-009](../05-software/reviews/RR-2026-009.md) POL-1, decided by delegation "go with your recommendation"):
  75  
  76  | Rule | Consequence |
  77  |---|---|
  78  | P-01 | Every SWSR keeps `status: proposed` until its parent TSR is approved (CR-05) **and** the SWSR has a recorded review. Being implemented in a build is shown in the `Baseline` column (`Impl`, `Partial`, `Not impl`), not by moving `status` to `implemented` |
  79  | P-02 | A code change in an SR-A or SR-Q path needs a CT-1 change record with an impact analysis ([WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md)), whether or not Part 6 is active |
  80  | P-03 | Text describing behaviour names the pins it was read from. When a change is integrated, the work products that describe the old behaviour are updated in the same pull request or carry an open item that names the change |
  81  | P-04 | Work products and change records do not describe pre-entry work as Part 6 completion, verification or approval |
  82  | P-05 | At Phase A, every pre-entry artifact is rechecked against the entry baseline and becomes an **input** to Clauses 6–11. Nothing carries over as completed evidence |
  83  
  84  ### 2.4 Upstream compatibility
  85  
  86  LionDriver keeps upstream openpilot behaviour and the upstream vehicle list unless a safety requirement forces a change. Part 6 work must not restructure application code for documentation convenience. After activation, code changes come only from approved software safety requirements, through [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) change requests. Before activation they follow the pre-entry rules of §2.3 (CT-1 change record with impact analysis). Each change records its effect on upstream functionality and on configurations outside the claimed scope. Supporting a vehicle is not the same as having assurance evidence for it (§7).
  87  
  88  ### 2.5 Human-controlled decisions
  89  
  90  | Activity | Automation may | Only an authorized human may |
  91  |---|---|---|
  92  | Requirements | Check syntax, attributes, uniqueness, links; propose wording | Accept, allocate an ASIL, approve |
  93  | Trace links | Propose candidate links, flagged `proposed-by: tool` | Record a link as approved |
  94  | Analyses (FMEA, FTA, DFA, FFI) | Generate worksheets, check completeness | Rate, conclude, approve |
  95  | Verification | Run tests, collect results, compute coverage | Judge pass/fail of a requirement, accept anomalies, approve reports |
  96  | Safety case | Compute status, flag stale evidence | Accept a claim, change a review state |
  97  
  98  Reviewers and independence levels are those of [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) and [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md).
  99

 123  
 124  Missing upstream inputs are **not** to be filled in by the software lifecycle. If a TSR, ASIL or allocation is missing, the gap is recorded as an open action against the system-level owner. No SWSR is invented to cover it.

 150  ## 4. Lifecycle specification, Clauses 5–11
 151  
 152  For each clause, the table gives the work products in two classes:
 153  
 154  - **Principal**: work products the standard expects for the clause (check the exact list and the ASIL-dependent tables against the licensed copy);
 155  - **Supporting**: engineering artifacts LionDriver uses to produce or support the principal work products. Not every supporting artifact is an individually required ISO 26262 work product.
 156  
 157  Existing homes are given so nothing is duplicated. "Exists at baseline" says what is in the repository today. It never means that the activity is done.
 158  
 159  ### 4.1 Clause 5 — General topics for software development
 160  
 161  **Objective (paraphrased):** a suitable, controlled development environment, a defined lifecycle, and appropriate languages, guidelines, methods and tools for the ASIL.
 162  
 163  | Class | Artifact | Home | Exists at baseline |
 164  |---|---|---|---|
 165  | Principal | Documentation of the software development environment, including guidelines and methods | [WP-W-01](../05-software/WP-W-01-software-development-environment-guidelines.md) | Draft |
 166  | Supporting | Software lifecycle definition and development procedures | WP-W-01 + this document §15 | Partial |
 167  | Supporting | Language selection rationale (C for the safety MCU; Python, C++ for the QM host) | WP-W-01 §2 | Draft |
 168  | Supporting | Coding guidelines (MISRA C:2012 for envelope C; Python and C++ host guidance) and design guidelines | WP-W-01 §5–6, §10–12 | Draft |
 169  | Supporting | Deviation register | WP-W-01 §9 (`DEV-` IDs) | Draft; DEV-01…DEV-06 records exist for the global MISRA suppressions, rationale still TBD (WP-W-01 OI-2) |
 170  | Supporting | Toolchain inventory, compiler and build configuration records | WP-W-01 §7; [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md) | Draft |
 171  | Supporting | Software dependency inventory (SBOM) | [WP-W-11 §5](../05-software/WP-W-11-cybersecurity-implementation-verification.md); submodule pins in [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md) | Planned |

 174  | Supporting | Configuration and calibration data approach | [WP-W-09](../05-software/WP-W-09-configuration-calibration-data.md) | Draft |
 175  | Supporting | Development environment verification records | New record set (§10) | Not established |

 179  Phase A must inspect the actual toolchain (compiler versions and flags, the `ALLOW_DEBUG` test build in GAP-41, the mutable cppcheck branch in GAP-34) instead of assuming that upstream practice is enough for the allocated ASIL.

 181  ### 4.2 Clause 6 — Specification of software safety requirements
 182  
 183  **Objective (paraphrased):** derive software safety requirements from the technical safety requirements and system design, refine the HSI, and verify that the requirements are consistent and complete.
 184  
 185  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 186  |---|---|---|
 187  | Principal | Software safety requirements specification | [WP-W-02](../05-software/WP-W-02-software-safety-requirements.md) (document) + `trace/items/swsr.yaml` (records) |
 188  | Principal | Refined hardware-software interface specification | [WP-S-05](../03-system/WP-S-05-hsi-specification.md) refined; software part in WP-W-02 §11 |
 189  | Supporting | TSR-to-software allocation | `trace/items/tsr.yaml` `allocated_to` |
 190  | Supporting | ASIL attribute and allocation rationale; timing, fault detection, fault reaction, degraded-operation and interface constraints | Attributes on each SWSR record |
 191  | Supporting | Configuration applicability | `applies_to` attribute (§7) |
 192  | Supporting | Verification method and acceptance criteria | Attributes on each SWSR record |
 193  | Supporting | Requirements quality analysis | Checker output + review record |
 194  | Supporting | Review and approval evidence | `case/reviews/` records linked to the SWSR baseline |
 195  
 196  **SWSR record.** Extends the [WP-P-06](../07-supporting/WP-P-06-requirements-management-traceability.md) attribute set. Proposed fields (final schema in Phase B):
 197  
 198  | Field | Required | Notes |
 199  |---|---|---|
 200  | `id` | Yes | `SWSR-<nnn>[a]`; never reused |
 201  | `title`, `text` | Yes | One requirement per record |
 202  | `parents` | Yes | TSR IDs. A SWSR without an approved parent is `proposed` only |
 203  | `rationale` | Yes | |
 204  | `asil` | Yes | Inherited or decomposed; decomposition needs a [WP-A-01](../08-analyses/WP-A-01-asil-decomposition.md) reference |
 205  | `allocated_to` | Yes | Architecture element ID(s) (§4.3) |
 206  | `applies_to` | Yes | Configuration IDs or a platform scope (§7) |
 207  | `category` | Yes | functional, timing, fault-detection, fault-reaction, degraded-mode, interface, data |
 208  | `verification_method`, `acceptance_criteria` | Yes | |
 209  | `status` | Yes | Per §2.2 |
 210  | `version`, `baseline` | Yes | |
 211  | `review_ref`, `approved_by`, `approved_date` | When approved | Must point to a review record |
 212  | `proposed_by` | When tool-generated | `tool` or `ai`; blocks approval until a human review is recorded |
 213  
 214  **Current state.** `swsr.yaml` at `682f184` is generated from WP-W-02 by `trace/tools/sync_requirements.py` and carries `id`, `text`, `asil`, `parents`, `allocated_to`, `verification`, `acceptance`, `baseline_impl`, `status` and `source`. Compared with the table above:
 215  
 216  | Field | State |
 217  |---|---|
 218  | `id`, `text`, `parents`, `asil`, `allocated_to`, `status` | Present |
 219  | `verification_method`, `acceptance_criteria` | Present in §3–§9 tables. **Empty for the 11 SW-HW interface SWSRs of WP-W-02 §11**, whose table has no such columns |
 220  | `title`, `rationale`, `category` | Missing; rationale is only in WP-S-02 for the parent TSR |
 221  | `applies_to` | Missing. All SWSRs implicitly concern CFG-001; shared code (e.g. `lateral.h`) is not marked |
 222  | `version`, `baseline` | Missing per record; the WP-W-02 header carries one version for all |
 223  | `review_ref`, `approved_by`, `approved_date` | Missing; no review records exist |
 224  | `proposed_by` | Missing. Every commit that wrote WP-W-02 carries a Claude co-author trailer, so all 134 SWSRs are AI-assisted drafts. Record this per requirement before any review |
 225  
 226  Adding the missing fields is Phase B work. It does not block pre-entry engineering.
 227  
 228  ### 4.3 Clause 7 — Software architectural design
 229  
 230  **Objective (paraphrased):** an architecture that meets the software safety requirements, supports safety analysis, and is verified against them.
 231  
 232  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 233  |---|---|---|
 234  | Principal | Software architectural design specification | [WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md) + `trace/items/arch.yaml` (element records, proposed) |
 235  | Principal | Software safety analysis report | [WP-W-04](../05-software/WP-W-04-software-safety-analysis.md) |
 236  | Principal (where applicable) | Dependent failures analysis report, software level | WP-W-04 §4, consistent with [WP-A-03](../08-analyses/WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md) |
 237  | Supporting | Component decomposition, interfaces, data-flow, control-flow, scheduling and execution models | WP-W-03 §3–5; diagrams as code |
 238  | Supporting | Timing and resource analysis, memory allocation analysis | WP-W-03 §7 (estimates; **to be measured**) |
 239  | Supporting | Safety mechanism design, fault-handling architecture | WP-W-03, [WP-W-05 §6](../05-software/WP-W-05-software-unit-design.md) |
 240  | Supporting | Freedom-from-interference analysis | WP-W-03 §6, consistent with [WP-A-02](../08-analyses/WP-A-02-coexistence-freedom-from-interference.md) |
 241  | Supporting | Software FMEA, software FTA where useful | WP-W-04 §3; FTA links to [WP-A-04](../08-analyses/WP-A-04-system-fta-fmea.md) |
 242  | Supporting | Design rationale; architectural verification and review records | WP-W-03 §8; `case/reviews/` |
 243  
 244  The architecture describes the code as it is: the opendbc safety layer and panda firmware (E-03) and the host stack (E-01). An element is safety-related only through an allocated SWSR or an interference path found by the FFI analysis. Target-state changes (for example the fault → safe-state reaction, GAP-08) are recorded as change requests against approved SWSRs, never as current architecture. Every architecture change is assessed for its effect on upstream functionality and on all supported platforms that share the changed code (for example `opendbc/safety/lateral.h` is shared across brands).
 245  
 246  ### 4.4 Clause 8 — Software unit design and implementation
 247  
 248  **Objective (paraphrased):** unit designs and code that implement the allocated requirements and follow the design and coding guidelines, in a form that can be verified.
 249  
 250  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 251  |---|---|---|
 252  | Principal | Software unit design specification | [WP-W-05](../05-software/WP-W-05-software-unit-design.md) + `trace/items/units.yaml` (proposed) |
 253  | Principal | Software unit implementation | Source at a pinned submodule commit (`opendbc_repo`, `panda`) |
 254  | Supporting | Unit interfaces, algorithms, state machines (WP-W-05 §5), design constraints | WP-W-05 |
 255  | Supporting | Coding-rule compliance records, static analysis findings, complexity analysis | CI artifacts bound to a build ID (§8.3) |
 256  | Supporting | Generated code records | WP-W-01 §11 (capnp, acados and tinygrad outputs; QM unless allocated) |
 257  | Supporting | Code review evidence, implementation trace tags | PR reviews; requirement tags in code per WP-P-06 |
 258  | Supporting | Controlled software baselines | `case/baselines/`, release tags ([WP-P-10](../07-supporting/WP-P-10-release-management.md)) |
 259  
 260  **Inherited code.** Each reused unit gets a reuse assessment (§6). Wide deployment, test coverage and community use count as supporting information, never as design or verification evidence ([WP-M-01 T-06](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring)).
 261  
 262  ### 4.5 Clause 9 — Software unit verification
 263  
 264  **Objective (paraphrased):** show that units meet their design and requirements, contain no unintended functionality, and are verified with the methods and structural coverage the ASIL calls for.
 265  
 266  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 267  |---|---|---|
 268  | Principal | Software verification specification (unit level) | [WP-W-06 §6](../05-software/WP-W-06-software-unit-verification.md) (`VS-UV-` cases) |
 269  | Principal | Software verification report (unit level) | WP-W-06 §8 + result records (§8.3) |
 270  | Supporting | Strategy; test specifications, cases, harnesses and scripts | WP-W-06 §3; `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/tests/` |
 271  | Supporting | Requirements-based, boundary, robustness and fault-injection tests | `VS-UV-` cases linked to SWSRs |
 272  | Supporting | Static verification results | MISRA and cppcheck outputs per build |
 273  | Supporting | Structural coverage: statement, branch, MC/DC where the ASIL calls for it; coverage gap analysis | Coverage records per build (§8.3) |
 274  | Supporting | Execution records, anomaly records, corrective actions | Result records; [WP-P-03](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md) |
 275  | Supporting | Verification review and approval | `case/reviews/` |
 276  
 277  Methods and coverage objectives are chosen from the ASIL-dependent tables of the licensed standard for the **approved** ASIL of each unit. Today only line coverage is measured, on host builds with `ALLOW_DEBUG` (GAP-13, GAP-41). No branch or MC/DC figure exists and none may be shown until one is measured. Passing unit tests does not show that the software is safe.
 278  
 279  ### 4.6 Clause 10 — Software integration and verification
 280  
 281  **Objective (paraphrased):** integrate the software units in a defined sequence and show that the integrated software meets the architectural design, interfaces, timing and resource constraints, and that the safety mechanisms work.
 282  
 283  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 284  |---|---|---|
 285  | Principal | Refined software verification specification (integration) | [WP-W-07 §5](../05-software/WP-W-07-software-integration-verification.md) (`VS-SWI-`) |
 286  | Principal | Integrated embedded software | Firmware and host build identified by build ID and commit set |
 287  | Principal | Refined software verification report (integration) | WP-W-07 §8 + result records |
 288  | Supporting | Integration strategy, sequence, dependency records | WP-W-07 §2 |
 289  | Supporting | Integration build manifests | New: manifest per build (commits of all submodules, toolchain versions, flags, configuration ID) |
 290  | Supporting | Interface, inter-component, timing, resource and safety-mechanism tests | `VS-SWI-` cases |
 291  | Supporting | Fault-handling verification | Links to [WP-V-05](../06-validation/WP-V-05-fault-injection.md) |
 292  | Supporting | Regression, anomaly tracking, configuration-specific evidence, reviews | Result records; WP-P-03; `case/reviews/` |
 293  
 294  Every integration result names the build manifest, the configuration ID, the test environment ID and the requirements covered. A result without these is not admitted as evidence.
 295  
 296  ### 4.7 Clause 11 — Testing of the embedded software
 297  
 298  **Objective (paraphrased):** show that the embedded software, in its target environment, meets the software safety requirements.
 299  
 300  | Class | Artifact | Home |
 301  |---|---|---|
 302  | Principal | Refined software verification specification (embedded) | [WP-W-08 §5](../05-software/WP-W-08-embedded-software-testing.md) (`VS-SWQ-`) |
 303  | Principal | Refined software verification report (embedded) | WP-W-08 §7 + result records |
 304  | Supporting | Test strategy, target-environment test plan | WP-W-08 §2–3 |
 305  | Supporting | SWSR verification matrix | Generated from `trace/` (§8); WP-W-08 §6 becomes a generated view |
 306  | Supporting | Functional, robustness, fault detection and reaction, boundary, timing and resource, configuration-specific and regression tests | `VS-SWQ-` cases |
 307  | Supporting | Test environment configuration records | HIL bench record (D-04) |
 308  | Supporting | Execution records, residual anomaly assessments, reviews | Result records; WP-P-03; `case/reviews/` |
 309  
 310  Precondition: a LionDriver-controlled target environment (HIL bench, D-04; GAP-30). Clause 11 testing verifies the software against SWSRs. It is not item-level safety validation ([WP-V-01](../06-validation/WP-V-01-safety-validation.md), Part 4) and does not show that the vehicle is safe.
 311  
 312  ## 5. Interfaces to supporting processes and other parts
 313  
 314  | Interface | Provides to Part 6 | Receives from Part 6 | Owning work product |
 315  |---|---|---|---|
 316  | Part 2 — safety management | Safety plan, tailoring, roles, independence, confirmation measures | Work product status, evidence for the safety case and FSA | [WP-M-02](WP-M-02-safety-plan.md), [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md), [WP-K-01](../10-safety-case/WP-K-01-safety-case.md) |
 317  | Part 3 — concept | Safety goals, FSRs, AoUs | Feedback when software cannot meet an FSR | [WP-C-03](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md), [WP-C-04](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md) |
 318  | Part 4 — system | TSRs, system architecture, HSI, FTTI | Software verification results for system integration | [WP-S-02](../03-system/WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md)…[WP-S-05](../03-system/WP-S-05-hsi-specification.md), [WP-S-08](../03-system/WP-S-08-system-integration-test.md) |
 319  | Part 8 §6 — requirements management | Attribute set, trace model | SWSR records | [WP-P-06](../07-supporting/WP-P-06-requirements-management-traceability.md) |
 320  | Part 8 §7 — configuration management | Baselines, safety-relevant file list | Build manifests | [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md) |
 321  | Part 8 §8 — change management | Change requests, impact analysis | Affected software records | [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md), [WP-M-12](WP-M-12-impact-analysis.md) |
 322  | Part 8 §9 — verification | Review procedure | Review records | [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) |
 323  | Part 8 §10 — documentation | Document control | — | [WP-P-04](../07-supporting/WP-P-04-documentation-management.md) |
 324  | Part 8 §11 — tool confidence | TCL and qualification | Tool list and use cases | [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md) |
 325  | Part 8 §12 — software component qualification | Qualification method | Reuse assessments (§6) | [WP-P-08](../07-supporting/WP-P-08-software-component-qualification.md) |
 326  | Part 9 — ASIL decomposition, FFI, DFA, safety analyses | System-level analyses | Software-level analyses | [WP-A-01](../08-analyses/WP-A-01-asil-decomposition.md)…[WP-A-04](../08-analyses/WP-A-04-system-fta-fmea.md), [WP-W-04](../05-software/WP-W-04-software-safety-analysis.md) |
 327  | Problem resolution | Anomaly process | Anomaly records | [WP-P-03](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md) |
 328  | ISO/SAE 21434 | CSRs allocated to software | Security implementation and verification | [WP-W-11](../05-software/WP-W-11-cybersecurity-implementation-verification.md) |
 329  | ISO 21448, ISO/PAS 8800 | SOTIF and AI requirements, ML lifecycle | Software-side links | [WP-C-06](../02-concept/WP-C-06-sotif-insufficiencies-triggering-conditions.md), [WP-C-11](../02-concept/WP-C-11-ai-system-definition-and-safety-requirements.md), [WP-W-10](../05-software/WP-W-10-ml-engineering.md) |
 330  
 331  Each authoritative record has one owner. Part 6 records refer to the records above. They do not copy them.
 332  
 333  ## 6. Existing software and reuse assessment
 334  
 335  LionDriver's software is almost all inherited. The assessment method lives in [WP-P-08](../07-supporting/WP-P-08-software-component-qualification.md), within the strategy of [WP-M-01 T-03, T-05, T-06](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring) and [WP-M-12](WP-M-12-impact-analysis.md). This section fixes the record each assessment produces.
 336  
 337  | Field | Content |
 338  |---|---|
 339  | Component, source, owner | Repository, path, upstream owner, fork |
 340  | Version and baseline | Pinned commit; upstream tag if any |
 341  | Intended function; safety relevance | Allocated SWSRs or interference paths; ASIL or QM |
 342  | Existing development evidence | Requirements, design, reviews found (usually none) |
 343  | Existing verification evidence | Tests, coverage type, static analysis, mutation; where it runs |
 344  | Operational assumptions | Configuration, platform, environment assumed by upstream |
 345  | Known defects and limitations | Upstream issues, GAP findings |
 346  | Dependencies | Software, toolchain, configuration |
 347  | Gaps | Requirements, architecture, verification |
 348  | Strategy | Re-verify to ASIL (T-03), qualify at QM (T-05), argue non-interference, or replace |
 349  | Additional engineering needed | Concrete actions, with owner |
 350  | Review | Reviewer, independence, date, outcome |
 351  
 352  Assessment candidates, from the safety-relevant file list ([`safety-relevant-paths.txt`](../07-supporting/safety-relevant-paths.txt)): the opendbc safety layer and Toyota mode, panda firmware paths the envelope relies on, the host processes in the actuation path, and QM libraries (msgq, capnp, rednose, tinygrad runtime). Upstream maturity, deployment history and test counts do not on their own satisfy any ISO 26262 objective.
 353  
 354  ## 7. Multi-vehicle configuration management
 355  
 356  The Part 6 lifecycle runs once at platform level. Configuration-specific assurance is added on top of it, so the lifecycle is not repeated for every vehicle. The approach follows the configurable-software guidance of ISO 26262-6 Annex C and builds on [WP-W-09](../05-software/WP-W-09-configuration-calibration-data.md) and the configuration records in [`case/configurations/`](../case/configurations/).
 357  
 358  ### 7.1 Three tiers
 359  
 360  | Tier | Record classification | Meaning |
 361  |---|---|---|
 362  | Upstream-compatible | `upstream-compatible` | Supported by the upstream code. No LionDriver evaluation. **No assurance claim** |
 363  | LionDriver-evaluated | `liondriver-evaluated` | Analysed by LionDriver. Evidence in progress. Today only CFG-001 (Toyota Corolla, 12th generation (E210), TSS 2.0), `in-progress` |
 364  | Assurance-supported | `assurance-supported` | Has at least one claim with accepted evidence ([`case/README.md` §5](../case/README.md)). None today |
 365  
 366  ### 7.2 Records
 367  
 368  | Record | Content | Home |
 369  |---|---|---|
 370  | Common platform component | Shared code (e.g. `safety.h`, `lateral.h`), with every configuration that uses it | `trace/items/arch.yaml` |
 371  | Vehicle-specific interface | Brand safety mode, DBC, messages, limits | `trace/items/arch.yaml` + WP-W-09 |
 372  | Hardware configuration | Device revision, harness | `case/configurations/` |
 373  | Software variant | Build flags, safety mode, `ALLOW_DEBUG` state | Build manifest (§8.3) |
 374  | Calibration variant | Car params, fingerprints, limits, model weights | WP-W-09 `DVR-` records |
 375  | Feature configuration | e.g. openpilot longitudinal, Experimental Mode (D-08) | `case/configurations/` |
 376  | Safety assumptions, constraints, known limitations | AoUs and limits per configuration | `trace/items/aou.yaml`; configuration record |
 377  | Build identifiers | Firmware and host build IDs | Build manifest |
 378  | Verification and evidence applicability | Which configurations a result covers | `applies_to` on every verification result and evidence record |
 379  
 380  **Rule:** every safety claim and every evidence item names the configurations it covers. Evidence for shared code covers another configuration only after an applicability analysis for that configuration has been reviewed. Compatibility does not imply assurance.
 381  
 382  ## 8. Requirements and evidence traceability
 383  
 384  ### 8.1 Trace chain
 385  
 386  | Link | From → to | ID forms |
 387  |---|---|---|
 388  | 1 | Technical safety requirement → software safety requirement | `TSR-` → `SWSR-` |
 389  | 2 | SWSR → architecture element | `SWSR-` → `SWA-` (proposed prefix) |
 390  | 3 | Architecture element → software unit | `SWA-` → `SWU-` (proposed prefix) |
 391  | 4 | Unit / SWSR → verification case | → `VS-UV-`, `VS-SWI-`, `VS-SWQ-` |
 392  | 5 | Verification case → verification result | → `VR-` (proposed), bound to a build manifest |
 393  | 6 | Result → assurance evidence | → `case/evidence` record |
 394  | 7 | Evidence → safety case claim | → `case/claims` record |
 395  
 396  Further links: hazards and safety goals (`H-`, `SG-`), FSRs, safety mechanisms, anomalies (WP-P-03), change requests (WP-P-02), baselines, reviews, and cross-discipline requirements (`CSR-`, `SOTIF-`/`FM-`, `AIR-`). New prefixes must be added to the checker's ID pattern and to [`assurance/README.md`](../README.md) in Phase B.
 397  
 398  ### 8.2 Engine capabilities
 399  
 400  Builds on [`trace/tools/check_trace.py`](../trace/tools/check_trace.py) (checks K1–K4, K12, R1–R4 and X1–X3 exist; K2 partly; [`trace/README.md` §6](../trace/README.md#6-consistency-checks)) and the case validator.
 401  
 402  | Capability | Basis | State |
 403  |---|---|---|
 404  | Missing-link and broken-reference detection | K3 | Exists for SG, FSR, TSR, SWSR, HWSR; `allocated_to` not yet checked |
 405  | Coverage (every SW-allocated TSR has an SWSR) | K4 | Exists |
 406  | Markdown ↔ YAML equality | X1, X2, X3 | Exists |
 407  | Orphan detection (unit without SWSR, architecture element without SWSR) | New | Planned; needs unit and element records |
 408  | Unverified requirement detection (SWSR not cited by any verification case) | New | Planned; a one-off count at `682f184` is in [RR-2026-009 §1](../05-software/reviews/RR-2026-009.md#1-main-result) |
 409  | Stale evidence (result older than the last change to its subject) | New, uses git history of linked paths | Planned |
 410  | Change impact analysis | Extends `tools/sync/sync_report.py` and [WP-M-12](WP-M-12-impact-analysis.md) | Planned |
 411  | Configuration applicability analysis | `applies_to` (§7) | Planned |
 412  | Baseline comparison | `case/baselines/` + git | Planned |
 413  | Review status reporting | `case/reviews/` | Partial (case records only) |
 414  
 415  Trace links are explicit fields in reviewed records. An AI or tool may propose a link (`proposed_by`). The link counts only when a human review accepts it. Links are never inferred at report time.
 416  
 417  ### 8.3 Evidence binding
 418  
 419  A verification result record carries at least: result ID, verification case ID, requirement IDs, build manifest ID (all submodule commits, toolchain and flags), configuration ID, test environment ID, tool versions, execution date, outcome, raw artifact location, anomaly references, reviewer and review state. Coverage figures carry the tool, the metric (statement, branch, MC/DC) and the exact scope measured.
 420  
 421  ## 9. Living Safety Case integration
 422  
 423  Part 6 evidence enters the safety case through the existing records in [`case/`](../case/README.md). The case already enforces that only a recorded human review accepts anything.
 424  
 425  The integration exposes, per claim: the supporting arguments, linked evidence with version and source baseline, applicable configurations, verification and review status, open issues and defeaters, change impacts, and approval history.
 426  
 427  When code, requirements, tests or configurations change:
 428  
 429  1. affected records are found through explicit links (§8), never through text search or AI inference;
 430  2. linked evidence is flagged stale and drops to `partial` until re-run or re-justified;
 431  3. a review obligation (an action record) is created for each affected claim;
 432  4. the previous approved baseline is kept unchanged (`superseded`, not edited);
 433  5. a claim's conclusion changes only through a new review record by an authorized reviewer.
 434  
 435  Automated workflows never accept claims and never display completion or coverage values that were not measured.
 436  
 437  ## 10. Repository layout
 438  
 439  The structure proposed in the source specification is mapped onto the existing tree. No parallel `assurance/software/` hierarchy is created.
 440  
 441  | Proposed location | Existing or planned home | Decision |
 442  |---|---|---|
 443  | `management/software-safety-plan/` | [WP-M-02](WP-M-02-safety-plan.md) + this document | Reuse; no new folder |
 444  | `software/05-development-environment/` … `11-embedded-software-testing/` | [`05-software/`](../05-software/) WP-W-01, -02, -03/-04, -05, -06, -07, -08 | Reuse. Clause-specific records go in `trace/items/` (requirements, architecture, units) and in a results store (below) |
 445  | `software/configuration-profiles/` | [`case/configurations/`](../case/configurations/) + WP-W-09 | Reuse |
 446  | `analyses/software-fmea/`, `software-fta/` | [WP-W-04](../05-software/WP-W-04-software-safety-analysis.md); system level in [`08-analyses/`](../08-analyses/) | Reuse; structured worksheets may be added under `05-software/analyses/` in Phase C if the Markdown tables become unmanageable |
 447  | `analyses/dependent-failures/`, `freedom-from-interference/` | [WP-A-02](../08-analyses/WP-A-02-coexistence-freedom-from-interference.md), [WP-A-03](../08-analyses/WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md), WP-W-04 §4, WP-W-03 §6 | Reuse |
 448  | `traceability/` | [`trace/`](../trace/README.md) | Reuse; add item kinds |
 449  | `evidence/` | [`case/evidence/`](../case/evidence/) for evidence records; raw results as CI artifacts or a results folder decided in Phase B | Reuse + decide |
 450  | `reviews/` | [`case/reviews/`](../case/reviews/) | Reuse |
 451  | `baselines/` | [`case/baselines/`](../case/baselines/) + git tags ([WP-P-10](../07-supporting/WP-P-10-release-management.md)) | Reuse |
 452  | `reports/` | Generated outputs only (dashboard SVGs under `docs/assets/liondriver/assurance/`) | Reuse; generated files are never authoritative |
 453  | `templates/` | New `assurance/templates/` in Phase B | Create when needed |
 454  
 455  Sensitive vulnerability information is not published automatically; it follows [`SECURITY.md`](../../SECURITY.md) and [WP-O-05](../09-production-operation/WP-O-05-cybersecurity-incident-response-updates.md).
 456  
 457  ## 11. Work product register extension
 458  
 459  [WP-M-00](WP-M-00-work-product-register.md) remains the master register. Phase B adds a machine-readable Part 6 register (proposed `assurance/trace/items/register-part6.yaml`, or a `case/` kind, decided in Phase B) generated into WP-M-00 §3.5 so the two cannot drift.
 460  
 461  Fields per record: work product ID, name, ISO 26262 clause, governing process, owner, ASIL applicability, component, configuration, source requirements, required inputs, expected outputs, verification obligations, review authority, status (§2.2), version, baseline, evidence references, open findings, approval record, and **classification**:
 462  
 463  | Classification | Meaning |
 464  |---|---|
 465  | `required` | Expected by the standard for the applicable ASIL, per the licensed text |
 466  | `supporting` | Engineering artifact supporting a required work product |
 467  | `tailored` | Optional or tailored, with a reference to the tailoring rationale |
 468  | `not-applicable` | With rationale |
 469  
 470  The register does not claim that every supporting artifact in §4 is an individually required ISO 26262 work product.
 471  
 472  ## 12. Automation and CI
 473  
 474  Extends [`.github/workflows/assurance.yaml`](../../.github/workflows/assurance.yaml). The jobs stay read-only.
 475  
 476  | Check | Basis |
 477  |---|---|
 478  | Schema validation; missing required fields; invalid identifiers; duplicates | Extend K1, K2 and `case/tools/validate.py` |
 479  | Broken trace links | K3 extended |
 480  | Missing verification evidence for approved SWSRs | New |
 481  | Stale evidence references | New (§8.2) |
 482  | Unreviewed changes to approved records | New; compares against the last review record's revision |
 483  | Inconsistent configuration applicability | New (§7) |
 484  | Missing baseline references | Extend `validate.py` |
 485  | Invalid lifecycle transitions | New (§2.2) |
 486  | Incomplete release evidence | New; input to [WP-P-10](../07-supporting/WP-P-10-release-management.md) |
 487  
 488  Each check produces a machine-readable result (JSON) and a readable summary. The output separates **structural completeness** (records and links present) from **demonstrated safety** (accepted reviews of evidence). A green CI run is reported as "records consistent" and never as ISO 26262 compliance.
 489  
 490  ## 13. Software engineering dashboard
 491  
 492  Extends [`case/tools/generate_dashboard.py`](../case/tools/generate_dashboard.py) and keeps its rules: values come from records, generated files are checked for freshness in CI, and no value is typed by hand.
 493  
 494  - **Design:** the shared assurance figure palette, with [`01-management/figures/fig_m_management.py`](figures/fig_m_management.py) as the source: ink `#0F172A` text on light surfaces; discipline accents SAFETY `#C2410C`, SW `#0F766E`, HW `#4F46E5`; status GREEN `#2E9E6A`, AMBER `#D97706`, RED `#D2553F`. Green only for an approved or baselined status. `generate_dashboard.py` still uses a navy and gold scheme; it is moved to this palette when it is extended.
 495  - **Views:** Clause 5–11 lifecycle overview; work product register and maturity; requirements traceability; architecture relationships; verification results; coverage where measured; open findings; review obligations; configuration applicability; baseline history; links to the Living Safety Case.
 496  - **Drill-down:** lifecycle stage → work products → records → evidence → review.
 497  - **Missing data:** show `Not established`, `Not assessed` or `No evidence recorded`. Never show an estimated percentage.
 498  
 499  ## 14. Acceptance criteria for the framework
 500  
 501  The implemented framework is ready for engineering review when:
 502  
 503  1. all applicable Part 6 activities are in the register (§11);
 504  2. clause-specific templates and schemas exist;
 505  3. SWSRs have controlled, checked trace links;
 506  4. architecture elements link to requirements and units;
 507  5. verification evidence links to exact build manifests;
 508  6. configuration applicability is explicit on claims, evidence and results;
 509  7. change impact analysis lists affected records;
 510  8. human review and approval controls are enforced by tooling;
 511  9. CI detects structural and trace inconsistencies;
 512  10. the Living Safety Case can reference approved evidence;
 513  11. upstream functionality and licence obligations ([`LICENSE`](../../LICENSE)) are kept;
 514  12. no tool output makes an unsupported safety or compliance claim.
 515  
 516  Meeting these criteria shows that the **framework** exists. It does not show ISO 26262-6 compliance. Compliance depends on doing the lifecycle activities, producing the evidence, and having it assessed (CR-06, CR-09, CR-10, FSA).
 517  
 518  ## 15. Implementation phases
 519  
 520  | Phase | Content | Entry condition | Maps to |
 521  |---|---|---|---|
 522  | A — Readiness and gap assessment | Inspect the repository, prerequisites, architecture, toolchain and verification evidence at the entry baseline; produce the Part 6 gap assessment and the implementation plan | D-14 authorizes Phase A; §3.1 E-01…E-05 at least `In review` | Late P2 / G2 |
 523  | B — Work product infrastructure | Register, schemas, templates, status model, baselines, trace kinds, CI checks | Phase A report approved; entry baseline approved (all of §3.1, or justified exceptions) | P3 / G3 |
 524  | C — Requirements and architecture | Clauses 6 and 7 with approved system inputs | Phase B acceptance criteria 1–3 met | P3 / G3 |
 525  | D — Unit design and verification | Clauses 8 and 9 | SWSRs and architecture approved for the units in scope | P3 / G3 |
 526  | E — Integration and embedded testing | Clauses 10 and 11 | HIL bench available (D-04); units verified | P4 / G4 |
 527  | F — Living assurance integration | Status, trace, results, change impacts and approved evidence wired into the safety case | Runs alongside C–E; completed before G4 | P4–P5 |
 528  
 529  Phases advance on approved outputs, never because templates or scripts exist. Phase dependencies on Parts 2, 3 and 4 are carried in [WP-M-01 §7](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#7-phases-gates-and-sequence) and [WP-M-03 §3](WP-M-03-project-plan.md#3-work-breakdown-structure).

  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose and scope
  16  
  17  This plan sets out how functional safety is managed for the LionDriver reference configuration defined in [WP-M-01 §3.1](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#31-reference-configuration-the-only-scope-claims-apply-to): Toyota Corolla, 12th generation (E210), TSS 2.0 (US, ICE, `TOYOTA_COROLLA_TSS2`), one identified comma device revision with panda safety MCU, and a tagged LionDriver release.
  18  
  19  It implements the [assurance strategy (WP-M-01)](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md) for ISO 26262. It does not repeat the strategy. The companion plans cover the other disciplines:
  20  
  21  | Discipline | Plan |
  22  |---|---|
  23  | SOTIF | [WP-M-08](WP-M-08-sotif-plan.md) |
  24  | Cybersecurity | [WP-M-09](WP-M-09-cybersecurity-plan.md) |
  25  | AI safety | [WP-M-10](WP-M-10-ai-safety-plan.md) |
  26  | Project scope, effort, schedule | [WP-M-03](WP-M-03-project-plan.md) |
  27  
  28  This plan is a living document. It is updated at every gate review (§8) and whenever an approved change alters scope, ASIL, roles or tailoring.
  29  
  30  ## 2. Safety objectives
  31  
  32  | # | Objective | Measured by |
  33  |---|---|---|
  34  | SO-1 | Identify the hazards of the item caused by E/E malfunctions and derive safety goals with ASILs | Approved HARA ([WP-C-03](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md)) with I3 confirmation review |
  35  | SO-2 | Show that the safety envelope (opendbc safety mode on the panda MCU, plus the firmware paths it relies on) meets the safety goals regardless of what the QM driving stack commands | FSC, TSC, FFI and DFA analyses; verification evidence for the envelope (WP-M-01 §4) |
  36  | SO-3 | Back-fill requirements, architecture and unit design for the inherited envelope code and verify them to the HARA ASIL | WP-W-02…WP-W-08 approved; traceability complete in [`trace/`](../trace/README.md) |
  37  | SO-4 | Close the high-severity envelope gaps from the gap assessment (GAP-01…GAP-09) or argue their residual risk | Change requests closed; safety case argument |
  38  | SO-5 | Keep every safety-relevant change under configuration and change control, with impact analysis | [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md), [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) records |
  39  | SO-6 | Provide independent confirmation that the work and its results are adequate | Confirmation reviews, audit and assessment per [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) |
  40  | SO-7 | Make no claim that the evidence does not support | Safety case ([WP-K-01](../10-safety-case/WP-K-01-safety-case.md)) reviewed against this plan at each gate |

  44  ### 3.1 Role assignments
  45  
  46  The project has one maintainer today. One person holding several roles is allowed for planning and authoring. It is not allowed where independence is required (verification review of own work at I1 or higher, confirmation measures, QA audits). Roles marked TBD must be filled before the gate shown.

  94  
  95  ### 3.3 AI-assisted authoring
  96  
  97  Added in v0.2. Most work products and most envelope changes to date were drafted with an AI coding assistant working under the maintainer's direction. Rules:
  98  
  99  - An AI-drafted work product or code change is the author's work. The person who commits it is the author for independence purposes (WP-P-05) and answers for its content.
 100  - AI drafting is not a verification activity. An AI-produced audit or checklist run (for example RR-2026-005…008) is an author-side pre-review audit at I0. It gives no review credit at I1 or above.
 101  - Tool classification of the assistant (tool impact, tool error detection, resulting TCL) is open as OI-7 and belongs in [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md). Until it is closed, every AI-drafted change to safety-relevant code is covered by the same verification as hand-written code: CI (MISRA, coverage, mutation), the HIL campaign and human review.
 102

 124  ### 4.1 Early implementation (at-risk work)
 125  
 126  Added in v0.2. Envelope hardening from P3 has started before G0. This is allowed under these conditions:
 127  
 128  1. The change is compiled only when `LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=1` ([WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md)). Since LionDriver #36 the top-level `SConstruct` sets it by default, so the panda firmware flashed on the device carries the changes; building with `LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=` (empty) gives upstream behaviour. CI tests both variants.
 129  2. Each change has a change impact record in [`07-supporting/impact/`](../07-supporting/impact/README.md) and traces to a draft TSR and SWSR.
 130  3. The change is at-risk work. It earns no credit toward G2 or G3 until (a) the TSRs and SWSRs it implements are approved, (b) the change has a verification review per [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) at I1 or above, and (c) the HIL evidence named in [WP-W-08](../05-software/WP-W-08-embedded-software-testing.md) exists. If a later HARA or TSC decision changes the requirement, the change is reworked or reverted.
 131  4. The hold point above still applies. Implemented envelope changes do not permit public-road operation.
 132  
 133  Status at v0.5, 2026-10-14 (none reviewed at I1; no HIL evidence). Envelope changes are compiled in the reference build only; the host changes (`pandad`, `selfdrived`, `controlsd`) apply to every build:

 157  The requirement column names the main TSR only; each impact record lists the full set. A further envelope change, a wrong-length RX frame treated as an RX fault ([opendbc-rx-length-fault](../07-supporting/impact/change-2026-10-13-opendbc-rx-length-fault.md)), is decided but not implemented. The Chill-mode default ([chill-mode-default](../07-supporting/impact/change-2026-10-14-chill-mode-default.md), D-08) is a configuration change outside the envelope and is not listed. The pinned commits are in [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md).
 158  
 159  ## 5. Work products
 160  
 161  The list of work products, their source clauses, gates and status is the [work product register (WP-M-00)](WP-M-00-work-product-register.md). This plan does not duplicate it. Rules:
 162  
 163  - Every work product in WP-M-00 is either produced, or marked `N/A (tailored)` with a reference to WP-M-01 §5.
 164  - The status in WP-M-00 is the authoritative status. A work product moves to `Approved` only after its verification review per [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) and, where WP-M-06 requires it, its confirmation review.
 165  - A work product's `Baseline` field names the commit its content refers to. If the referenced code changes, the change process ([WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md)) decides whether the work product must be updated.
 166  
 167  ## 6. Tailoring
 168  
 169  Tailoring decisions T-01…T-13 and their rationale are in [WP-M-01 §5](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring). The safety-relevant consequences for this plan are:
 170  
 171  | Tailoring | Effect on this plan |
 172  |---|---|
 173  | T-01 Modification of an existing item | Impact analysis (WP-M-12) is a P0 activity; full concept phase follows |
 174  | T-03 Envelope re-verified to HARA ASIL | ASIL activities in P2–P4 apply to the envelope code only; the SoC stack is QM plus SOTIF |
 175  | T-04 COTS hardware | Hardware activities are qualification plus LionDriver-performed analyses, not HW development |
 176  | T-06 No proven-in-use claim | WP-P-09 is supporting evidence only |
 177  | T-08 comma.ai as upstream supplier without DIA | Supplier-side activities fall to LionDriver (WP-M-11) |
 178  | T-09 Independence | Confirmation measures at I2/I3 need an external party (WP-M-06) |
 179  
 180  Each tailoring decision is subject to confirmation review (WP-M-06).

 201  ## 8. Confirmation measures
 202  
 203  Confirmation reviews, the functional safety audit and the functional safety assessment, including their independence levels, timing and records, are planned in [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md). Summary for gate planning:
 204  
 205  | Gate | Confirmation measure (summary) |
 206  |---|---|
 207  | G0 | Confirmation review of this plan, the impact analysis and the tailoring rationale |
 208  | G1 | Confirmation review of the HARA (I3) and the FSC; interim functional safety assessment |
 209  | G2 | Confirmation review of the TSC and safety analyses; interim assessment; first functional safety audit |
 210  | G3–G4 | Confirmation reviews of HW/SW analyses, tool qualification, component qualification; audit |
 211  | G5 | Confirmation review of the safety case; final functional safety assessment |
 212  
 213  ## 9. Safety anomaly handling
 214  
 215  A safety anomaly is any observation that may indicate a safety goal violation, an error in a safety work product, or a deviation from this plan. Sources include CI failures in safety tests, review findings, test and HIL results, vehicle-test events, upstream security or safety fixes, user reports and field data.
 216  
 217  | Step | Rule |
 218  |---|---|
 219  | Report | Anyone can report, through the GitHub issue forms "Problem report" or "Field report" ([WP-P-03 §3](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md#3-intake)), which open the issue with the `triage` label. Security-sensitive reports go through GitHub private vulnerability reporting instead (`SECURITY.md`, [WP-O-05](../09-production-operation/WP-O-05-cybersecurity-incident-response-updates.md)). No-blame reporting applies ([WP-M-04](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md)) |
 220  | Triage | Safety manager triages within 1 working day when any safety triage box on the form is ticked, otherwise within 10 working days ([WP-P-03 §3.3](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md#33-triage-time); targets not yet demonstrated). Classifies as: potential safety goal violation / work product error / process deviation / not safety-relevant, and sets the WP-P-03 category (`cat-*`) and severity (`sev-*`) labels. Every issue classified as anything other than not safety-relevant also gets the `safety-anomaly` label, which is what [WP-M-04](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md) and the release record ([WP-K-06](../10-safety-case/WP-K-06-release-record.md)) query |
 221  | Immediate action | A potential safety goal violation stops vehicle testing on the affected configuration until the safety manager releases it. Any test driver may stop testing at any time |
 222  | Resolution | Through [WP-P-03](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md), with impact analysis through [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) |
 223  | Closure | Closure needs verification evidence and, for safety goal violations, review by someone other than the person who fixed it |
 224  | Gate rule | No gate passes with an open anomaly classified as potential safety goal violation unless the safety manager records an accepted rationale and the assessor is informed |
 225  
 226  ![Figure 9-1: the safety anomaly handling flow: report, triage, immediate action, resolution, closure, with the gate rule](figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg)
 227  
 228  *Figure 9-1.* The steps of the table above in order, each with the rule of its row; the immediate-action step, which can stop vehicle testing, is drawn in red. Generated by [`figures/fig_m_management.py`](figures/fig_m_management.py); CI fails if it is stale. Source: [`figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg`](figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg).
 229  
 230  
 231  ## 10. Progress tracking and gate reviews
 232  
 233  - Each gate G0…G6 has a GitHub milestone. Each work product and each gap action has an issue linked to its milestone ([WP-M-03 §8](WP-M-03-project-plan.md)).
 234  - The safety manager reviews progress against this plan at least monthly and records the result in the milestone tracking issue.
 235  - A gate review is held at the end of each phase. Inputs: WP-M-00 status, open anomalies, open risks (WP-M-07), confirmation measure results (WP-M-06), QA findings (WP-M-05), open items of the phase's work products.
 236  - Gate outcome: `Pass`, `Pass with conditions` (conditions tracked as issues with owners and due gate), or `Fail`. The record is stored under [`10-safety-case/gates/`](../10-safety-case/gates/README.md), using the template there.
 237  - A gate cannot pass if a confirmation review that WP-M-06 assigns to it has not been performed, unless the safety manager and assessor agree a documented deferral.
 238  
 239  ## 11. Safety case planning
 240  
 241  The safety case ([WP-K-01](../10-safety-case/WP-K-01-safety-case.md)) is built incrementally, following the claim structure G0–G5 in [WP-M-01 §3.2](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#32-top-level-claim).
 242  
 243  | Gate | Safety case content |
 244  |---|---|
 245  | G1 | Argument structure (GSN) for G0 and G1–G5; safety goals; evidence slots linked to planned work products; explicit assumptions and AoUs |
 246  | G2 | Envelope argument (FFI, DFA, TSC) with evidence for the architecture |
 247  | G3–G4 | Verification and qualification evidence linked; residual gaps recorded as counter-evidence or assumptions |
 248  | G5 | Complete argument incl. SOTIF ([WP-K-02](../10-safety-case/WP-K-02-sotif-release-argument.md)) and cybersecurity ([WP-K-03](../10-safety-case/WP-K-03-cybersecurity-case.md)) links; input to the FSA |
 249  
 250  Rules: every claim points to evidence that exists in the repository at a named baseline; missing evidence is shown as missing; known weaknesses (for example single-channel hardware, unverifiable OEM ECU behaviour) are stated in the argument, not omitted.
 251  
 252  ## 12. Reuse of upstream openpilot
 253  
 254  LionDriver inherits all of its code from upstream openpilot (gap assessment §1). Rules:
 255  
 256  | Rule | Detail |
 257  |---|---|
 258  | U-1 No inherited safety credit | Upstream claims (`docs/SAFETY.md` mentions HARA, FMEA, HIL tests) are not used as evidence. Those artefacts are not in the repository |
 259  | U-2 Configuration control first | Safety-relevant upstream code is used only from LionDriver-controlled forks pinned by commit (D-01) |
 260  | U-3 Deliberate sync | Upstream changes enter only through a sync change request with impact analysis against the safety-relevant file list (D-02, WP-P-02). Upstream safety and security fixes are monitored and assessed as anomalies (§9) |
 261  | U-4 Envelope code | Upstream code in the ASIL path is re-verified under T-03; it is treated as LionDriver-owned once forked |
 262  | U-5 QM code | Upstream QM code is qualified per ISO 26262-8 §12 at QM or argued as non-interfering (T-05, [WP-P-08](../07-supporting/WP-P-08-software-component-qualification.md)) |
 263  | U-6 Upstream tests | Upstream tests are reused as verification assets after review against LionDriver requirements; their pass/fail results are evidence only when run in LionDriver-controlled CI at a recorded baseline |
 264  | U-7 Fork policy | The comma.ai fork policy in `docs/SAFETY.md` is an organizational constraint ([WP-M-04 §2.3](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md)) |
 265  | U-8 Models | Model weights are pinned per release; any model change is SOTIF-relevant (D-05, [WP-M-10](WP-M-10-ai-safety-plan.md)) |
 266  
 267  Upstream management and the absence of a DIA are handled in [WP-M-11](WP-M-11-upstream-and-supplier-management.md).
 268

This exceeds the SG-02 1 s warning time; TSR-516's ≤ 0.5 s holds only if the 1 s persistence counts as detection time. LKA torque is refused from the first fault frame. A03 AI-generated review has no independent verification credit under the project rules

Authority boundary · Safety manager · G0–G5

WP-M-02:100 · subject 20569e916c93 · SHA256 212d14b034a90bac…

96
97Added in v0.2. Most work products and most envelope changes to date were drafted with an AI coding assistant working under the maintainer's direction. Rules:
98
99- An AI-drafted work product or code change is the author's work. The person who commits it is the author for independence purposes (WP-P-05) and answers for its content.
100- AI drafting is not a verification activity. An AI-produced audit or checklist run (for example RR-2026-005…008) is an author-side pre-review audit at I0. It gives no review credit at I1 or above.
101- Tool classification of the assistant (tool impact, tool error detection, resulting TCL) is open as OI-7 and belongs in [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md). Until it is closed, every AI-drafted change to safety-relevant code is covered by the same verification as hand-written code: CI (MISRA, coverage, mutation), the HIL campaign and human review.

  15  ## 1. Purpose and scope
  16  
  17  This plan sets out how functional safety is managed for the LionDriver reference configuration defined in [WP-M-01 §3.1](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#31-reference-configuration-the-only-scope-claims-apply-to): Toyota Corolla, 12th generation (E210), TSS 2.0 (US, ICE, `TOYOTA_COROLLA_TSS2`), one identified comma device revision with panda safety MCU, and a tagged LionDriver release.
  18  
  19  It implements the [assurance strategy (WP-M-01)](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md) for ISO 26262. It does not repeat the strategy. The companion plans cover the other disciplines:
  20  
  21  | Discipline | Plan |
  22  |---|---|
  23  | SOTIF | [WP-M-08](WP-M-08-sotif-plan.md) |
  24  | Cybersecurity | [WP-M-09](WP-M-09-cybersecurity-plan.md) |
  25  | AI safety | [WP-M-10](WP-M-10-ai-safety-plan.md) |
  26  | Project scope, effort, schedule | [WP-M-03](WP-M-03-project-plan.md) |
  27  
  28  This plan is a living document. It is updated at every gate review (§8) and whenever an approved change alters scope, ASIL, roles or tailoring.
  29  
  30  ## 2. Safety objectives
  31  
  32  | # | Objective | Measured by |
  33  |---|---|---|
  34  | SO-1 | Identify the hazards of the item caused by E/E malfunctions and derive safety goals with ASILs | Approved HARA ([WP-C-03](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md)) with I3 confirmation review |
  35  | SO-2 | Show that the safety envelope (opendbc safety mode on the panda MCU, plus the firmware paths it relies on) meets the safety goals regardless of what the QM driving stack commands | FSC, TSC, FFI and DFA analyses; verification evidence for the envelope (WP-M-01 §4) |
  36  | SO-3 | Back-fill requirements, architecture and unit design for the inherited envelope code and verify them to the HARA ASIL | WP-W-02…WP-W-08 approved; traceability complete in [`trace/`](../trace/README.md) |
  37  | SO-4 | Close the high-severity envelope gaps from the gap assessment (GAP-01…GAP-09) or argue their residual risk | Change requests closed; safety case argument |
  38  | SO-5 | Keep every safety-relevant change under configuration and change control, with impact analysis | [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md), [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) records |
  39  | SO-6 | Provide independent confirmation that the work and its results are adequate | Confirmation reviews, audit and assessment per [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) |
  40  | SO-7 | Make no claim that the evidence does not support | Safety case ([WP-K-01](../10-safety-case/WP-K-01-safety-case.md)) reviewed against this plan at each gate |

  95  ### 3.3 AI-assisted authoring
  96  
  97  Added in v0.2. Most work products and most envelope changes to date were drafted with an AI coding assistant working under the maintainer's direction. Rules:
  98  
  99  - An AI-drafted work product or code change is the author's work. The person who commits it is the author for independence purposes (WP-P-05) and answers for its content.
 100  - AI drafting is not a verification activity. An AI-produced audit or checklist run (for example RR-2026-005…008) is an author-side pre-review audit at I0. It gives no review credit at I1 or above.
 101  - Tool classification of the assistant (tool impact, tool error detection, resulting TCL) is open as OI-7 and belongs in [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md). Until it is closed, every AI-drafted change to safety-relevant code is covered by the same verification as hand-written code: CI (MISRA, coverage, mutation), the HIL campaign and human review.
 102  
 103  ## 4. Lifecycle activities per phase
 104  
 105  Phases and gates are defined in [WP-M-01 §7](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#7-phases-gates-and-sequence) and [WP-M-00 §2](WP-M-00-work-product-register.md#2-gates). This table assigns the safety activities in each phase. Work product IDs refer to the register.

 107  | Phase | Safety activities | Responsible | Key outputs | Gate entry criteria |
 108  |---|---|---|---|---|
 109  | P0 Governance | Approve plans; assign roles; take configuration control of opendbc and panda (D-01, done #3); upstream sync once per minor release with impact analysis (D-02, done #4, [WP-M-11 §5](WP-M-11-upstream-and-supplier-management.md#5-upstream-synchronization-procedure-d-02)); make fork CI meaningful (D-03, done #6 except CR-CI-08); set up review rules, CODEOWNERS, branch protection; impact analysis of the inherited baseline; engage an external assessor (D-06) | SM, PM | WP-M-00…M-13, WP-P-01…P-06 | — |
 110  | P1 Concept | Item definition and AoUs on Toyota ECUs; ODD; HARA and safety goals; driver/HMI misuse analysis; FSC with FSR allocation; vehicle test procedures before any LionDriver-specific road test; initial safety case argument | SM (HARA, FSC), SL (SOTIF inputs), CSM (TARA link) | WP-C-01…C-11, WP-V-07, WP-K-01 v1 | G0 passed |
 111  | P2 System design | Back-fill TSRs from envelope code; TSC and architecture; FTTI and detection-time budget (GAP-06); HSI; FFI and DFA, including the SoC-configures-panda weakness (GAP-09); system FTA/FMEA; ASIL decomposition if needed | ENG, SM, HW | WP-S-01…S-07, WP-A-01…A-04, WP-V-02 | G1 passed incl. HARA confirmation review |
 112  | P3 HW/SW development | HW safety requirements, FMEDA, metrics and PMHF; component qualification of the comma device and STM32H7; SW safety requirements, architecture, safety analysis, unit design; envelope hardening (IWDG, fault → safe state, mode lock, E2E, driver-torque and EPS-status monitoring); unit verification incl. structural coverage beyond line coverage (GAP-13); tool and SW component qualification | ENG, HW, SM | WP-H-01…H-05, H-07, WP-W-01…W-06, W-09…W-11, WP-P-07…P-09 | G2 passed |
 113  | P4 Integration and verification | Build the HIL bench (D-04); SW and HW integration tests on the Cortex-M7 target; system integration; fault injection campaign; fork-owned process-replay references | ENG, HIL bench owner | WP-S-08, S-09, H-06, W-07, W-08, WP-V-03, V-05 | G3 passed; HIL bench available |

 115  | P6 Operation | Field monitoring, safety anomaly handling after release, controlled updates, incident response | SM, CSM | WP-O-04, O-05, WP-P-03 | G5 passed; release record signed |

 124  ### 4.1 Early implementation (at-risk work)
 125  
 126  Added in v0.2. Envelope hardening from P3 has started before G0. This is allowed under these conditions:
 127  
 128  1. The change is compiled only when `LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=1` ([WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md)). Since LionDriver #36 the top-level `SConstruct` sets it by default, so the panda firmware flashed on the device carries the changes; building with `LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=` (empty) gives upstream behaviour. CI tests both variants.
 129  2. Each change has a change impact record in [`07-supporting/impact/`](../07-supporting/impact/README.md) and traces to a draft TSR and SWSR.
 130  3. The change is at-risk work. It earns no credit toward G2 or G3 until (a) the TSRs and SWSRs it implements are approved, (b) the change has a verification review per [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) at I1 or above, and (c) the HIL evidence named in [WP-W-08](../05-software/WP-W-08-embedded-software-testing.md) exists. If a later HARA or TSC decision changes the requirement, the change is reworked or reverted.
 131  4. The hold point above still applies. Implemented envelope changes do not permit public-road operation.
 132  
 133  Status at v0.5, 2026-10-14 (none reviewed at I1; no HIL evidence). Envelope changes are compiled in the reference build only; the host changes (`pandad`, `selfdrived`, `controlsd`) apply to every build:

 159  ## 5. Work products
 160  
 161  The list of work products, their source clauses, gates and status is the [work product register (WP-M-00)](WP-M-00-work-product-register.md). This plan does not duplicate it. Rules:
 162  
 163  - Every work product in WP-M-00 is either produced, or marked `N/A (tailored)` with a reference to WP-M-01 §5.
 164  - The status in WP-M-00 is the authoritative status. A work product moves to `Approved` only after its verification review per [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) and, where WP-M-06 requires it, its confirmation review.
 165  - A work product's `Baseline` field names the commit its content refers to. If the referenced code changes, the change process ([WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md)) decides whether the work product must be updated.

 167  ## 6. Tailoring
 168  
 169  Tailoring decisions T-01…T-13 and their rationale are in [WP-M-01 §5](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring). The safety-relevant consequences for this plan are:
 170  
 171  | Tailoring | Effect on this plan |
 172  |---|---|
 173  | T-01 Modification of an existing item | Impact analysis (WP-M-12) is a P0 activity; full concept phase follows |
 174  | T-03 Envelope re-verified to HARA ASIL | ASIL activities in P2–P4 apply to the envelope code only; the SoC stack is QM plus SOTIF |
 175  | T-04 COTS hardware | Hardware activities are qualification plus LionDriver-performed analyses, not HW development |
 176  | T-06 No proven-in-use claim | WP-P-09 is supporting evidence only |
 177  | T-08 comma.ai as upstream supplier without DIA | Supplier-side activities fall to LionDriver (WP-M-11) |
 178  | T-09 Independence | Confirmation measures at I2/I3 need an external party (WP-M-06) |
 179  
 180  Each tailoring decision is subject to confirmation review (WP-M-06).

 212  
 213  ## 9. Safety anomaly handling
 214  
 215  A safety anomaly is any observation that may indicate a safety goal violation, an error in a safety work product, or a deviation from this plan. Sources include CI failures in safety tests, review findings, test and HIL results, vehicle-test events, upstream security or safety fixes, user reports and field data.
 216  
 217  | Step | Rule |
 218  |---|---|
 219  | Report | Anyone can report, through the GitHub issue forms "Problem report" or "Field report" ([WP-P-03 §3](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md#3-intake)), which open the issue with the `triage` label. Security-sensitive reports go through GitHub private vulnerability reporting instead (`SECURITY.md`, [WP-O-05](../09-production-operation/WP-O-05-cybersecurity-incident-response-updates.md)). No-blame reporting applies ([WP-M-04](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md)) |
 220  | Triage | Safety manager triages within 1 working day when any safety triage box on the form is ticked, otherwise within 10 working days ([WP-P-03 §3.3](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md#33-triage-time); targets not yet demonstrated). Classifies as: potential safety goal violation / work product error / process deviation / not safety-relevant, and sets the WP-P-03 category (`cat-*`) and severity (`sev-*`) labels. Every issue classified as anything other than not safety-relevant also gets the `safety-anomaly` label, which is what [WP-M-04](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md) and the release record ([WP-K-06](../10-safety-case/WP-K-06-release-record.md)) query |
 221  | Immediate action | A potential safety goal violation stops vehicle testing on the affected configuration until the safety manager releases it. Any test driver may stop testing at any time |
 222  | Resolution | Through [WP-P-03](../07-supporting/WP-P-03-problem-resolution.md), with impact analysis through [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) |
 223  | Closure | Closure needs verification evidence and, for safety goal violations, review by someone other than the person who fixed it |
 224  | Gate rule | No gate passes with an open anomaly classified as potential safety goal violation unless the safety manager records an accepted rationale and the assessor is informed |
 225  
 226  ![Figure 9-1: the safety anomaly handling flow: report, triage, immediate action, resolution, closure, with the gate rule](figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg)
 227  
 228  *Figure 9-1.* The steps of the table above in order, each with the rule of its row; the immediate-action step, which can stop vehicle testing, is drawn in red. Generated by [`figures/fig_m_management.py`](figures/fig_m_management.py); CI fails if it is stale. Source: [`figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg`](figures/fig-m02-9-1-anomaly.svg).
 229  
 230  
 231  ## 10. Progress tracking and gate reviews
 232  
 233  - Each gate G0…G6 has a GitHub milestone. Each work product and each gap action has an issue linked to its milestone ([WP-M-03 §8](WP-M-03-project-plan.md)).
 234  - The safety manager reviews progress against this plan at least monthly and records the result in the milestone tracking issue.
 235  - A gate review is held at the end of each phase. Inputs: WP-M-00 status, open anomalies, open risks (WP-M-07), confirmation measure results (WP-M-06), QA findings (WP-M-05), open items of the phase's work products.
 236  - Gate outcome: `Pass`, `Pass with conditions` (conditions tracked as issues with owners and due gate), or `Fail`. The record is stored under [`10-safety-case/gates/`](../10-safety-case/gates/README.md), using the template there.
 237  - A gate cannot pass if a confirmation review that WP-M-06 assigns to it has not been performed, unless the safety manager and assessor agree a documented deferral.
 238  
 239  ## 11. Safety case planning
 240  
 241  The safety case ([WP-K-01](../10-safety-case/WP-K-01-safety-case.md)) is built incrementally, following the claim structure G0–G5 in [WP-M-01 §3.2](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#32-top-level-claim).
 242  
 243  | Gate | Safety case content |
 244  |---|---|
 245  | G1 | Argument structure (GSN) for G0 and G1–G5; safety goals; evidence slots linked to planned work products; explicit assumptions and AoUs |
 246  | G2 | Envelope argument (FFI, DFA, TSC) with evidence for the architecture |
 247  | G3–G4 | Verification and qualification evidence linked; residual gaps recorded as counter-evidence or assumptions |
 248  | G5 | Complete argument incl. SOTIF ([WP-K-02](../10-safety-case/WP-K-02-sotif-release-argument.md)) and cybersecurity ([WP-K-03](../10-safety-case/WP-K-03-cybersecurity-case.md)) links; input to the FSA |
 249  
 250  Rules: every claim points to evidence that exists in the repository at a named baseline; missing evidence is shown as missing; known weaknesses (for example single-channel hardware, unverifiable OEM ECU behaviour) are stated in the argument, not omitted.
 251  
 252  ## 12. Reuse of upstream openpilot
 253  
 254  LionDriver inherits all of its code from upstream openpilot (gap assessment §1). Rules:
 255  
 256  | Rule | Detail |
 257  |---|---|
 258  | U-1 No inherited safety credit | Upstream claims (`docs/SAFETY.md` mentions HARA, FMEA, HIL tests) are not used as evidence. Those artefacts are not in the repository |
 259  | U-2 Configuration control first | Safety-relevant upstream code is used only from LionDriver-controlled forks pinned by commit (D-01) |
 260  | U-3 Deliberate sync | Upstream changes enter only through a sync change request with impact analysis against the safety-relevant file list (D-02, WP-P-02). Upstream safety and security fixes are monitored and assessed as anomalies (§9) |
 261  | U-4 Envelope code | Upstream code in the ASIL path is re-verified under T-03; it is treated as LionDriver-owned once forked |
 262  | U-5 QM code | Upstream QM code is qualified per ISO 26262-8 §12 at QM or argued as non-interfering (T-05, [WP-P-08](../07-supporting/WP-P-08-software-component-qualification.md)) |
 263  | U-6 Upstream tests | Upstream tests are reused as verification assets after review against LionDriver requirements; their pass/fail results are evidence only when run in LionDriver-controlled CI at a recorded baseline |
 264  | U-7 Fork policy | The comma.ai fork policy in `docs/SAFETY.md` is an organizational constraint ([WP-M-04 §2.3](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md)) |
 265  | U-8 Models | Model weights are pinned per release; any model change is SOTIF-relevant (D-05, [WP-M-10](WP-M-10-ai-safety-plan.md)) |
 266  
 267  Upstream management and the absence of a DIA are handled in [WP-M-11](WP-M-11-upstream-and-supplier-management.md).
 268
