A04 Safety integrity ratings depend on unverified OEM/driver assumptions

Evidence needed · HARA owner / vehicle test lead · G1

WP-C-03:154 · subject 20569e916c93 · SHA256 9934f123a47eadb9…

150### 5.1 H-01 Unintended or excessive lateral motion
151
152| HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
153|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
154| HE-01.1 | OS-03 undivided road, oncoming traffic, 60–90 km/h | Departure into the oncoming lane; head-on collision | S3 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **C** | S3: head-on at combined speeds > 100 km/h, life-threatening. E4: undivided roads make up a large share of driving. C2 ⚠: credits AOU-01/AOU-02 (EPS limits LKA torque to an overpowerable level). A supervising driver with hands near the wheel corrects a sustained limited-torque deviation within ~1 s in most cases (≥ 90%). **If AOU-01/02 are not verified: C3 → ASIL D** |
155| HE-01.2 | OS-01/OS-02 highway, adjacent traffic or barrier, 90–120 km/h | Lateral departure into an adjacent vehicle or barrier | S3 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **C** | As HE-01.1. Lateral deviation at speed with adjacent traffic can trigger loss of control or secondary collisions |
156| HE-01.3 | OS-04 urban, pedestrians and cyclists near the lane edge, 30–60 km/h | Vehicle drifts into a vulnerable road user | S3 | E3 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | S3: VRU impact above 30 km/h. E3: urban VRU proximity within the ODD. C2: lower speed gives more time but less lateral margin. If AOU-01/02 fail: C3 → ASIL C |
157| HE-01.4 | Torque while not engaged, OS-01/OS-03, driver steering manually | Unexpected steering disturbance during manual driving | S3 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **C** | Driver hands on the wheel (manual driving), so C2 rather than C3. Same S/E as HE-01.1. If AOU-01/02 fail: C3 → ASIL D |
158

Why it matters: The HARA explicitly increases the steering case from C to D if credited EPS assumptions are not verified. Current lower targets cannot be treated as established.

AI-author preparation: Prepare a configuration-specific AoU verification matrix and failed-assumption ASIL variants; retain provisional ratings.

Closure evidence: Approved HARA, witnessed characterization records, configuration binding and independent confirmation.

Task: TASK-A04 · dependencies: TASK-A06

Original document at the assessed commit (read-only)
   1  # WP-C-03 Hazard Analysis and Risk Assessment (HARA)
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-C-03 Hazard analysis and risk assessment, safety goals |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-3:2018 §6 (and Annex B for E/C classification guidance); shared hazard log with ISO 21448 §6 |
   7  | Version | 0.3 (Figures 2-1 and 6-1 added, generated from the model and this document; 2026-10-13). 0.2 (Cartesian method, 2026-10-12) |
   8  | Status | Draft — **ratings are proposals for review. They are not approved and must not be used to argue safety until the confirmation review (I3) passes** |
   9  | ASIL / scope | Item LD-SDA, reference configuration |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1); confirmation review by an independent assessor (I3) per [WP-M-06](../01-management/WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) |
  12  | Approver | Safety manager |
  13  | Baseline | `3ed97c8` (code unchanged since `8b8c6ae` apart from `process_replay/ref_commit`) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Scope and inputs
  16  
  17  - Item: [WP-C-01 item definition](WP-C-01-item-definition.md) (functions F-01…F-09, boundary, AOU-01…AOU-11).
  18  - ODD: [WP-C-02](WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md).
  19  - The HARA rates **malfunctioning behaviour of the item** (E/E faults). Hazards caused by functional insufficiencies of the intended functionality (perception and ML performance limits) and by misuse use the same hazard list, but they are evaluated in [WP-C-05](WP-C-05-sotif-hazard-identification.md) under ISO 21448.
  20  - As ISO 26262-3 §6 requires, the item's own safety mechanisms (the panda envelope, selfdrived checks, DM) are **not** credited in the ratings. External measures and existing vehicle elements (the EPS's own LKA torque authority, the brake system, the PCM) are credited in controllability **only where an AoU states it**. If that AoU fails verification, the alternative rating shown applies.
  21  
  22  ## 2. Method
  23  
  24  The HARA uses a **Cartesian (matrix) method**, generated with the hara-builder tool. The full matrix is in [`hara/WP-C-03-hara-cartesian.xlsx`](hara/WP-C-03-hara-cartesian.xlsx). Every analyst input is held as readable data in [`hara/hara_model.py`](hara/hara_model.py), and the step counts are in [`hara/hara-cartesian-summary.md`](hara/hara-cartesian-summary.md). For review, every hazardous-event row is also in [`hara/hara-events.csv`](hara/hara-events.csv) (one line per row, with rationale), and the function × guide-word classification is in [`hara/hara-function-guideword.csv`](hara/hara-function-guideword.csv).
  25  
  26  1. **Functions** F-01…F-09 are taken from [WP-C-01 §2.1](WP-C-01-item-definition.md).
  27  2. **Malfunction identification.** All 14 guide words (M01 No function, M02 Stops functioning, M03 Unrequested, M04 Stuck, M05 Excessive, M06 Partial, M07 Early, M08 Late, M09 Too short, M10 Too long, M11 Delayed, M12 Inverse, M13 Erratic, M14 Uneven) are applied to every function: 9 × 14 = 126 pairs.
  28     - Each pair is classified SC (safety-critical), NSC or NA, with a rationale. All 126 rows stay in the workbook (tab 11), so nothing is dropped without a reason.
  29     - An SC pair whose vehicle-level response is identical to another SC pair is marked *subsumed by* that pair and is not expanded twice.
  30  3. **Situation analysis.** Each SC pair that is not subsumed is crossed with 15 operational situations (§3) and 6 weather/surface conditions (§3.1), giving 90 combinations per pair. Situations OS-14 and conditions W04–W06 are outside the ODD and are included as reasonably foreseeable misuse.
  31  4. **Classification of every row.**
  32     - Severity S0–S3, using injury potential to occupants and other road users. S0 is used where the malfunction is **not hazardous in that situation**, and the row records why. This is how combinations are screened out without deleting them.
  33     - Exposure E0–E4 per ISO 26262-3:2018 Table B.2 (duration). The combination of a situation and a condition is rated no more frequent than the rarer of the two:
  34  
  35       | Class | Share of operating time |
  36       |---|---|
  37       | E4 | > 10 % |
  38       | E3 | 1–10 % |
  39       | E2 | < 1 % |
  40       | E1 | very rare |
  41  
  42       The generator's own default exposure anchors differ from Table B.2, so the workbook's exposure tab is corrected after generation (`hara_model.py --postprocess`).
  43     - Controllability C0–C3 for a typical attentive driver; for other road users, their ability to avoid harm. Ratings that credit an unverified AoU are flagged ⚠, and the summary also gives the result with C+1 (AoU fails).
  44     - S and C come from rating rules per hazard and situation (`RULES` in `hara_model.py`), adjusted for the condition (e.g. snow/ice C+1 for lateral hazards). Each rule carries its rationale into the workbook row.
  45  5. **ASIL determination.** The ASIL is computed in the workbook with the ISO 26262-3 Table 4 lookup and checked against an independent computation in `hara_model.py`; all rows agree. An equivalent rule is to sum the numeric class levels:
  46  
  47     | S + E + C | ASIL |
  48     |---|---|
  49     | 10 | D |
  50     | 9 | C |
  51     | 8 | B |
  52     | 7 | A |
  53     | ≤ 6 | QM |
  54  
  55     Any class at 0 gives QM (S0: no ASIL).
  56  6. **Hazardous events and safety goals.**
  57     - The generator groups ASIL-rated rows per (function, hazard) as candidate goals (workbook tab 13).
  58     - These are consolidated per vehicle-level hazard into SG-01…SG-07 (§6), each taking the highest ASIL of its rows. For each distinct worst case, §5 records a representative hazardous event (HE) with full rationale.
  59     - The HE rows in §5 are the governing cases. The workbook holds every combination behind them.
  60  7. **Scope variant.** Experimental Mode (D-08) is not decided, so a second workbook ([`hara/WP-C-03-hara-cartesian_experimental.xlsx`](hara/WP-C-03-hara-cartesian_experimental.xlsx)) adds function F-02X and shows what option B would add (§5.10).
  61  
  62  Rationale is given for every rating. Ratings that depend on unverified AoUs are flagged ⚠.
  63  
  64  ### 2.1 Counts
  65  
  66  <!-- hara-counts:start (generated by hara/hara_model.py) -->

 129  | **H-02** Unannounced loss or degradation of lateral control | F-01 M01, F-01 M02, F-01 M04, F-01 M06, F-01 M11, F-03 M02, F-08 M04 | F-01 M08→M11, F-01 M09→M02, F-03 M13→M02, F-08 M05→M04, F-08 M13→M04 |

 131  | **H-04** Unintended or excessive deceleration | F-02 M03, F-02 M10, F-02 M13, F-04 M03, F-06 M03 | F-02 M07→M03, F-04 M05→M03, F-04 M07→M03 |
 132  | **H-05** Driver unable to override or disengage | F-03 M01, F-03 M06, F-03 M11 | F-03 M04→M01, F-03 M08→M11, F-03 M10→M01 |
 133  | **H-06** Unannounced loss of longitudinal deceleration | F-02 M01, F-02 M02, F-02 M06, F-02 M11 | F-02 M08→M11, F-02 M09→M02 |
 134  | **H-07** Displayed system state differs from actual state (mode confusion) | F-05 M01, F-05 M04 | F-05 M02→M01, F-05 M06→M01, F-05 M08→M01, F-05 M09→M01, F-05 M11→M01, F-05 M12→M04 |
 135  | **H-08** Suppression of the vehicle's stock pre-collision braking | F-08 M03 | — |
 136  
 137  Pairs classified NSC that need a note:
 138  
 139  | Pair | Why not a hazardous event of its own |
 140  |---|---|
 141  | F-04 M01/M02/M04/M06/M08/M11 (DM fails to detect inattention) | Not a vehicle-level hazard by itself. It is a latent fault of a measure that the controllability of H-02 and H-06 relies on. Handled in the FSC (FSR on DM integrity) and in SOTIF misuse analysis ([WP-C-08](WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md)) |
 142  | F-08 M01/M02/M06/M08/M10/M11 (envelope limits or gating not enforced) | Latent fault of a safety mechanism; hazardous only together with an F-01/F-02/F-03 malfunction (dual-point). Handled through the FSC/TSC and the hardware metrics, not rated as a separate event (ISO 26262-3 §6: safety mechanisms of the item are not credited in the HARA) |
 143  | F-06 M01 / F-07 all (missing FCW or LDW, false LDW) | No actuation; the driver is responsible and the stock PCS gives its own warning (subject to H-08) |
 144  | F-09 all (logging, upload, remote access, update) | No kinetic authority. Cyber-induced effects are analysed in the TARA ([WP-C-09](WP-C-09-tara.md)) and map onto H-01…H-08 |
 145  
 146  **H-07 (mode confusion)** was reserved in version 0.1 and rated only through H-02, H-05 and H-06. The guide-word pass on F-05 (M01 missing take-over alert, M04 frozen or wrong engagement indication) makes it a hazard with its own events (§5.7). Its events are covered by SG-02 and SG-06, whose take-over-warning wording already includes a wrong state indication (§6).

E4: undivided roads make up a large share of driving. C2 ⚠: credits AOU-01/AOU-02 (EPS limits LKA torque to an overpowerable level).  172  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 173  | HE-03.1 | OS-05 following a lead at a short gap | Rear-end collision with the lead vehicle | S2 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | S2: moderate speed difference in following. C2 credits AOU-03 (driver braking overrides) and AOU-05 (PCM bounds the ACC acceleration request). Without AOU-05, high-magnitude acceleration is possible → C3 → ASIL C |
 174  | HE-03.2 | OS-06 standstill in a queue, pedestrian crossing in front | Vehicle moves off and hits a pedestrian | S3 | E3 | C2 | **B** | S3: VRU. E3: stop-and-go with crossing pedestrians. C2: the driver must brake immediately; a low-speed launch is limited |

 179  ### 5.4 H-04 Unintended or excessive deceleration
 180  
 181  | HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
 182  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 183  | HE-04.1 | OS-08 close following vehicle, 50–120 km/h | Rear-end impact by the following vehicle | S2 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | S2: rear impacts on a modern car are mostly S1–S2. Severe at large speed differences, but deceleration is limited by the PCM's ACC envelope (AOU-05). C2: the following driver and the ego driver (pressing gas overrides) can usually react. If the deceleration is unbounded (AOU-05 fails) → C3 → ASIL C |
 184  | HE-04.2 | OS-02/OS-16 curve on a wet road (W02) | Instability under unexpected braking | S3 | E3 | C1 ⚠ | **A** | VSC available (AOU-10; if it fails: C2 → ASIL B). Moderate ACC deceleration on wet roads is controllable for most drivers |
 185  | HE-04.3 | OS-08 close follower; driver monitoring escalates to forced deceleration although the driver is attentive (F-04 M03) | Rear impact by the following vehicle | S2 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | New in 0.2: DM is a source of H-04, not only a controllability measure. Same S/E/C as HE-04.1 (deceleration bounded by the PCM, AOU-05). Without AOU-05 → C3 → ASIL C |
 186  | HE-04.4 | OS-13 manual driving, item not engaged; the item commands braking (F-02 M03) | Rear impact by the following vehicle during manual driving | S2 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | E4: most onroad time with the device installed is not engaged. C2 credits AOU-05. The envelope's engagement gating is not credited (ISO 26262-3 §6). Without AOU-05 → C3 → ASIL C |
 187  | HE-04.5 | OS-08 close follower; false FCW provokes driver reflex braking (F-06 M03) | Rear impact by the following vehicle | S2 | E4 | C1 | **A** | New in 0.2 (version 0.1 rated false FCW QM). The warning does not actuate; most drivers glance at the scene and brake moderately or not at all (C1). The model FCW also fires when not engaged (`selfdrived.py:442`) |

 190  
 191  | HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
 192  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 193  | HE-05.1 | OS-10 driver emergency braking while the system keeps commanding acceleration | Longer stopping distance; collision | S3 | E3 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | Credits AOU-03 (the brake system works mechanically and the PCM cancels ACC on brake). Without AOU-03 → C3 → ASIL C |
 194  | HE-05.2 | OS-10 driver evasive steering while the system keeps commanding counter-torque | Evasive manoeuvre impaired; collision | S3 | E3 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | Credits AOU-01/AOU-02 (driver can overpower the EPS LKA torque). Without them → C3 → ASIL C |

 196  | HE-05.4 | OS-10 brake or cancel releases one axis only (F-03 M06 partial disengagement) | Continued lateral or longitudinal actuation during the driver's emergency action | S3 | E3 | C2 ⚠ | **B** | As HE-05.1/05.2; credits AOU-01/02/03. Without them → C3 → ASIL C |

 198  ### 5.6 H-06 Unannounced loss of longitudinal deceleration
 199  
 200  | HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
 201  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 202  | HE-06.1 | OS-07 approaching stationary or slow traffic at highway speed; the system silently stops decelerating (for example, a stale plan holds zero acceleration) | High-speed rear-end collision | S3 | E3 | C2 | **B** | S3: high closing speed. E3. C2: supervising driver, but reliance on ACC delays the reaction. The stock PCS may mitigate this but is not credited (it is a separate system, and AOU-04 is unverified) |
 203  | HE-06.2 | OS-06 slow queue | Low-speed rear-end collision | S1 | E3 | C2 | **QM** | S1: low-speed impact, light injuries |
 204  | HE-06.3 | OS-05 following a lead at a short gap; the lead brakes and the deceleration is silently lost (F-02 M01/M02/M06/M11) | Rear-end collision at moderate speed difference | S2 | E4 | C2 | **B** | S2: following speeds, moderate speed difference. E4: most engaged time has a lead. C2: supervising driver, but reliance on ACC delays the reaction |
 205  
 206  ### 5.7 H-07 Displayed system state differs from actual state (mode confusion)
 207  
 208  | HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
 209  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 210  | HE-07.1 | OS-02 highway curve or OS-16 rural curve; the system has disengaged but still shows engaged, or the take-over alert is missing (F-05 M01/M04) | The driver believes the system steers; departure in the curve | S3 | E3 | C2 | **B** | S3: departure into barrier, traffic or the oncoming lane. E3: curves near R_ODD. C2: the drift develops quickly and the driver's belief that the system is active delays the reaction |
 211  | HE-07.2 | OS-01 highway, straight or gentle curve | The driver believes the system steers; slow lane departure | S3 | E4 | C1 | **B** | S3: departure at highway speed. E4. C1: slow drift on a straight road; an attentive driver notices it |
 212  | HE-07.3 | OS-07 approaching slower or stationary traffic; the driver believes ACC is active | The driver expects the vehicle to slow; high closing speed | S3 | E3 | C2 | **B** | S3: high closing speed. E3. C2: reliance on a function that is not active |
 213  
 214  ### 5.8 H-08 Suppression of the vehicle's stock pre-collision braking
 215  
 216  | HE | Situation | Effect | S | E | C | ASIL | Rationale |
 217  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 218  | HE-08.1 | OS-09 imminent collision where the stock PCS would have braked (engaged or not engaged) | Collision not mitigated | S3 | E2 | C3 | **B** | S3: PCS-relevant impacts. E2: PCS-relevant situations are rare. C3: by definition the driver did not avoid the situation. The item must not degrade a stock safety function, whether or not LD-SDA is engaged |
 219  
 220  ### 5.9 Malfunctions rated QM
 221  
 222  | Malfunction | Rating | Rationale |
 223  |---|---|---|
 224  | Missing FCW (F-06) | QM | The warning supplements driver supervision. The stock PCS gives its own warning (subject to H-08) |
 225  | False FCW (F-06) without a close follower | QM | Startle without actuation, or a moderate driver brake (S1). With a close follower it is HE-04.5 (ASIL A). Recurrent false alerts are a SOTIF and HMI concern ([WP-C-08](WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md)) |
 226  | Missing LDW (F-07) | QM | Manual driving with the driver in full control |
 227  | Loss of logging or upload | QM | No effect on vehicle motion. Relevant to field monitoring and cybersecurity only |

 235  | EX-09.1 | OS-15 approaching a signalised or stop-controlled intersection (F-02X M01 does not stop) | Vehicle enters the intersection against a red light or stop sign | S3 | E3 | C2 ⚠ | B | C2 needs evidence: Experimental Mode invites over-trust that it stops (SH-09, WP-C-05). Without it → C3 → ASIL C. Would need a new safety goal ("SG-09", ASIL B) |
 236  | EX-04.1 | OS-05/OS-08 dense traffic, false stop for a misread signal or shadow (F-02X M03) | Rear impact by the following vehicle | S2 | E4 | C2 ⚠ | B | Same as HE-04.1; covered by SG-04 |

 238  ## 6. Safety goals
 239  
 240  | ID | Safety goal | ASIL | Source HEs | Safe state | Preliminary FTTI | Notes |
 241  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 242  | **SG-01** | The item shall not cause lateral motion of the vehicle that exceeds what the driver can control, including any steering actuation while the item is not engaged | **C** ⚠ (D if AOU-01/02 fail) | HE-01.1–01.4, HE-02.3 | LKA torque request zero, steer request bit cleared; driver informed | **≤ 0.5 s** (preliminary: `docs/SAFETY.md` cites 0.9 s to reach 1 m lateral deviation at maximum actuation; FTTI to be derived in [WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md)) | The current envelope detection times (RX ≤ 2 s, heartbeat 3–5 s) are not consistent with this FTTI (GAP-06). The envelope's continuous limiting (magnitude, rate, measured tracking) does act within one frame |
 243  | **SG-02** | The item shall not lose or degrade lateral control while engaged without giving the driver an adequate take-over warning | **B** | HE-02.1–02.2, HE-02.4–02.5, HE-07.1–07.2 | Driver warned (visual + acoustic) and lateral control handed back with a smooth torque ramp-down | ≤ 1 s from fault to warning (preliminary) | Mode awareness (HMI) and DM integrity contribute. Covers H-07: a wrong or frozen engagement indication is a missing take-over warning |
 244  | **SG-03** | The item shall not cause unintended vehicle acceleration | **B** ⚠ (C if AOU-05 fails) | HE-03.1–03.5 | Acceleration request ≤ 0 (no positive acceleration); ACC cancelled to the PCM | ≤ 1 s (preliminary) | |
 245  | **SG-04** | The item shall not cause deceleration that exceeds what following traffic and the driver can control | **B** ⚠ (C if AOU-05 fails) | HE-04.1–04.5 | Deceleration limited; transition to "inactive" (no ACC command; coast) with a bounded jerk | ≤ 1 s (preliminary) | The envelope has no longitudinal jerk limit (GAP-04) |
 246  | **SG-05** | The item shall release control immediately when the driver brakes, cancels, or overrides steering | **B** ⚠ (C if AOU-01/02/03 fail) | HE-05.1–05.4 | Disengaged: no lateral or longitudinal actuation | ≤ 0.2 s from driver input to release (preliminary) | The envelope does not monitor driver steering torque on the LKA path (GAP-02) |
 247  | **SG-06** | The item shall not lose longitudinal deceleration capability while engaged without giving the driver an adequate take-over warning | **B** | HE-06.1, HE-06.3, HE-07.3 | Driver warned; ACC cancelled so that the driver brakes | ≤ 1 s (preliminary) | Covers the longitudinal part of H-07 |
 248  | **SG-07** | The item shall not suppress, delay or alter the vehicle's stock pre-collision (PCS/AEB) function | **B** | HE-08.1 | Camera PCS messages forwarded unchanged; if forwarding cannot be guaranteed (e.g. harness fault), relay released to restore the stock camera link | ≤ 0.2 s for loss or delay of forwarding (preliminary, [WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md) §3.6); content: per frame | Verification through AOU-04 and harness fault analysis |
 249

Its representative events (§5) are connected to the goals whose "Source HEs" column above lists them. HE-02.3 goes to SG-01, the H-07 events go to SG-02 and SG-06, and HE-06.2 (QM) has no goal. Source: [`hara/figures/fig-c03-6-1-hazard-sg-overview.svg`](hara/figures/fig-c03-6-1-hazard-sg-overview.svg), generated by [`hara/hara_figures.py`](hara/hara_figures.py); change §4–§6 and regenerate.
 253  
 254  ### 6.1 Observations for the functional safety concept
 255  
 256  1. **SG-01 sets the ASIL for the envelope.** At ASIL C (or D if the EPS assumptions fail), a single-channel STM32H7 without a working watchdog, with report-only faults and no E2E counters (GAP-01, -07, -08, -11) cannot be argued as it stands. The FSC ([WP-C-04](WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md)) has to choose between:
 257     - (a) hardening the envelope to ASIL C;
 258     - (b) ASIL decomposition, e.g. C(C) = B(C) on the envelope + A(C) credited to the EPS-internal limitation, which is only valid if independence and AoU evidence exist;
 259     - (c) reducing the actuation authority (torque limit, rate) until controllability C1 can be shown, which lowers the ASIL.
 260  
 261     Option (c) combined with vehicle controllability testing is the most realistic route for an aftermarket item. It also matches the ISO 11270 reasoning already present in `docs/SAFETY.md`.
 262  2. **The ratings depend heavily on the vehicle AoUs** (AOU-01/02/03/05). Characterizing the vehicle (EPS torque authority and timeout, PCM ACC envelope, brake override) is therefore a G1 activity, not a later validation activity.
 263  3. **Driver monitoring underpins controllability** in H-02 and H-06 (and the C2 ratings generally). The FSC must give DM integrity an ASIL attribute (inherited from SG-02/SG-06) or justify why not. Today DM runs entirely on the QM SoC, with the validity flag hard-coded `True` at the source (GAP-21).
 264  4. **SG-07 has to hold even when LD-SDA is disengaged.** It covers the harness and relay hardware and the forwarding logic.
 265  5. **New sources found by the Cartesian method (version 0.2).** None changes a safety goal ASIL, but each needs coverage in the FSC:
 266     - Driver monitoring commanding forced deceleration while the driver is attentive (HE-04.3). DM is a source of H-04 as well as a controllability measure. SG-04 therefore applies to the DM-initiated deceleration; WP-C-04 covers it with FSR-04.05 and the envelope bounds FSR-04.01/04.02, so the DM chain itself can stay QM (WP-C-04 §7).
 267     - The envelope stuck in a blocking state while the SoC shows "engaged" (F-08 M04, HE-02.4). SG-02 then requires that a block by the envelope is detected and announced to the driver within the SG-02 time.
 268     - Braking commanded while not engaged (HE-04.4). This adds to HE-01.4 (torque while not engaged): the engagement gating carries SG-01 and SG-04.
 269     - Mode confusion (H-07), now rated, is covered by SG-02 and SG-06. The HMI path that shows the engagement state and gives take-over alerts carries ASIL B.
 270     - False FCW with a close follower is ASIL A (HE-04.5), not QM as in version 0.1.
 271  6. **AoU sensitivity.** If every ⚠ AoU fails, 26 rows reach ASIL D, all of them H-01 at E4 (OS-01, OS-03, OS-05, OS-08, OS-13), and 267 rows reach ASIL C. This confirms observation 2: AOU-01/02 (EPS torque authority) carry the SG-01 rating.
 272

59## 4. Envelope strategy decision (HARA §6.1, OI-4)
60
61SG-01 at ASIL C (D if the EPS assumptions fail) drives the integrity of the envelope. The HARA lists three options. This section evaluates them and recommends one. **The decision belongs to the maintainer (acting safety manager); this section is a recommendation.**
62
63### 4.1 Evaluation
64
65| Criterion | (a) Harden the envelope to ASIL C | (b) ASIL decomposition C(C) = B(C) envelope + A(C) EPS | (c) Reduce actuation authority so controllability C1 can be shown, lowering SG-01 to ASIL B |

Why it matters: Option (c) requires measured controllability and speed-dependent limits before re-rating; ASIL reduction and decomposition are different arguments.

AI-author preparation: Prepare strategy alternatives and their controllability evidence needs; distinguish re-rating from decomposition.

Closure evidence: Signed strategy plus validated limit data, revised allocations and confirmed HARA.

Task: TASK-A05 · dependencies: TASK-A04

  15  ## 1. Purpose and inputs
  16  
  17  This document derives functional safety requirements (FSRs) from the safety goals SG-01…SG-07 of the [HARA (WP-C-03)](WP-C-03-hara.md) and allocates them to the architectural elements of the [item definition (WP-C-01)](WP-C-01-item-definition.md). It also records the envelope strategy decision that the HARA left open (WP-C-03 §6.1, OI-4), the integrity attributes of driver monitoring (WP-C-03 §6.1 obs. 3), and the refined assumptions of use (AoU) on the vehicle and driver.
  18  
  19  Inputs:
  20  
  21  - [WP-C-01](WP-C-01-item-definition.md): functions F-01…F-09, elements E-01…E-06, interfaces IF-01…IF-09, AOU-01…AOU-11 (AOU-12 and AOU-13, defined in §11, were added to WP-C-01 in its v0.2).
  22  - [WP-C-03](WP-C-03-hara.md) v0.2 (Cartesian HARA): hazards H-01…H-08; SG-01…SG-07 with ASIL, safe states and preliminary FTTIs; the new hazardous-event sources of WP-C-03 §6.1 obs. 5.
  23  - [WP-C-02](WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md): ODD and intended functionality.
  24  - [Gap assessment](../00-assessment/gap-assessment.md): GAP-01…GAP-37.
  25  - [WP-M-01 §4](../01-management/WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#4-safety-architecture-argument-the-central-strategy): safety envelope pattern.
  26  
  27  Interface to cybersecurity (ISO/SAE 21434 §9.5): the cybersecurity goals that protect each safety goal against an attacker are mapped in [WP-C-10 §8](WP-C-10-cybersecurity-goals-and-concept.md#8-relation-to-safety-goals), against the FSR allocation of §5 and §10 below. A change to an FSR's allocation between the envelope (E-03) and the SoC (E-01) changes that mapping and must be reflected there.
  28  
  29  The ASILs in this document are those proposed in the HARA. The HARA is not yet confirmed (I3). If the confirmation review changes a rating, the FSR ASILs here change with it.

  48  
  49  | SG | Statement (short) | ASIL | Safe state | Preliminary FTTI |
  50  |---|---|---|---|---|
  51  | SG-01 | No uncontrollable lateral motion, no steering actuation while not engaged | C ⚠ (D if AOU-01/02 fail) | LKA torque 0, steer request cleared | ≤ 0.5 s |
  52  | SG-02 | No loss/degradation of lateral control without adequate take-over warning | B | Warning + torque ramp-down | ≤ 1 s to warning |
  53  | SG-03 | No unintended acceleration | B ⚠ | Accel ≤ 0; ACC cancelled | ≤ 1 s |
  54  | SG-04 | No deceleration beyond what following traffic and driver can control | B ⚠ | Inactive (no ACC command) with bounded jerk | ≤ 1 s |
  55  | SG-05 | Release on driver brake, cancel, steering override | B ⚠ | Disengaged | ≤ 0.2 s |
  56  | SG-06 | No loss of longitudinal deceleration without take-over warning | B | Warning; ACC cancelled | ≤ 1 s |

  58  
  59  ## 4. Envelope strategy decision (HARA §6.1, OI-4)
  60  
  61  SG-01 at ASIL C (D if the EPS assumptions fail) drives the integrity of the envelope. The HARA lists three options. This section evaluates them and recommends one. **The decision belongs to the maintainer (acting safety manager); this section is a recommendation.**
  62  
  63  ### 4.1 Evaluation
  64  
  65  | Criterion | (a) Harden the envelope to ASIL C | (b) ASIL decomposition C(C) = B(C) envelope + A(C) EPS | (c) Reduce actuation authority so controllability C1 can be shown, lowering SG-01 to ASIL B |
  66  |---|---|---|---|
  67  | What it needs | ASIL C hardware metrics (SPFM/LFM/PMHF targets) on a COTS single-channel MCU; ASIL C software process (MC/DC, GAP-13); E2E with counters on all RX (GAP-01); IWDG and fault reaction (GAP-07/08); DFA of shared SoC/MCU power and clock | Two **independent** elements that each satisfy the safety goal on their own; DFA showing independence; evidence that the EPS implements an ASIL A(C) torque limitation | Vehicle characterization of the EPS torque authority (AOU-01/02); a physically derived, speed-dependent torque limit in the envelope; controllability tests (WP-C-08 §8) showing ≥ 99 % of drivers control envelope-bounded worst-case actuation |
  68  | Feasibility for an aftermarket retrofit | Low to medium. No supplier FMEDA data for the device; the shared PCB/power with the QM SoC makes the ASIL C hardware metrics hard to reach (WP-M-01 §9) | Low. Toyota provides no evidence of the EPS internal limitation, its ASIL or its independence. ISO 26262-9 §5 requires that independence be shown, not assumed. An external element cannot receive a decomposed ASIL without evidence of its development | Medium to high. Needs vehicle testing, which is needed anyway for AOU-01/02 (HARA OI-1). Matches the ISO 11270 reasoning in `docs/SAFETY.md` |
  69  | Effect on availability | None | None | Less steering authority: some curves inside today's operating range can no longer be followed. Narrows the ODD (WP-C-02 §4.2) |
  70  | Residual dependence on unverified AoUs | Low for SG-01 (the envelope alone carries the goal) | High (EPS) | Medium (EPS limit still bounds the worst case; driver controllability is measured, not assumed) |
  71  | Effort | Highest | Medium, but blocked on evidence that LionDriver cannot obtain | Medium |
  72  
  73  ### 4.2 Recommendation
  74  
  75  **Recommended: option (c) reduced authority, combined with hardening of the envelope to ASIL B.** Option (b) is kept as a documented fallback but is not recommended as the primary route.
  76  
  77  Rationale:
  78  
  79  1. Option (c) attacks the risk at its source. The worst-case consequence of any E/E fault on the lateral path is bounded by what the envelope lets through. If that bound is chosen so that a typical driver controls it (C1), HE-01.1/01.2/01.4 become S3 E4 C1 = ASIL B, the same level as SG-02…SG-07. The whole envelope then has one target ASIL (B).
  80  2. ASIL B hardening is needed under every option (IWDG, fault → safe state, safety-mode lock, E2E counters, FTTI-consistent timeouts). Option (c) does not add hardware-metric targets that the COTS device is unlikely to meet.
  81  3. Option (b) requires evidence about the Toyota EPS that is not available. LionDriver cannot show the EPS was developed to ASIL A or that it is independent of the item (it consumes the item's command). A decomposition argument built on it would not survive an I3 review.
  82  4. Option (a) remains possible later if hardware metrics turn out better than expected. Nothing in (c) prevents it.
  83  
  84  Conditions attached to the recommendation:
  85  
  86  - **Until the controllability evidence exists, SG-01 stays ASIL C** and the SG-01 FSRs below carry ASIL C. They are expected to drop to ASIL B once the HARA is re-rated on evidence. The HARA owner re-rates; this document does not.
  87  - The reduced limit must be derived physically (torque at the wheel → lateral acceleration and lateral deviation over the FTTI, per speed), not tuned. That closes GAP-04 for the lateral path.
  88  - The controller (E-01) limits must sit below the envelope limits with margin, so that normal operation never runs at the enforcement boundary (gap assessment §3.1 notes zero margin today).
  89  
  90  ### 4.3 Fallback: ASIL decomposition (if C1 cannot be shown)
  91  
  92  If vehicle tests show that C1 cannot be reached at a torque level that keeps the function useful, the fallback is decomposition of SG-01:
  93  
  94  - **ASIL C(C) = ASIL B(C) on the envelope (E-03) + ASIL A(C) on a second, independent limiting element.**
  95  - The second element should be inside LionDriver's control, not the Toyota EPS: for example an independent hardware torque/request monitor on the harness side, or a second MCU checking `0x2E4` against measured EPS torque and steering state, able to open the relay.
  96  - Independence requirements (to be analysed in [WP-A-01](../08-analyses/WP-A-01-asil-decomposition.md) and [WP-A-03](../08-analyses/WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md)): separate power supply path or supervised supply, separate clock, separate CAN receive path, no shared software, no configuration of one element by the other, and a de-energise-to-safe actuation (relay open = stock path).
  97  - Crediting the EPS as the A(C) element is **not recommended** for the reasons in §4.1.
  98  
  99  ## 5. Functional safety requirements
 100  
 101  ![Figure 5-1: for each safety goal SG-01 to SG-07 with its ASIL, safe state and preliminary FTTI, the FSRs of §5 with their ASIL, the other goals each serves in the §9 trace, and markers for the elements E-03, E-05, E-01, E-01/DM, E-01/HMI, E-02, EXT-EPS, EXT-PCM and EXT-CLU named in the Alloc column](fsc/figures/fig-c04-5-1-sg-fsr-allocation.svg)
 102  
 103  *Figure 5-1.* Safety goals → FSRs → allocated elements. Each band is one goal with its ASIL, safe state and preliminary FTTI from §3. Each row is one FSR of §5 with its ASIL and the other goals that the §9 trace marks for it. A marker sits in the column of every element named in its Alloc column. A marker shows allocation only: every FSR is `Proposed`, and the Impl column gives the implementation status. Roles given in brackets in the Alloc column (detection, alert, backstop) are not drawn. Change those sections and regenerate; do not edit the SVG.
 104  
 105  Attributes: ASIL as currently derived; **Alloc** = allocation; **Verif** = verification method (A = analysis, R = review, T-SIL = software test, T-HIL = hardware-in-the-loop test, T-VEH = vehicle test, FI = fault injection). **Impl** = what the baseline code already does (file:line) or the GAP that blocks it. Paths for E-03 are under `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/` unless prefixed `panda/`. Paths for E-01 are under `openpilot/`. All statuses are `Proposed`.

 107  ### 5.1 SG-01 Lateral motion (ASIL C ⚠)
 108  
 109  | ID | Requirement | ASIL | Alloc | Verif | Impl / GAP |
 110  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 111  | FSR-01.01 | The envelope shall limit the commanded LKA torque magnitude to a speed-dependent bound derived from driver controllability (lateral acceleration and lateral deviation reached within the FTTI). | C | E-03 | A, T-SIL, T-VEH | Partial: fixed 1500 raw (`modes/toyota.h:173`); not speed-dependent, no physical rationale (GAP-04) |
 112  | FSR-01.02 | The envelope shall limit the rate of change of the commanded LKA torque (rise, fall and real-time window) to bounds derived from controllability. | C | E-03 | A, T-SIL | Implemented without rationale: 15/25 raw per frame, 450 raw per 250 ms (`modes/toyota.h:174-177`, `lateral.h:49-57, 87-95`) (GAP-04) |
 113  | FSR-01.03 | The envelope shall limit the commanded LKA torque to within a bounded difference of the measured EPS motor torque. | C | E-03 | T-SIL | Implemented: 350 raw (`modes/toyota.h:176`, `lateral.h:10-23`) |

 115  | FSR-01.05 | The envelope shall grant actuation authority only on a driver engagement action confirmed by the vehicle (rising edge of PCM cruise active) and shall revoke it when PCM cruise becomes inactive. | C | E-03 | T-SIL, T-VEH | Implemented (`safety.h:518-527`); PCM_CRUISE has no counter (GAP-01) |

 122  | FSR-01.12 | On any detected fault affecting lateral actuation, the envelope shall enter the lateral safe state (torque 0, steer request cleared) and keep it until a new valid engagement. | C | E-03 | T-SIL, FI | Partial: violating frames dropped and `controls_allowed` cleared on RX faults (`lateral.h:141-148`, `safety.h:112-121`); a TX violation alone does not revoke authority |
 123  | FSR-01.13 | The EPS shall limit LKA torque to an overpowerable level and shall end LKA torque within a bounded time after `0x2E4` stops or carries the request bit cleared. (External measure, AOU-01R) | — (ext.) | EXT-EPS | T-VEH | Unverified (GAP-05) |

 141  
 142  Note on FSR-02.02: the detection of input loss lies in the QM stack. At ASIL B this is not acceptable on its own. The ASIL B argument for SG-02 rests on FSR-01.07 (envelope removes actuation on stale commands) + FSR-02.05 (independent acoustic warning), both on E-03, with tighter timing (§8). FSR-02.02/02.03 on E-01 are QM measures that give the earlier, better-explained warning in the common case.

 197  
 198  ## 6. Safe states, transitions and emergency operation
 199  
 200  | State | Definition | Entered by | Exit |
 201  |---|---|---|---|
 202  | SS-L (lateral safe) | `0x2E4` torque 0, steer request cleared | Any lateral fault, driver brake/cancel, authority revoked | New valid engagement (PCM rising edge) |
 203  | SS-G (longitudinal safe) | `0x343` inactive value; ACC cancel requested | Any longitudinal fault, driver brake/cancel | New valid engagement |
 204  | SS-S (envelope silent) | SILENT/NO_OUTPUT: no TX, relay released (stock camera path) | Heartbeat loss, MCU fault, unknown mode (`panda/board/main.c:33-54, 193-213`) | Restart / re-initialisation |
 205  
 206  - **Emergency operation: none.** The item is fail-silent. On any fault it hands the driving task back to the driver with a warning. No minimal-risk manoeuvre is performed, apart from the DM no-response force deceleration (FSR-02.09), which is a misuse measure rather than a fault reaction. Rationale: a supervised Level 2 system with a driver in the loop; the safe states above are reachable within one CAN frame and are controllable by an attentive driver (to be confirmed in WP-C-08 §8 tests, esp. torque removal in a curve, HE-02.1).
 207  - **Transition rule:** a fault that makes the command untrusted (envelope fault, corrupted or stale command) → immediate SS-L/SS-G. A fault where the command is still trustworthy but the function will soon be unavailable (e.g. thermal, low disk) → warning first, then ramp to SS-L/SS-G (soft disable). The present soft disable does not make this distinction (GAP-16); FM-03 in [WP-C-07](WP-C-07-sotif-functional-modifications.md) proposes it.
 208  - **Warning on every fault-induced transition** (FSR-02.03, FSR-06.02), with an SoC-independent fallback (FSR-02.05).
 209  
 210  ## 7. Driver monitoring integrity attributes (HARA §6.1 obs. 3)
 211  
 212  The HARA asks the FSC to give DM an ASIL attribute inherited from SG-02/SG-06, or to justify why not.
 213  
 214  **Proposal: DM is not assigned an ASIL. Its requirements (FSR-02.06…02.09) are QM, managed under ISO 21448 as measures against foreseeable misuse ([WP-C-05](WP-C-05-sotif-hazard-identification.md), [WP-C-08](WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md)), with integrity-supporting attributes listed below.**
 215  
 216  Justification:
 217  
 218  1. ISO 26262-3 rates controllability for a typical driver, under the assumption that the driver is in the state the item's intended use requires (AOU-06). The C2 ratings for HE-02.x and HE-06.x assume a supervising driver, not one kept attentive by DM. DM addresses misuse (inattention), which is in ISO 21448 scope.
 219  2. A DM failure has no vehicle-level effect on its own (HARA §4). It becomes relevant only when combined with driver inattention **and** a separate hazard trigger — a multiple-point situation.
 220  3. The DM chain is an ML model plus Python on the QM SoC. An ASIL B allocation could not be met without an independent ASIL B monitor of driver state, which does not exist in the item.
 221  
 222  Integrity-supporting attributes (QM, but required):
 223  
 224  | Attribute | Requirement | Covered by |
 225  |---|---|---|
 226  | Latent-fault detection | DM must detect loss of its own input or credibility and fall back to a stricter policy | FSR-02.07 |
 227  | No bypass | DM cannot be disabled or demoed while engagement is possible | FSR-02.08 |
 228  | Liveness gating | Engagement is not possible, and an engaged system soft-disables, when DM output is missing | Implemented: `dmonitoringd` invalid → `commIssue` (onboard review; `selfdrive/monitoring/dmonitoringd.py:25-33`) |
 229  | Performance | Detection performance is validated against misuse scenarios | WP-C-08 §8, [WP-V-02](../06-validation/WP-V-02-sotif-vv-strategy.md) |
 230  
 231  **HARA v0.2 (HE-04.3):** DM is also a *source* of unintended deceleration (forced deceleration on an attentive driver). That path is covered by FSR-04.05 and the envelope bounds FSR-04.01/04.02 on E-03, which apply whatever requests the deceleration. So the conclusion above holds and the DM chain itself stays QM.
 232  
 233  **Condition:** the I3 confirmation review must agree that the HARA C2 ratings do not depend on DM. If the assessor disagrees, the alternative is to re-rate HE-02.1/HE-06.1 under the assumption of a distracted driver (likely C3 → ASIL C) and then decide between an ASIL-capable DM supervisor and an ODD/authority restriction. This is OI-3.
 234  
 235  ## 8. FTTI allocation (preliminary)
 236  
 237  FTTIs are preliminary (HARA OI-3). Detailed timing belongs in [WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md). The allocation below sets the budget the TSRs must meet.
 238  
 239  | SG | FTTI (prelim.) | Detection budget | Reaction budget | Current behaviour | Gap |
 240  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 241  | SG-01 (continuous limiting) | ≤ 0.5 s | 0 (per-frame check) | ≤ 1 frame (10 ms) | Per-frame limits act within one frame | — |
 242  | SG-01 (RX loss/corruption) | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | ≤ 0.1 s | RX timeout up to ≈2 s (`safety.h:321-344`) | GAP-06, GAP-01 |
 243  | SG-01 (SoC command loss) | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | ≤ 0.1 s | 3 s mismatch / 5 s loss (`main.c:182-213`) | GAP-06 |
 244  | SG-01 (MCU execution fault) | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.1 s (HW watchdog; set by SG-07) | ≤ 0.1 s | No HW watchdog | GAP-07 |
 245  | SG-02, SG-06 (loss without warning) | ≤ 1 s to warning | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.5 s (warning) | Soft disable warns at once, but actuates on stale inputs up to ≈3.5 s | GAP-16 |
 246  | SG-03, SG-04 | ≤ 1 s | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | as SG-01 detection | GAP-06 |
 247  | SG-05 (driver brake/cancel) | ≤ 0.2 s | ≤ 1 brake message period (25 ms at 40 Hz) | ≤ 1 frame | Envelope revokes authority on the next RX frame, but a rejected `0x2E4` frame is dropped rather than replaced by zero torque, so the EPS may keep acting until its own timeout (TSR-109 in [WP-S-02](../03-system/WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md)) | GAP-46; verify on vehicle (T-VEH) |
 248  | SG-05 (steering override) | ≤ 0.2 s | — | — | Not in envelope | GAP-02 |
 249  | SG-07 (forwarding loss) | ≤ 0.2 s (preliminary, WP-S-04 §3.6) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≤ 0.1 s (relay release) | No forwarding supervision; no IWDG | GAP-43, GAP-49 (`0xe7` stops forwarding); TSR-711 |
 250  | SG-07 (content) | per frame | 0 | — | Static forwarding; PCS IDs in the TX whitelist | GAP-42 (FSR-07.02) |
 251  
 252  Note on SG-02 in curves: [WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md) §3.2 estimates ≈ 0.7 s to a 0.6 m lateral drift at the ODD curve bound, which is shorter than driver reaction. The SG-02 budget above (≤ 0.5 s detection + ≤ 0.5 s to warning) is sufficient on straight roads and gentle curves but not alone at the ODD curve bound; SG-02 there relies on immediate warning and on torque being ramped rather than cut (FSR-02.04). Resolution (re-rate C of HE-02.1 or tighten R_ODD) is open in WP-S-04 OI-2 and [WP-C-03](WP-C-03-hara.md) OI-7.

 309  ## 12. Warning and degradation concept
 310  
 311  ### 12.1 Degradation levels
 312  
 313  | Level | Name | Lateral | Longitudinal | Driver info | Trigger examples |
 314  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 315  | L0 | Full function | Active | Active | Engaged indication (device + cluster cruise + LKAS HUD) | — |
 316  | L1 | Override | Active (driver torque dominates) / blocked | Blocked while gas pressed | Override indication | Driver gas or steering (`events.py:728, 736`) |
 317  | L2 | Take-over request, planned | Active with valid inputs, ramping down ≤ soft-disable time | Force decel to cruise 0 | Soft-disable alert, then immediate-disable form < 0.5 s | Non-critical faults (thermal, memory, calibration) |
 318  | L3 | Immediate disengagement | SS-L | SS-G + cancel | Immediate-disable alert (visual + acoustic) | Untrusted command or input, envelope/RX fault, relay fault, CAN error |
 319  | L4 | Envelope silent | SS-S | SS-S | Panda buzzer (SoC lost) | SoC heartbeat loss, MCU fault |
 320  | L5 | Lockout | Not available | Not available | No-entry alert with remaining time | DM non-compliance (`policy.py:42-44`) |
 321  
 322  There is no degraded "lateral-only" or "longitudinal-only" operating mode in the reference configuration.
 323  
 324  ### 12.2 Warning requirements (summary)
 325  
 326  - Every transition to L2/L3/L4 caused by a fault produces an acoustic and visual take-over request (FSR-02.03, 02.05, 06.02).
 327  - Alert text must describe the actual fault (GAP-19: `canError` shows "Unknown Vehicle Variant", `events.py:928-936`). Alert design and timing are reviewed in [WP-C-08](WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) §4.
 328  - `speedTooHigh` (≈149 km/h) warns without disengaging (`events.py:989-996`). This is an ODD exit, not a fault; handled in WP-C-02 §6 and FM-07 of WP-C-07.
 329

 345  ## 14. Completeness check (fsc-builder method)
 346  
 347  The FSRs above were written by hand from the safety goals. Their completeness is checked with the fsc-builder method. Each safety goal is expanded into a fault tree, OR(input failure, process failure, output failure), over a block diagram of the item: 20 ASIL-relevant nodes plus 5 external nodes for context. That gives one basic event per (safety goal, node), 140 in all.
 348  
 349  [`fsc/fsc_model.py`](fsc/fsc_model.py) records, for every basic event, the FSRs that address it, or why the node cannot violate that goal. The result is [`fsc/fsc-coverage.md`](fsc/fsc-coverage.md), and the generated workbook with the mapping in tab 09 is [`fsc/WP-C-04-fsc-completeness.xlsx`](fsc/WP-C-04-fsc-completeness.xlsx).
 350  
 351  CI runs `fsc_model.py --check`. It fails if:
 352  
 353  - a basic event has neither an FSR nor a reason;
 354  - a cited FSR does not exist here;
 355  - an FSR here is not cited by any basic event;
 356  - the coverage table is stale.
 357  
 358  The template FSR wording in the generated workbook (tab 05) is not the requirement set; the FSRs in §5 are.
 359  
 360  The check found the following:
 361  
 362  - **SoC reset and boot control of the MCU (node N13, GAP-38):** handled at TSR level (TSR-511) but not stated at FSR level. FSR-01.10 now includes it.
 363  - **New FSRs for the new HARA v0.2 sources:** FSR-02.10 (envelope blocking while the SoC shows engaged, HE-02.4), FSR-02.11 (HMI path failure, H-07), FSR-04.05 (item-initiated deceleration, HE-04.3) and FSR-04.06 (false FCW, HE-04.5).
 364  - **Trace extensions:**
 365    - FSR-03.02 now also serves SG-04 (no braking without authority, HE-04.4).
 366    - FSR-05.05/05.06 now also serve SG-06 (longitudinal mode confusion, HE-07.3).
 367    - FSR-02.09 now also serves SG-04 (DM forced deceleration).
 368  - **Loss of the 12 V supply (node N08):** the item cannot warn. The driver is informed only by the cluster cruise indicator (FSR-05.05) and the EPS ending LKA torque (FSR-01.13), which rests on AOU-01R and AOU-12.
 369  - **No ASIL decomposition is proposed.** No two nodes are redundant peers with disjoint shared resources. The SoC and the MCU share the device supply and PCB, and the envelope takes the SoC command stream as an input. This matches §4: the SoC is QM and the envelope is the safety mechanism, with freedom from interference argued in [WP-A-02](../08-analyses/WP-A-02-coexistence-freedom-from-interference.md).

A06 Assessment configuration is not fully identified

Evidence needed · Configuration manager / maintainer · G1

  26  
  27  ## 2. Purpose and functional description
  28  
  29  LD-SDA helps the driver by controlling steering (lane centring) and speed (adaptive cruise with stop-and-go) on roads within its ODD ([WP-C-02](WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md)). The driver stays responsible for the driving task at all times. The driver must supervise continuously and be ready to take over immediately. A driver monitoring function enforces attention.
  30  
  31  ### 2.1 Functions
  32  
  33  | ID | Function | Description | Main implementation |
  34  |---|---|---|---|
  35  | F-01 | Lateral control (lane centring) | Computes a desired path or curvature from the camera-based ML driving model and commands steering torque to the Toyota EPS over its LKA interface (`STEERING_LKA`, `0x2E4`). Lane changes are driver-initiated with the turn signal and assisted (desire input) | `selfdrive/modeld`, `selfdrive/controls/controlsd.py`, `selfdrive/controls/lib/latcontrol_torque.py`, `opendbc/car/toyota/carcontroller.py` |
  36  | F-02 | Longitudinal control (ACC with stop-and-go) | Holds the set speed or follows a lead vehicle. Commands acceleration and deceleration to the powertrain/brake ECUs through `ACC_CONTROL` (`0x343`), including permit-braking, standstill and cancel. Lead detection fuses the stock radar track and vision. In Experimental Mode the end-to-end model also proposes acceleration (stops for traffic lights and signs; see WP-C-02 for whether it is in scope) | `selfdrive/controls/plannerd.py`, `lib/longitudinal_planner.py`, `radard.py`, `carcontroller.py:174-254` |
  37  | F-03 | Engagement and mode management | Engagement through the stock cruise controls. The safety MCU grants control authority only on the rising edge of the PCM cruise-active signal. Disengages on brake, cancel or faults. Supports override (driver gas, driver steering) | `selfdrive/selfdrived/state.py`, `selfdrived.py`; `opendbc/safety/safety.h:518-527` |
  38  | F-04 | Driver monitoring | A driver-facing camera and ML model estimate attention. Escalating warnings (5/8/13 s on the vision policy), forced deceleration on no response, and lockout after repeated non-compliance | `selfdrive/modeld/dmonitoringmodeld.py`, `selfdrive/monitoring/policy.py` |
  39  | F-05 | Driver information and warnings | Device display and sounds (`soundd`), plus the instrument cluster LKA HUD (`LKAS_HUD`, `0x412`). Alerts follow the event classes (warning, soft disable, immediate disable, no-entry) | `selfdrive/selfdrived/events.py`, `alertmanager.py`, `selfdrive/ui` |
  40  | F-06 | Forward collision warning | Warning based on the model's hard-brake prediction or a planner crash estimate. Does not brake. The stock Toyota PCS stays in charge of AEB | `selfdrived.py:441-445`, `modeld/fill_model_msg.py:142-147` |
  41  | F-07 | Lane departure warning | Warning when the vehicle is not engaged laterally, above 31 mph, with no recent turn signal | `selfdrive/controls/lib/ldw.py` |
  42  | F-08 | Safety envelope | On the panda MCU: TX whitelist, actuation limits (torque magnitude/rate/measured tracking, accel bounds), engagement gating, RX plausibility checks, relay malfunction detection, heartbeat supervision | `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/*`, `panda/board/*` |
  43  | F-09 | Data logging, upload, remote access, software update (non-driving) | Drive logging, upload to a server, remote RPC (athena), SSH, OTA update (`updated`). Relevant for cybersecurity and field monitoring | `system/loggerd`, `system/athena`, `system/updated` |
  44  
  45  ### 2.2 Vehicle functions replaced, preserved and affected
  46  
  47  | Stock TSS 2.0 function | With LD-SDA installed | Basis |
  48  |---|---|---|
  49  | Lane Departure Alert / Lane Tracing Assist (camera-based steering assist) | **Replaced.** The harness relay intercepts the camera's LKA messages; `0x2E4`, `0x191` and `0x412` from the camera are blocked from the car side | `opendbc/safety/safety.h:274-281`; `toyota.h:6-32` |
  50  | Dynamic Radar Cruise Control | **Replaced** by openpilot longitudinal control (`0x343` from the camera blocked, relay-checked when openpilot longitudinal is active) | `toyota/interface.py:105`; `toyota.h:32-36` |
  51  | Pre-Collision System (AEB, pedestrian detection) | **Intended to be preserved** (camera PCS messages forwarded). **Not verified** — AOU-04, and hazard H-08 in [WP-C-03](WP-C-03-hara.md) | `docs/INTEGRATION.md` |
  52  | Road sign assist, automatic high beams | Assumed unaffected. Not verified (OI-3) | — |
  53  | EPS, brakes, ABS/VSC, powertrain | Not modified. Commanded only through existing ADAS interfaces | — |

 114  ### 3.4 Interfaces
 115  
 116  | ID | Interface | Counterpart | Direction | Content (examples) | Safety relevance |
 117  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 118  | IF-01 | Vehicle CAN, car side (panda bus 0) | EPS (`0x2E4`, `0x191`); ECM/PCM and brake actuator via the PCM ACC interface (`0x343`, `0x1D2`); instrument cluster (`0x412`); stock PCS receivers (`0x283`); forward radar, diagnostic address (`0x750`) | TX | `0x2E4` STEERING_LKA (torque, steer request), `0x191` STEERING_LTA (no actuation: LTA requests are refused in the LKA torque configuration), `0x343` ACC_CONTROL (accel, permit braking, cancel), `0x412` LKAS_HUD (cluster LKA HUD), `0x1D2` PCM_CRUISE (PCM cancel command; not sent by the reference car) (TX whitelist `toyota.h:6-32`). With openpilot longitudinal, as in the reference configuration (§2.2), the whitelist also admits `0x283` PRE_COLLISION (zero payload only) and `0x750` radar diagnostic (tester present to the radar only); the reference car sends neither (`toyota.h:28-39, 376-383, 487-493` at opendbc `a57b667`; [DN-01](../05-software/design-notes/DN-01-tx-rate-supervision.md) §2.1) | Actuation path (SG-01, SG-03, SG-04) |
 119  | IF-02 | Vehicle CAN, car side (panda bus 0); radar CAN (panda bus 1) | ABS/VSC (`0xAA`, `0x226`); EPS (`0x260`, `0x262`); ECM/PCM (`0x1D2`, `0x1D3`); forward radar (`0x180`–`0x19F`) | RX | `0xAA` WHEEL_SPEEDS (wheel speeds), `0x260` STEER_TORQUE_SENSOR (driver and EPS torque), `0x1D2` PCM_CRUISE (cruise active, gas released), `0x226` BRAKE_MODULE (brake pressed), `0x262` EPS_STATUS (LKA/LTA state; read by `carstate.py:122-126`; checked for presence, checksum and rate in the reference build only (TSR-110); not in upstream builds), `0x1D3` PCM_CRUISE_2 (cruise main on; checked for presence, checksum and rate in the reference build only, where MAIN_ON is cross-checked against CRUISE_ACTIVE (TSR-405); not in upstream builds), `0x180`–`0x19F` radar tracks on bus 1 (`radar_interface.py:10-12`, `:23`). Reference-build RX checks: `toyota.h:50-63, 261-275` at opendbc `a57b667` | Engagement gating, override detection, limits |
 120  | IF-03 | Camera side CAN (panda bus 2) | Toyota forward camera (and through it the car-side receivers of its forwarded messages) | RX/forward | Camera messages forwarded to the car, except intercepted control messages | PCS preservation (H-08) |
 121  | IF-04 | SoC ↔ panda | Internal (E-01 ↔ E-03) | SPI | CAN TX/RX streams, heartbeat (`0xf3`), safety-mode set (`0xdc`), health | Freedom from interference ([WP-A-02](../08-analyses/WP-A-02-coexistence-freedom-from-interference.md)) |
 122  | IF-05 | Road-facing cameras | Road environment | In | Images for the driving model and calibration | SOTIF |
 123  | IF-06 | Driver-facing camera (IR) | Driver | In | Images for the DM model | Controllability basis |
 124  | IF-07 | HMI | Driver | Out/In | Display, sounds, device touchscreen settings (toggles, e.g. Experimental Mode), cluster HUD | Mode awareness, warnings |
 125  | IF-08 | Power | Vehicle 12 V supply and ignition (through E-05 harness) | In | Vehicle 12 V via the harness; ignition detection | Availability, safe shutdown |
 126  | IF-09 | Network (Wi-Fi/LTE) | comma.ai back end (athena, connect), update origin, SSH clients | Bidirectional | Athena RPC, uploads, OTA updates, SSH | Cybersecurity ([WP-C-09](WP-C-09-tara.md)) |

 132  ## 4. Operating modes and states
 133  
 134  | Mode | Description | Actuation |
 135  |---|---|---|
 136  | Off / offroad | Ignition off or device offroad. pandad puts the panda in `NO_OUTPUT` (`pandad.cc:204-207`). A panda found in `SILENT` is also switched to `NO_OUTPUT` (`pandad.cc:195-196`) | None |
 137  | Onroad, disengaged | Car safety mode active, `controls_allowed = false` | None (non-zero torque or accel rejected, `lateral.h:98-101`) |
 138  | Pre-enabled / enabled | Engaged after the PCM cruise rising edge and no NO_ENTRY condition (`state.py:79-90`) | Lateral and longitudinal |
 139  | Overriding | Driver gas or steering input while engaged. Longitudinal blocked while gas pressed (`longitudinal.h:3-5`) | Lateral; no longitudinal |
 140  | Soft disabling | Fault detected; warning; actuation continues up to 3 s (`state.py:7`) — see GAP-16 | Lateral and longitudinal (forced decel) |
 141  | Immediate disable | Critical fault; control released at once | None |
 142  | Driver lockout | DM non-compliance; engagement prevented for 1–30 min (`policy.py:42-44`) | None |
 143  | Chill vs Experimental Mode | Longitudinal policy selection (MPC vs end-to-end model). Upstream default is Experimental (`params_keys.h:43`, GAP-18) | — |
 144  | Debug / maneuver / joystick modes | Developer modes that replace control processes (`process_config.py:34-47`) | **Excluded from the reference configuration** (OI-5) |
 145  
 146  ![Figure 4-1: operating modes of LD-SDA (off/offroad, onroad disengaged, pre-enabled/enabled, overriding, soft disabling, immediate disable, driver lockout) with host and panda state, actuation chips and the record transitions between them, coloured by who enforces them](figures/fig-c01-4-1-operating-modes.svg)
 147  
 148  *Figure 4-1.* The §4 modes as one state diagram, drawn from the table above. Each mode shows the selfdrived state, the panda safety mode and `controls_allowed`, and what may actuate; each arrow names the transitions of the state-machine records ([WP-W-05](../05-software/WP-W-05-software-unit-design.md) §5: `sm-selfdrived` D/E/O/SD/X/N/P, `sm-toy-auth` T, `sm-safety-mode` M) and is coloured by who enforces it: the panda envelope, the host only, or the host only while the envelope still allows actuation (soft disabling, GAP-16). The configuration rows (Chill/Experimental, GAP-18; debug modes, OI-5) are shown as a strip. Generated by [`figures/fig_c01_modes.py`](figures/fig_c01_modes.py); CI fails if it is stale.
 149  
 150  ![Figure 4-2: illustrative engagement episode on a shared time axis: selfdrived state, panda controls_allowed, panda safety mode, lateral and longitudinal commands, HMI alerts and DM lockout, with the 3 s soft disable and the 1–30 min lockout](figures/fig-c01-4-2-engagement-timeline.svg)
 151  
 152  *Figure 4-2.* One illustrative episode through the §4 modes, with the host, the envelope and the actuators on one time axis. The times between events are made up; only the 3 s soft disable (`state.py:7`) and the 1–30 min lockout (`policy.py:42-44`) are code values. Transition labels are record IDs (T1, T2/T3, T6, D3, E1, E2, O2, SD2, X2, N1). The red band is the GAP-16 window: the host is soft disabling while `controls_allowed` stays 1; after SD2 the panda clears only at T2, once the PCM drops cruise on the host's cancel request (AOU-05), or at latest at T6. Generated by [`figures/fig_c01_modes.py`](figures/fig_c01_modes.py); CI fails if it is stale.

 154  ## 5. Legal requirements, national and international standards
 155  
 156  | Source | Applicability |
 157  |---|---|
 158  | FMVSS (US) | The vehicle's FMVSS compliance must not be degraded (e.g. FMVSS 126 ESC, 135 brakes are unaffected by design — verify). No FMVSS covers L2 ADAS function |
 159  | NHTSA Standing General Order 2021-01 (as amended) | Crash reporting for L2 ADAS applies to manufacturers and operators. Used as the model for [WP-O-04](../09-production-operation/WP-O-04-field-monitoring.md) |
 160  | State vehicle codes | Driver remains legally responsible. Some states regulate testing of automated driving; L2 is generally outside those rules (to be confirmed per test state, OI-6) |
 161  | ISO 11270 (LKAS), ISO 15622 (ACC), ISO 22179 (FSRA) | Performance and actuation limit references. ISO 11270 and ISO 15622 are the references upstream cites (`docs/SAFETY.md:32`). ISO 22179 is added by LionDriver because the reference configuration uses full-speed range ACC with stop-and-go |
 162  | UNECE R79 (ACSF), R171 (DCAS), EU 2025/1899 DM reference | Not legally binding in the US. Used as benchmarks for HMI, DM and actuation limits |
 163  | ISO 26262, ISO 21448, ISO/SAE 21434, ISO/PAS 8800 | Applied voluntarily per [WP-M-01](../01-management/WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md) |
 164  
 165  ## 6. Known hazards and experience from similar items
 166  
 167  - Unintended or excessive steering torque, unexpected loss of steering assistance in curves, phantom braking, insufficient braking for stationary vehicles, failure to release on driver override, mode confusion (driver believes the system is engaged when it is not).
 168  - Over-reliance and inattention on hands-free capable L2 systems (NHTSA investigations of L2 systems). openpilot uses vision-based DM and **does not require hands on the wheel**. This is an important factor for controllability and misuse.
 169  - Limitations listed upstream in `docs/LIMITATIONS.md`: poor weather, glare, sharp curves, construction zones, stationary vehicles, cut-ins, pedestrians, traffic lights not detected.
 170  
 171  ## 7. Assumptions of use (AoU) and external measures
 172  
 173  Each assumption needs verification evidence before it is credited in the HARA or safety concept. Status `Unverified` means it is only an assumption.
 174  
 175  | ID | Assumption | Credited in | Verification approach | Status |
 176  |---|---|---|---|---|
 177  | AOU-01 | The Toyota EPS limits the torque applied for LKA requests to a level a normal driver can overpower. It rejects or fades out implausible requests, and it ends LKA torque within a bounded time if LKA messages stop (a code comment suggests ≈1.5–2 s, `carstate.py:15-16`) | HARA controllability of H-01/H-05 | Bench and vehicle characterization: max LKA torque at the wheel, timeout behaviour, fault states | Unverified |
 178  | AOU-02 | A normal driver can override full LKA torque with steering force within the controllability criterion set by CA-01 in WP-C-08 §7 (ISO 11270-type limits; ≤ 50 N at the rim indicative) | HARA C-rating H-01, H-05 | Vehicle measurement with a steering force gauge | Unverified |
 179  | AOU-03 | Driver brake pedal application always produces braking regardless of ACC commands, and the PCM cancels ACC on brake application | HARA H-05 | Vehicle test | Unverified |
 180  | AOU-04 | The stock PCS/AEB keeps full function with the harness installed and openpilot longitudinal active | HARA H-08 | Review of forwarded and blocked messages; PCS target test (e.g. soft target) | Unverified |
 181  | AOU-05 | The PCM bounds ACC acceleration and deceleration requests to its own ACC envelope and honours the cancel bit | HARA H-03/H-04 | Vehicle test with injected out-of-range requests (on a closed course) | Unverified |
 182  | AOU-06 | The driver is licensed, attentive, briefed according to [WP-O-03](../09-production-operation/WP-O-03-user-information-safety-warnings.md), and keeps their eyes on the road | Controllability (all) | Enforced by F-04; misuse analysis [WP-C-08](WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) | Assumption (SOTIF-managed) |
 183  | AOU-07 | During development, only trained safety drivers operate the item on public roads, under [WP-V-07](../06-validation/WP-V-07-vehicle-test-operations.md) | All | Training records | Not started |
 184  | AOU-08 | The device is mounted and calibrated according to installation instructions | SOTIF | Calibration check (`calibrationd`) and installation checklist [WP-O-01](../09-production-operation/WP-O-01-installation-and-provisioning-control.md) | Partially (calibration check exists) |
 185  | AOU-09 | The vehicle is maintained, unmodified (suspension, tyres, steering), and has no active DTCs in the EPS, brake or powertrain systems | All | Pre-drive checklist | Not started |
 186  | AOU-10 | VSC/ABS are enabled and functional | H-01, H-04 | Pre-drive checklist; CAN status signal | Not started |
 187  | AOU-11 | The comma.ai back end and network are untrusted; no safety function depends on them | CS / FuSa | Architecture review | Design constraint |
 188  | AOU-12 | The cluster cruise indicator is driven by PCM state and is not influenced by item TX messages other than `0x412` LKAS_HUD | FSC FSR-05.05; independent cue for SG-02/SG-06 (WP-S-02 TSR-303, TSR-309) | Vehicle test (WP-S-09 VS-SQ-07) | Unverified |
 189  | AOU-13 | The de-energised harness relay connects the forward camera to the vehicle CAN (stock path) without any active component in the path | FSC FSR-07.03; SG-07 safe state | Harness inspection and test (WP-H-02 UK-03; WP-S-08 VS-SI-18) | Unverified |
 190  | AOU-14 | The reference build (`LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=1`) is used with openpilot's default alternative experience; no setting that sets alternative-experience bits is enabled. The firmware fixes the value at 0; a different host value leads to a controls mismatch | WP-S-02 TSR-512 (SWSR-512a) | Installation checklist; host-side guard to be decided (IA mode lock OI-3) | Unverified |
 191  | AOU-15 | The reference build (`LIONDRIVER_REFERENCE=1`) is used only on camera-ACC configurations. It refuses PCS_HUD `0x411` from the SoC, so on radar-ACC cars with openpilot longitudinal (`DISABLE_RADAR`) the FCW HUD indication is lost | WP-S-02 TSR-705 (decision D-1) | Installation checklist (reference vehicle is camera-ACC); release record of the supported configurations | Unverified |

108### 5.1 Provisional qualitative assessment

Why it matters: WP-H-04 lacks BOM-based calculation; WP-H-03 has generic estimates, not part-specific compliance evidence. Generic results cannot close the metric obligation.

AI-author preparation: List BOM, rates, mission profile and diagnostic-coverage inputs needed per safety goal; retain uncomputed result fields.

Closure evidence: Reviewed FMEDA and reproducible part-specific SPFM/LFM/PMHF with traceable inputs and valid targets.

Original document at the assessed commit (read-only)
   1  # WP-H-04 Hardware architectural metrics (SPFM, LFM)
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-H-04 Hardware architectural metrics (SPFM, LFM) |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-5:2018 §8 (evaluation of the hardware architectural metrics), Annex C (metric definitions, informative), Annex D (diagnostic coverage, informative); ISO 26262-11:2018 (semiconductors, informative) |
   7  | Version | 0.3 (§5 status and §4 targets drawn as Figure 5-1; no value changed; 2026-10-13). 0.2 (status note 2026-10-12: DC-01 implemented, not verified, no DC credit; §5.1, §6 step 4). 0.1: initial draft |
   8  | Status | Draft (method and plan; no metric computed) |
   9  | ASIL / scope | ASIL C (provisional, SG-01) / ASIL B (SG-02…SG-07) |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1 minimum, external reviewer, T-09) |
  12  | Approver | TBD (per [WP-M-02](../01-management/WP-M-02-safety-plan.md)) |
  13  | Baseline | `bf6ce8c` (status update 2026-10-12) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose
  16  
  17  States how LionDriver will compute the single-point fault metric (SPFM) and latent fault metric
  18  (LFM) for the hardware of LD-SDA, which targets apply, what the status is, and the plan to get to
  19  a result. It addresses **GAP-15** (no SPFM/LFM). Under tailoring **T-04**, LionDriver computes the
  20  metrics itself because no supplier evidence exists.
  21  
  22  **No metric has been computed. None can be computed today, because there is no BOM, no
  23  schematic and no failure-rate data** ([WP-H-02](WP-H-02-hardware-design.md) §5, UK-01/02).
  24  
  25  ## 2. Scope of the calculation
  26  
  27  | Item | Decision |
  28  |---|---|
  29  | Hardware in scope | Safety-related hardware of E-04 (panda MCU and its periphery: clock, supply, transceivers, relay driver, sense circuits, SPI and reset lines) and E-05 (harness relay, connector, wiring) as identified in WP-H-02 §3.2 |
  30  | Out of scope | SoC, cameras, display, modem (QM; their failures are covered by the envelope or are SOTIF). They enter the DFA, not the metrics. Vehicle ECUs (outside the item, T-02) |
  31  | Per safety goal | Metrics are computed **per safety goal**: SG-01 (with SG-03/SG-04/SG-05 sharing the same hardware path, computed separately because the safe states differ) and SG-07 (relay/harness-dominated). SG-02/SG-06 (warning on loss) are computed for the siren and heartbeat path |
  32  | Level | Item level for the hardware of the item, with the MCU split per ISO 26262-11 ([WP-H-03](WP-H-03-hardware-safety-analysis-fmeda.md) §6.2) |
  33  
  34  ## 3. Method
  35  
  36  ### 3.1 Definitions (our words; verify against the licensed text)
  37  
  38  For the safety-related hardware elements of one safety goal:
  39  
  40  - **λ** = total failure rate of the safety-related hardware.
  41  - **λ_SPF** = rate of single-point faults (no safety mechanism covers them).
  42  - **λ_RF** = rate of residual faults (the uncovered part of faults that a safety mechanism is
  43    meant to cover): λ_RF = λ_covered-element × (1 − DC_RF).
  44  - **λ_MPF,L** = rate of latent multiple-point faults (neither detected by a safety mechanism nor
  45    perceived by the driver within the multiple-point fault detection interval).
  46  - **λ_S** = safe faults.
  47  
  48  ```
  49  SPFM = 1 − (Σ λ_SPF + Σ λ_RF) / Σ λ
  50  LFM  = 1 − Σ λ_MPF,L / (Σ λ − Σ λ_SPF − Σ λ_RF)
  51  ```
  52  
  53  Only safety-related elements enter the sums. Elements not safety-related are excluded with a
  54  documented rationale in the FMEDA.
  55  
  56  ### 3.2 Calculation steps
  57  
  58  1. Freeze the hardware configuration: device revision, harness part number, MCU option bytes
  59     (WP-H-02 UK-04) — the metrics are valid for that configuration only.
  60  2. Fill the FMEDA ([WP-H-03](WP-H-03-hardware-safety-analysis-fmeda.md) §6) with failure rates
  61     from the agreed source and failure-mode distributions.
  62  3. For each failure mode decide: safety-related? violates the SG without a mechanism? which
  63     mechanism covers it (for the SPF/RF classification) and which covers latent faults?
  64  4. Assign DC per mechanism from the three-level scale (WP-H-03 §2.2). Claims above "low" need
  65     justification by analysis or fault injection ([WP-H-06](WP-H-06-hardware-integration-verification.md)).
  66  5. Compute the sums and the metrics per SG in a spreadsheet kept under configuration control
  67     (`trace/` or the analysis folder, per [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md)); record the version of every input.
  68  6. Sensitivity: vary the dominant failure rates and DC values within plausible bounds and show
  69     whether the target is still met.
  70  7. Independent review (T-09) of the FMEDA and the spreadsheet.
  71  
  72  ### 3.3 Rules LionDriver applies
  73  
  74  | Rule | Reason |
  75  |---|---|
  76  | A mechanism counts only if it leads to the safe state (or to a driver-perceived warning) within the FTTI budget of [WP-C-04](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md) §8 | A report-only fault (GAP-08) has no coverage |
  77  | Mechanisms in the QM SoC (e.g. host-side `controlsMismatch`, `canError`) are **not** credited for SPF/RF coverage | They are QM; crediting them would need the SoC to be developed to the ASIL |
  78  | Vehicle measures (EPS LKA limit and timeout, PCM clamp) are **not** credited as DC | External measures; they are handled in the PMHF/EEC argument ([WP-H-05](WP-H-05-random-hardware-failures-pmhf.md) §5) and in the HARA controllability |
  79  | Driver perception counts as latent-fault detection only for effects the driver cannot miss (e.g. engagement refused at start-up with a visible message) | Matches the "perceived" class |
  80  | Start-up tests count for latent faults if executed every drive cycle and if their result blocks engagement | Drive-cycle interval is the multiple-point fault detection interval assumed here |
  81  
  82  ## 4. Targets
  83  
  84  | Metric | ASIL B | ASIL C | ASIL D | Source |
  85  |---|---|---|---|---|
  86  | SPFM | ≥ 90 % | ≥ 97 % | ≥ 99 % | ISO 26262-5 §8 (commonly used values — **verify against licensed text**) |
  87  | LFM | ≥ 60 % | ≥ 80 % | ≥ 90 % | as above |
  88  
  89  Applicable targets ([WP-H-01](WP-H-01-hardware-safety-requirements.md) §3):
  90  
  91  | SG | Current ASIL | Target set | Expected after FSC option (c) |
  92  |---|---|---|---|
  93  | SG-01 | C (provisional) | SPFM ≥ 97 %, LFM ≥ 80 % | B: SPFM ≥ 90 %, LFM ≥ 60 % |
  94  | SG-02…SG-06 | B | SPFM ≥ 90 %, LFM ≥ 60 % | unchanged |
  95  | SG-07 | B | SPFM ≥ 90 %, LFM ≥ 60 % | unchanged |
  96  
  97  ## 5. Current status
  98  
  99  | Step (§3.2) | Status |
 100  |---|---|

 105  | 6 Sensitivity | Not started |
 106  | 7 Review | Not started |
 107  
 108  ### 5.1 Provisional qualitative assessment
 109  
 110  This is engineering judgement, not a metric. It is stated so the decisions in the FSC can be
 111  made with eyes open.
 112  
 113  **Baseline hardware and firmware (`8b8c6ae`): the ASIL B targets are very likely not met for
 114  SG-01 or SG-07, and the ASIL C targets are not met.** Reasons:
 115  
 116  1. The MCU die is the largest single contributor of failure rate among the safety-related parts
 117     *(expected; confirm with data)*. Today almost all of it is uncovered: no CPU self-test, no
 118     watchdog, RAM/flash ECC events not handled, no MPU, faults report-only (WP-H-03 FM-CPU-01,
 119     FM-CPU-03, FM-RAM-02, FM-FLS-02; GAP-07, GAP-08). The only hardware mechanism with a reaction is
 120     the CSS on the HSE. With the CPU, SRAM and flash mostly in λ_SPF, SPFM for SG-01 would be far
 121     below 90 %.
 122  2. For SG-07, relay stuck intercepting (FM-REL-02/04) and MCU hang with the relay driven
 123     (FM-CPU-03/05) are single-point faults with no mechanism. Their share depends on the relay's
 124     failure rate and mode distribution, which is unknown.
 125  3. LFM: the ECC logic itself, the CSS, the relay-malfunction check and the heartbeat timeout are
 126     not tested at start-up, so their faults are latent. The relay-malfunction check is exercised only
 127     when a stock message appears, which in normal operation never happens.
 128  
 129  *Status note 2026-10-12:* since 2026-10-12 DC-01 (IWDG1, window mode, fed from the 100 Hz safety task; panda `9f60e17`) is implemented, **not verified**. No DC credit is taken for it until HIL fault-injection evidence exists and the LSI tolerance is confirmed ([watchdog impact record](../07-supporting/impact/change-2026-10-12-panda-hw-watchdog.md) OI-3 and OI-2). Its own latent fault (LSI failure, WP-H-03 FM-CLK-04) needs the start-up test of OI-4 below. The assessment above is otherwise unchanged.
 130  
 131  **With the firmware-only changes of WP-H-02 §6 (DC-01, DC-04…DC-10):** SPFM ≥ 90 % for SG-01 looks
 132  *plausible but not certain*. The CPU core remains at "medium" coverage at best on a single
 133  non-lockstep core with a software test library; whether that is enough depends on the CPU's share
 134  of the total rate. ASIL C (97 %) is *unlikely* without DC-02 (external supervisor) or a second
 135  channel. This supports the FSC recommendation of option (c).
 136  
 137  **For SG-07:** ASIL B is *unlikely* to be met without relay readback (DC-03) unless the relay's
 138  "stuck intercepting" rate is shown to be small relative to the total, which needs part data.
 139  
 140  ![Figure 5-1: SPFM and LFM status: calculation steps 1 to 7 not done or qualitative only for lack of a BOM, and for SG-01, SG-03/SG-04, SG-05, SG-02/SG-06 and SG-07 the ASIL targets marked with no computed value](figures/fig-h04-5-1-metric-status.svg)
 141  
 142  *Figure 5-1.* Top: the calculation steps of §3.2 with their status from the §5 table. Bottom: the §4 targets per safety-goal row of §7 on a 50–100 % scale; the hatched track means no value exists (every “Met?” cell in §7 is “Not yet computed”). The right column repeats the engineering judgement of §5.1, which is not a metric. Drawn from §4, §5, §5.1 and §7. Source: [`figures/fig-h04-5-1-metric-status.svg`](figures/fig-h04-5-1-metric-status.svg); change it together with §4, §5 and §7.

256### 6.3 First quantitative estimate with generic failure rates (BL-002)

Meanwhile a block-level FMEDA with **generic** failure rates is kept as YAML in [`../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg001.yaml`](../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg001.yaml) and [`fmeda-sg009.yaml`](../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg009.yaml) (SG IDs of LD-HARA-001: SG-001 = SG-01 here, SG-009 = SG-07). The metrics are computed by `tools/safety/fmea_lint.py`, and the same formulas are used in the workbooks from `tools/safety/export_fmeda_xlsx.py`.

262| Goal | Target used | λ (FIT) | SPFM | LFM | λSPF + λRF (FIT) | Result |
263|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
264| SG-01 lateral | ASIL D (LD-HARA-001); ASIL C here | 220 | 96.9 % | 87.7 % | 6.9 | Misses D on SPFM and LFM; misses C on SPFM (97 %) by 0.1 point |
