   1  # WP-H-03 Hardware safety analysis (FMEA / FMEDA)
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-H-03 Hardware safety analysis (FMEA/FMEDA) |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-5:2018 §7 (safety analyses on HW design), §8 (metrics input), Annex D (diagnostic coverage of safety mechanisms, informative); ISO 26262-9:2018 §8 (safety analyses); ISO 26262-11:2018 (semiconductor failure modes, informative); ASPICE 4.0 HWE.2 |
   7  | Version | 0.2 (status update 2026-10-12: IWDG (DC-01), 0.3 s heartbeat revocation and the reference-build `0xc5` refusal recorded in the existing-diagnostic cells of FM-CPU-03/05/06, FM-CLK-04, FM-SPI-02, FM-REL-05; no DC credit taken until HIL evidence). 0.1: initial draft |
   8  | Status | Draft (qualitative FMEA); quantitative FMEDA: generic-rate estimate in §6.3, part-level template TBD |
   9  | ASIL / scope | ASIL C (provisional, SG-01) / ASIL B (SG-02…SG-07) |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1 minimum, external reviewer, T-09) |
  12  | Approver | TBD (per [WP-M-02](../01-management/WP-M-02-safety-plan.md)) |
  13  | Baseline | `bf6ce8c` (status update 2026-10-12) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose
  16  
  17  Closes the first step of **GAP-15** (no hardware safety analysis). It provides:
  18  
  19  1. the method for the hardware FMEA and FMEDA (§2);
  20  2. an initial **qualitative, block-level FMEA** of the safety-relevant hardware identified in
  21     [WP-H-02](WP-H-02-hardware-design.md) §3.2, with existing diagnostics and coverage estimates (§4);
  22  3. an **FMEDA table template** ready for failure rates once a BOM exists (§6).
  23  
  24  The quantitative results feed [WP-H-04](WP-H-04-hardware-metrics.md) (SPFM/LFM) and
  25  [WP-H-05](WP-H-05-random-hardware-failures-pmhf.md) (PMHF). Nothing quantitative is computed here:
  26  every rate and every metric is **TBD — requires BOM**.
  27  
  28  ## 2. Method
  29  
  30  ### 2.1 Approach
  31  
  32  | Step | Content | State |
  33  |---|---|---|
  34  | 1 | Define safety goals and safe states the hardware can violate (SG-01…SG-07, [WP-C-03](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md); safe states from [WP-C-04](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md) §6) | Done (inputs) |
  35  | 2 | Block-level qualitative FMEA (inductive): for each block, failure modes → local effect → effect on each SG → existing detection → fault class | **This version** |
  36  | 3 | Deductive check: hardware fault-tree branches under each SG top event (in [WP-A-04](../08-analyses/WP-A-04-system-fta-fmea.md)) to confirm no block is missing | Planned |
  37  | 4 | Component-level FMEDA: BOM parts, failure rates, failure-mode distribution, safety-related flag, fault classification (safe, single-point, residual, multiple-point detected/perceived/latent), DC per mechanism | Template (§6), blocked by BOM |
  38  | 5 | MCU internal FMEDA: split the STM32H7 into parts/sub-parts (CPU, flash, SRAM, buses, clock, power, FDCAN, GPIO, ADC, DMA, SPI, IWDG) per the ISO 26262-11 approach | Template (§6.2), needs supplier FIT or a die/package model |
  39  | 6 | Iterate with the design changes of WP-H-02 §6 and recompute | Planned |
  40  
  41  Fault classes used (ISO 26262-5 / -10 terms): **SPF** single-point fault (no mechanism covers
  42  it); **RF** residual fault (part of a fault not covered by a mechanism); **MPF-D/-P/-L**
  43  multiple-point fault detected, perceived or latent; **S** safe fault.
  44  
  45  ### 2.2 Diagnostic coverage scale
  46  
  47  The FMEA rates each mechanism with the three-level scale used in ISO 26262-5 Annex D. In our
  48  words:
  49  
  50  | Level | Meaning used here | Typical numeric value for the FMEDA |
  51  |---|---|---|
  52  | Low | The mechanism catches the fault only in some modes or with a long latency, or checks only part of the relevant logic | 60 % |
  53  | Medium | Catches most failure modes of the element, with known blind spots | 90 % |
  54  | High | Catches practically all failure modes of the element within the required time | 99 % |
  55  | None | No mechanism | 0 % |
  56  
  57  The numeric values are the conventional anchors of the Annex D scale; **verify against the
  58  licensed text**. A coverage claim above "low" requires justification (analysis or fault injection,
  59  [WP-H-06](WP-H-06-hardware-integration-verification.md)).
  60  
  61  ### 2.3 Failure-rate sources (proposal)
  62  
  63  | Element class | Proposed source | Reason |
  64  |---|---|---|
  65  | Discrete parts, relay, connectors, crystal, transceivers, regulators | **SN 29500** (Siemens) series, with its reference conditions adjusted to the mission profile | Widely used in automotive FMEDAs, covers relays and connectors, publicly purchasable, consistent stress model |
  66  | Integrated circuits without supplier data | SN 29500 (IC part) as primary; **IEC 61709** for the conversion of reference conditions | Consistency with the above |
  67  | STM32H7 (die + package) | Supplier FIT and failure-mode distribution from ST if obtainable ([WP-H-07](WP-H-07-hardware-component-qualification.md) QE-02); otherwise a die/package model of the IEC TR 62380 type as referenced in ISO 26262-11 (IEC TR 62380 is no longer maintained by IEC — verify status) | ISO 26262-11 gives the method for splitting a microcontroller into parts and distributing failure modes |
  68  | Soft errors (SRAM, flash, registers) | Supplier soft-error data (SER in FIT/Mbit) if available; otherwise a literature value with justification | Transient faults dominate SRAM; ECC credit depends on it |
  69  | Harness wiring and crimps | SN 29500 (connections) | |
  70  
  71  One source per class is used throughout; mixing handbooks within one class is avoided. The
  72  choice is to be confirmed by the safety manager (OI-2).
  73  
  74  ### 2.4 Mission profile (placeholder)
  75  
  76  To be defined in WP-H-05 §3: vehicle service life, operating hours per year, ambient and
  77  in-cabin temperature profile (windscreen-mounted device, sun load), and on/off cycles. Needed
  78  for SN 29500 stress factors and for the latent-fault exposure time.
  79  
  80  ## 3. Safety-goal violation mechanisms considered
  81  
  82  | SG | How a hardware fault can violate it |
  83  |---|---|
  84  | SG-01 | Envelope lets through an excessive or unauthorised `0x2E4` torque (wrong computation, corrupted limit or `controls_allowed`, corrupted frame after the check); relay stuck released while stock camera also commands (detected today, with delay) |
  85  | SG-02, SG-06 | Loss of actuation without warning: MCU hang or reset while engaged with no acoustic warning (siren path) and SoC not alerting |
  86  | SG-03, SG-04 | Excessive or unauthorised `0x343` acceleration/deceleration (same mechanisms as SG-01) |
  87  | SG-05 | Override/brake not acted on: corrupted RX processing or stuck `controls_allowed` |
  88  | SG-07 | Camera PCS messages blocked or altered: relay stuck intercepting with forwarding stopped (MCU hang, MCU in reset with relay held, readback absent), transceiver disturbing the joined bus, harness open |
  89  
  90  The vehicle-side measures (EPS torque limitation and LKA timeout, AOU-01R; PCM clamp, AOU-05R)
  91  are **external measures**. They are not counted as diagnostic coverage; they appear in the PMHF
  92  argument ([WP-H-05](WP-H-05-random-hardware-failures-pmhf.md) §5).
  93  
  94  ## 4. Qualitative block-level FMEA
  95  
  96  Code paths are under `panda/board/` unless prefixed. "DC today" is the coverage of the mechanisms
  97  present at baseline `8b8c6ae`; "DC planned" assumes the WP-H-02 §6 changes (DC-xx) are made and
  98  verified.
  99  
 100  *Status note 2026-10-12:* the IWDG (DC-01, panda `9f60e17`) and the 0.3 s heartbeat revocation (panda `c2e5d93`) are now implemented but **not verified on hardware**. They are recorded in the "Existing diagnostic" cells below; "DC today" stays at the baseline `8b8c6ae` values, and no DC is credited for them until HIL fault-injection evidence exists ([watchdog impact record](../07-supporting/impact/change-2026-10-12-panda-hw-watchdog.md) OI-3) and the LSI tolerance is confirmed (OI-2).
 101  
 102  ### 4.1 MCU core (CPU, buses, interrupt controller)
 103  
 104  | FM-ID | Failure mode | Local effect | SG effect | Class (today) | Existing diagnostic | DC today | Planned mechanism | DC planned |
 105  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 106  | FM-CPU-01 | Wrong instruction execution / wrong data path result (permanent) | Safety check computes wrong result (e.g. torque limit passes out-of-range value, `controls_allowed` wrongly true) | SG-01, SG-03, SG-04, SG-05 | SPF | None in MCU. Host compares `controls_allowed` with its own state after 2 s (`controlsMismatch`, onboard review) — QM, slow, and does not check limits | None | Software CPU self-test library at start-up and periodically (vendor STL if available for STM32H7, WP-H-07 QE-03), plus control-flow monitoring with the IWDG (DC-01) | Medium (single core, no lockstep; "high" is not credible without hardware redundancy) |
 107  | FM-CPU-02 | Transient upset (soft error in registers/pipeline) | As FM-CPU-01 for one cycle | SG-01… (one frame) | RF | None | None | Same as above; per-frame limit re-evaluation bounds a single-frame effect | Low–medium |
 108  | FM-CPU-03 | Program-flow hang (stuck in loop or ISR, stuck critical section) | TX stops; forwarding stops; relay stays in last state | SG-07 (relay driven, PCS forwarding lost); SG-02/06 (actuation lost — EPS timeout — without MCU warning) | SPF for SG-07 | SW watchdog in the same ISR (`drivers/simple_watchdog.h:7-17`) cannot see a hang; host sees missing panda states (QM). **2026-10-12:** IWDG window, started at the first relay drive, fed from the 100 Hz safety task after main-loop progress (`drivers/hw_watchdog.h`, panda `9f60e17`); no DC claimed until HIL | Low (host only) | IWDG window + safe service point (DC-01) → reset → relay released | High (for hang); medium for timing faults |
 109  | FM-CPU-04 | Unexpected exception (HardFault/NMI) | Reset via cookie path (`early_init.h:73-80`) | Transient loss of function; safe for SG-01; relay released after reset *(if HWSR-502 holds)* | S / MPF-D | Reset + `HEALTH_FLAG_*_RESET` reported (`main.c:319-325`) | High (for this mode) | — | High |
 110  | FM-CPU-05 | Exception routed to `Default_Handler` (unused vector, SVC, PendSV, DebugMon) | Infinite loop (`stm32h7/startup_stm32h7x5xx.s:111-114`) | As FM-CPU-03 | SPF for SG-07 | None. **2026-10-12:** the IWDG ends the loop in a reset once the relay has been driven (as FM-CPU-03); no DC claimed until HIL | None | DC-08 + IWDG | High |
 111  | FM-CPU-06 | Interrupt controller fault: CAN or tick IRQ lost or storm | Missing checks or overload | SG-01 (missed RX checks), SG-07 | RF | Interrupt-rate monitor, report-only (`drivers/interrupts.h:34-37`). **2026-10-12:** a lost safety-task IRQ stops the IWDG feed; a safety-task storm that feeds before the window opens (≈ 5 ms) resets; no DC claimed until HIL | Low | DC-04 (reaction) + IWDG | Medium |
 112  | FM-CPU-07 | DMA fault (SPI DMA2 writes wrong memory) | Corrupt safety state | SG-01… | RF | None | None | MPU (DC-06) on safety data; RAM ECC does not cover wrong-address writes | Medium |

 203  
 204  ## 5. Findings
 205  
 206  1. **Dominant single-point faults today**: FM-CPU-01 (no CPU diagnostics), FM-RAM-02/03 (ECC
 207     not listened to), FM-FLS-02 (no run-time flash check), FM-REL-02/04 (relay stuck intercepting —
 208     SG-07), FM-CPU-03/05 (hang with relay driven — SG-07), FM-PWR-02 (brown-out zone). With these
 209     the SPFM for SG-01 and SG-07 cannot reach the ASIL B target (WP-H-04 §4).
 210  2. **SG-07 has a hardware single-point path that no firmware change can close**: a relay stuck in
 211     the intercept position (FM-REL-02/04). Only readback (DC-03) or a harness design that makes this
 212     failure mode implausible (and evidence for it) can close it.
 213  3. **The "report-only" pattern (GAP-08) is the main reason diagnostics present in silicon give no
 214     coverage**: CSS is the only hardware mechanism with a reaction today.
 215  4. **No credible "high" coverage for the CPU** without hardware redundancy. With a software self-test
 216     library plus IWDG plus the vehicle external measures, the argument relies on the ASIL B target
 217     (FSC option (c)). An ASIL C claim for SG-01 would need DC-02 or a second channel (FSC §4.3).
 218

 256  ### 6.3 First quantitative estimate with generic failure rates (BL-002)
 257  
 258  §6.1 and §6.2 stay TBD until the BOM is known. Meanwhile a block-level FMEDA with **generic** failure rates is kept as YAML in [`../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg001.yaml`](../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg001.yaml) and [`fmeda-sg009.yaml`](../08-analyses/fmea/analyses/fmeda-sg009.yaml) (SG IDs of LD-HARA-001: SG-001 = SG-01 here, SG-009 = SG-07). The metrics are computed by `tools/safety/fmea_lint.py`, and the same formulas are used in the workbooks from `tools/safety/export_fmeda_xlsx.py`.
 259  
 260  The rates are order-of-magnitude estimates per part type, not values looked up for the actual parts. The results show where the architecture falls short. **They are not evidence of compliance.**
 261  
 262  | Goal | Target used | λ (FIT) | SPFM | LFM | λSPF + λRF (FIT) | Result |
 263  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
 264  | SG-01 lateral | ASIL D (LD-HARA-001); ASIL C here | 220 | 96.9 % | 87.7 % | 6.9 | Misses D on SPFM and LFM; misses C on SPFM (97 %) by 0.1 point |
 265  | SG-07 stock PCS | ASIL B | 242 | 86.8 % | 98.9 % | 32.0 | Misses B on SPFM |
 266  
 267  Largest contributors:
 268  - **SG-01:**
 269    - wrong result of a CPU limit or authority check, fully latent (12.5 FIT λMPF,L);
 270    - clock drift with no cross-check (5.0);
 271    - supply in the brown-out zone (4.5 FIT λSPF).
 272  - **SG-07:**
 273    - CAN transceiver faults with the relay driven (12.0 FIT λSPF);
 274    - relay welded or driver stuck on (6.0);
 275    - stock-path open in the relay box (3.0);
 276    - intermittent relay contact (3.0).
 277  
 278  **Sensitivity.** About 90 of the 220 FIT are single-bit SRAM upsets that ECC corrects. They count as covered multiple-point faults and so raise SPFM. With the SRAM soft-error rate at 10, 100 and 500 FIT for the 64 KB considered, the results move as follows:
 279  
 280  | SRAM rate (FIT) | SG-01 SPFM | SG-07 SPFM |
 281  |---|---|---|
 282  | 10 | 95.3 % | 79.2 % |
 283  | 100 | 96.9 % | 86.8 % |
 284  | 500 | 98.4 % | 94.7 % |
 285  
 286  A real SER figure for the STM32H725 (OI-3) therefore matters as much as the part list.
 287  
 288  **With the planned measures**, using conservative DC values:
 289  - **SG-01**, measures added in this order:
 290    - BOR/PVD reaction (DFM-ACT-005);
 291    - RAMECC double-error reaction (DFM-ACT-002);
 292    - clock cross-check (DFM-ACT-004);
 293    - a low-coverage CPU self-test (DFM-ACT-001);
 294    - TX read-back (TSR-517).
 295  
 296    Result: SPFM 99.3 %, LFM 95.3 %, λSPF + λRF 1.5 FIT, which meets ASIL D on generic rates.
 297  - **SG-07**, measures added in this order:
 298    - relay readback (DFM-ACT-007, DC-03);
 299    - releasing the relay on a transceiver fault (DFM-ACT-006);
 300    - a readback that also checks stock-path continuity.
 301  
 302    Result: SPFM 96.0 %, λSPF + λRF 9.6 FIT, which meets ASIL B. Readback alone reaches 90.5 %.
 303  - **Caveat for SG-07:** a welded relay cannot be released. The readback can only warn that stock AEB is unavailable, so the safe state for that case has to be a warning (OI-9).
 304

 317  | OI-9 | SG-07: decide the safe state when relay readback finds the relay welded in the intercept position (it cannot be released): driver warning that stock AEB is unavailable, and whether that is acceptable under SG-07 | G2 |
 318  | OI-10 | Replace the generic rates of §6.3 with part-specific values (BOM, ST data, SER) and add the dual-point PMHF term | G3 |

A09 Documented warning latency exceeds the stated warning budget

Documented discrepancy · Safety manager / system lead · G2

WP-S-04:267 · subject 20569e916c93 · SHA256 0a85dd04490e185e…

263| OI-4 | Measure the PCS message period and braking profile on the reference vehicle (A-PCS-1, A-PCS-2) and confirm or replace the preliminary SG-07 FTTI of §3.6; agree the acceptable speed-reduction loss in the HARA review | Safety engineer | G2 |
264| OI-7 | SG-07 forwarding loss has zero margin (FDTI + FRTI = FTTI). Decide whether to shorten the watchdog and forwarding-supervision times below 0.1 s once the PCS timing is measured | Safety engineer | G2 |
265| OI-5 | Execute M-01…M-11 and fill §6 results | Test lead | G4 |
266| OI-6 | Update FSC §8 budgets after this document is approved (FSC OI-4) | Safety engineer | G2 |
267| OI-8 | EPS-fault siren latency ≤ 1.26 s worst case from fault onset (T-28; corrected 2026-10-13 from ≈ 1.14 s: the speaker sounds only on every other 8 Hz tick) exceeds the SG-02 1 s warning budget (§3.7). TSR-516's ≤ 0.5 s after detection is met (≤ 0.26 s) only because WP-S-02 v0.14 defines detection as the 1 s persistence; measured from the first fault frame it is not. Caused by the maintainer decision of 2026-10-12 (persistent faults only, 1 s; EPS-fault impact record §7). Options: shorten the persistence (e.g. 0.7 s), or justify that LKA actuation is already refused from the first fault frame (lateral authority removed at onset; the warning is for a lasting loss). **Decision needed.** No requirement changed here (added 2026-10-12) | Safety manager | G2 |
268| OI-9 | Heartbeat loss when authority ended within the 5 s before it: TSR-516(a) asks for the siren ≤ 0.5 s after detection, but the fast siren (T-07a) sounds only when authority is granted at the revocation; this case keeps the 1 Hz siren at 4–5 s (T-07). Decide whether the case needs a warning within the SG-02 budget (the driver has already seen the disengagement) and align TSR-516(a) or the firmware. No requirement changed here (added 2026-10-13) | Safety manager | G2 |

Why it matters: The document records up to 1.26 s against SG-02 1 s; starting the clock after 1 s persistence can conceal end-to-end delay. This is a documented requirement discrepancy, not a new hardware measurement.

AI-author preparation: Draft an end-to-end timing discrepancy table and remedy options; include onset, detection, reaction and warning clocks.

Closure evidence: Approved consistent timing budget and witnessed end-to-end traces including tolerance and jitter.

Original document at the assessed commit (read-only)
   1  # WP-S-04 Timing Analysis: FTTI, Fault Detection and Reaction Budget
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-S-04 Timing analysis: FTTI, fault detection and reaction budget |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-4:2018 §6 (fault tolerant time interval, fault detection and fault reaction time intervals, emergency operation); ISO 26262-3:2018 §7 (FTTI as FSC input); ISO 26262-1:2018 (definitions, by reference); ASPICE 4.0 SYS.3 |
   7  | Version | 0.5 (heartbeat-loss siren, panda `c33343b`; EPS-siren worst case 1.26 s; 2026-10-13). 0.4 (§5 Figures 5-1 and 5-2 added: timing budgets per safety goal against the code-derived times, and the EPS-fault warning chain of OI-8; generated from §3.7 and §4 by `figures/fig_s04_timing.py`; no number changed; 2026-10-13). 0.3 (status update 2026-10-12: §4 code-derived times for the reference build at `bf6ce8c` (T-01, T-06a/b, T-09a, T-26…T-29), §5 verdicts, OI-8 EPS-fault warning latency; nothing verified on hardware). History: 0.2 (§3.6 preliminary SG-07 FTTI and budget, RR-2026-005 F-02; 2026-10-12) |
   8  | Status | Draft — kinematic estimates and code-derived times only; no measurement has been performed; not reviewed independently |
   9  | ASIL / scope | SG-01 (C until re-rated, B‡), SG-02…SG-07 (B) |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1) |
  12  | Approver | Safety manager |
  13  | Baseline | `bf6ce8c` (status update 2026-10-12; opendbc `7993399`, panda `b790fca`). Original analysis at `8b8c6ae` (opendbc `229dc70`, panda `92eb565`) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose
  16  
  17  The HARA gives preliminary FTTIs ([WP-C-03 §6](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md)) and the FSC splits them into detection and reaction budgets ([WP-C-04 §8](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md)). This document:
  18  
  19  1. defines the method used to derive each FTTI from vehicle dynamics (§2, §3);
  20  2. gives first kinematic estimates, parameterised where vehicle data is missing (§3);
  21  3. lists the detection and reaction times of the baseline code, derived from the source (§4);
  22  4. compares them with the budgets and lists the required changes (§5);
  23  5. specifies how the times will be measured (§6).
  24  
  25  It closes GAP-06 at specification level. HARA OI-3 and FSC OI-4 depend on it.
  26  
  27  ## 2. Definitions and method
  28  
  29  | Term | Use in this document |
  30  |---|---|
  31  | FTTI | Time from the occurrence of a fault in the item to the earliest possible occurrence of the hazardous event, if no safety mechanism acts |
  32  | FDTI | Fault detection time interval: fault occurrence → detection |
  33  | FRTI | Fault reaction time interval: detection → safe state reached at the actuator (EPS torque 0, PCM inactive) |
  34  | Budget rule | FDTI + FRTI ≤ FTTI, with a margin of at least 20 % of the FTTI for scheduling jitter, CAN latency and EPS/PCM response |
  35  | Driver reaction t_r | Not part of the FTTI. Used separately for controllability (C class) and for warning budgets (SG-02, SG-06) |
  36  
  37  Method per safety goal:
  38  
  39  1. Take the worst credible malfunctioning behaviour that the fault class can produce **without** the mechanism under analysis (e.g. EPS at its own LKA authority limit T_EPS when the envelope is defeated; envelope-limited torque when only the detection is missing).
  40  2. Integrate the vehicle response (lateral deviation, gap closure) from fault onset.
  41  3. FTTI = time until the response reaches the hazard threshold (lateral margin to the adjacent lane, minimum safe gap).
  42  4. Split into FDTI and FRTI per mechanism; compare with code-derived and measured times.
  43  
  44  Faults whose effect stays inside the envelope limits (e.g. a wrong but limited command from the QM SoC) are handled by the limits acting every frame and by driver controllability; they do not need detection within the FTTI. The FTTI matters for faults that **defeat** a limit or gate: lost or wrong RX data, SoC command path stalls with the EPS holding torque, MCU faults, relay faults.
  45  
  46  ## 3. FTTI derivation per safety goal
  47  
  48  ### 3.1 SG-01 lateral (lateral deviation vs time)
  49  
  50  Model: lateral acceleration error a_y builds either (i) as a step, (ii) with jerk j = 5 m/s³ (controller jerk limit, `openpilot/selfdrive/controls/lib/drive_helpers.py:9-14`), or (iii) as a linear ramp over 1.0 s, which is the envelope torque rate limit (15 raw/frame → 1500 raw in 100 frames, `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/modes/toyota.h:174`; host comment "1.0s time to peak torque", `opendbc_repo/opendbc/car/toyota/values.py:46`). Lateral deviation y(t) is integrated from rest relative to the lane. Speed does not appear because a_y is the input; the speed dependence enters through how much a_y a given torque produces (TSR-102).
  51  
  52  Time (s) to reach lateral deviation d:
  53  
  54  | d | a_y = 1 m/s² | 2 m/s² | 3 m/s² | 4 m/s² | 5 m/s² | Profile |
  55  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  56  | 0.3 m | 0.77 | 0.55 | 0.45 | 0.39 | 0.35 | step |
  57  | 0.6 m | 1.10 | 0.77 | 0.63 | 0.55 | 0.49 | step |
  58  | 0.9 m | 1.34 | 0.95 | 0.77 | 0.67 | 0.60 | step |
  59  | 0.6 m | 1.19 | 0.97 | 0.91 | 0.90 | 0.90 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  60  | 0.9 m | 1.44 | 1.14 | 1.05 | 1.03 | 1.03 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  61  | 0.6 m | 1.56 | 1.22 | 1.06 | 0.97 | 0.90 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  62  | 0.9 m | 1.81 | 1.40 | 1.22 | 1.11 | 1.03 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  63  
  64  Hazard threshold: the lateral margin from lane centre to the adjacent lane boundary, (lane width − vehicle width)/2. With the Corolla width ≈ 1.78 m (to be confirmed from the reference vehicle record) and the ODD lane widths (2.9–3.9 m, [WP-C-02 §3.1](../02-concept/WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md)): 0.56–1.06 m. The design value is **0.6 m** (narrow arterial lane), which is conservative for highways. For comparison, `docs/SAFETY.md:32` cites 0.9 s to 1 m at maximum actuation.
  65  
  66  Estimates:
  67  
  68  | Fault class | Worst behaviour | a_y assumption | FTTI estimate | Note |
  69  |---|---|---|---|---|
  70  | Envelope defeated (MCU fault, wrong mode, relay fault letting camera/SoC frames through unchecked) | EPS at its own authority T_EPS, step onset | a_y,EPS unknown (AOU-01R); 3–5 m/s² assumed | **0.5–0.6 s** | Supports the preliminary ≤ 0.5 s of the HARA |
  71  | Gate defeated (stale `0x1D2`/`0x226`, authority not revoked), limits intact | envelope-limited torque, 1 s ramp | a_y at τ_max: today unknown; after TSR-102 ≤ target (e.g. 2 m/s²) | 1.2 s at 2 m/s²; 0.9–1.1 s at 3–5 m/s² | Applies to SG-05 too (driver override not honoured) |
  72  | SoC command path stalls with EPS holding last torque | constant a_y at last torque (≤ τ_max) | as above, step from steady state | 0.6–0.8 s at 2–3 m/s² | Only if the EPS holds torque during drop-out (NF-02) |
  73  
  74  Conclusion: **FTTI(SG-01) = 0.5 s** is kept as the design value for faults that defeat the envelope; the budget of FSC §8 (detect ≤ 0.3 s, react ≤ 0.1 s, margin 0.1 s) holds. The value must be confirmed once a_y,EPS and τ_max(v) are measured (§6).
  75  
  76  ### 3.2 SG-02 / SG-06 loss without warning
  77  
  78  Hazard: departure (SG-02) or failure to decelerate (SG-06) before the driver takes over. The relevant time is fault → take-over request, plus driver reaction.
  79  
  80  | Item | Value | Basis |
  81  |---|---|---|
  82  | Lateral drift when assistance is lost in a curve | Loss of the curvature-holding a_y, up to 2.5–3.0 m/s² at the ODD curve bound (R_ODD = v²/2.5 m/s²) | WP-C-02 §3.2 |
  83  | Time to 0.6 m at 2.5 m/s² step | ≈ 0.69 s | §3.1 kinematics |
  84  | Driver reaction to an acoustic take-over request (attentive, hands near wheel) | 0.75–1.5 s (to be confirmed in [WP-C-08](../02-concept/WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) §8) | assumption |
  85  
  86  The physical time to departure in a curve (≈ 0.7 s) is shorter than driver reaction, so SG-02 in curves at the ODD bound relies on the warning being given at once and on torque being ramped rather than cut (FSR-02.04). **Warning budget: ≤ 0.5 s detection + ≤ 0.5 s to warning (FSC §8) is not sufficient alone for curves at the ODD bound;** it is sufficient on straight roads and gentle curves. This is a controllability and ODD question (OI-2), recorded for the HARA owner. For SG-06 (stationary lead at 30 m/s, decel need 3.5 m/s²), the margin depends on distance; the 1 s budget is kept.
  87  
  88  ### 3.3 SG-03 unintended acceleration
  89  
  90  Gap closure with ego acceleration error a relative to a lead at constant speed: Δs = ½·a·t².
  91  
  92  | a | 0.5 s | 1.0 s | 1.5 s | 2.0 s |
  93  |---|---|---|---|---|
  94  | 1.0 m/s² | 0.12 m | 0.50 m | 1.12 m | 2.0 m |
  95  | 2.0 m/s² | 0.25 m | 1.0 m | 2.25 m | 4.0 m |
  96  
  97  At typical following gaps (time gap ≥ 1.0 s → ≥ 20 m at 20 m/s) the gap loss within 1 s is ≤ 1 m at the envelope bound (+2.0 m/s²). HE-03.2 (launch toward a pedestrian from standstill) is the governing case: at 2.0 m/s² the vehicle moves 1 m in 1 s. **FTTI(SG-03) = 1 s** is kept; the standstill-launch case should be checked with the actual pedestrian distance assumptions in WP-C-08 (OI-3).
  98  
  99  ### 3.4 SG-04 excessive deceleration
 100  
 101  Following vehicle at time gap h, same speed v, reacting after t_r with equal deceleration: gap reduction ≈ v·t_r (independent of the deceleration level once both brake equally); collision if v·t_r ≥ h·v, i.e. t_r ≥ h. The hazard depends on the follower, not on detection time, so SG-04 is governed by the **deceleration onset limit and magnitude** (TSR-202, TSR-205) rather than by an FTTI for detection. For faults that defeat the bound (e.g. inactive transition step), FTTI(SG-04) = 1 s is kept as preliminary.
 102  
 103  ### 3.5 SG-05 release on driver action
 104  
 105  Requirement-based: the driver expects release within the time of their own action. The HARA sets ≤ 0.2 s from driver input to release. Budget: brake/cruise message period (25–30 ms) + envelope reaction (1 frame) + EPS/PCM response (TBD, AOU-01R/03R). Steering override: driver torque message period (20 ms at 50 Hz) + t_ovr (TSR-304).
 106  
 107  ### 3.6 SG-07 PCS preservation
 108  
 109  SG-07 has two parts with different timing.
 110  
 111  - **Content.** The item must not originate or alter a PCS-relevant frame. This is a per-frame property of the TX gate (TSR-705) and the forwarding block list (TSR-704). It has no detection budget: a wrong frame is either never sent or the design is wrong.
 112  - **Forwarding loss or delay.** While the relay intercepts, the stock camera frames that carry the PCS request reach the car only through the safety MCU. If forwarding stops (MCU hang, camera-side bus fault, a QM configuration command, queue overflow; WP-A-04 §8), the PCS braking request is delayed until the fault is detected and the relay returns the stock path. This part needs an FTTI.
 113  
 114  Hazard: the stock PCS brakes later than it would without the item, so less speed is removed before impact. If braking starts d seconds late and would otherwise have continued until impact, the speed reduction lost is at most a_PCS · d.
 115  
 116  | Item | Value | Basis |
 117  |---|---|---|
 118  | PCS braking deceleration a_PCS | ≈ 6 m/s² (assumed full PCS braking on dry road) | assumption A-PCS-1, to be measured (§6) |
 119  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.2 s | 6 × 0.2 = 1.2 m/s ≈ 4.3 km/h | kinematics |
 120  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.5 s | 3.0 m/s ≈ 10.8 km/h | kinematics |
 121  | PCS message period on the camera-side bus | not known | to be measured from drive logs (§6, OI-4) |
 122  | Relay drop-out time after the coil is released | ≈ 10–20 ms for typical automotive relays | assumption A-PCS-2; datasheet (WP-H-02) |
 123  
 124  **Preliminary FTTI(SG-07, forwarding loss) = 0.2 s.** A delay of 0.2 s removes at most about 4 km/h of the speed reduction the stock PCS would achieve, which is small next to the reductions an AEB function is designed for. A delay of 0.5 s or more (10 km/h or more) is not. This is engineering judgement on assumed values, recorded so the budget can be checked. It is replaced once the PCS braking profile and message timing are measured on the reference vehicle and the acceptable loss is agreed in the HARA review.
 125  
 126  Budget: **FDTI ≤ 0.1 s** to detect that forwarding has stopped (forwarding supervision, TSR-711; MCU hang through the hardware watchdog, TSR-501) and **FRTI ≤ 0.1 s** to release the relay (TSR-706) including relay drop-out.
 127  
 128  Consequences for the TSRs:
 129  
 130  - TSR-501 set the watchdog timeout to ≤ 0.2 s, which fits SG-01 (0.5 s) but not the SG-07 FDTI of 0.1 s. WP-S-02 v0.3 tightens it to ≤ 0.1 s. The alternative, releasing the relay by another path on an MCU hang, is open in WP-H-01 (HWSR-502). *Status 2026-10-12:* the IWDG is implemented with a 90 ms nominal timeout (reload 719, panda `b790fca`, T-09a); whether it stays ≤ 0.1 s depends on the LSI tolerance (watchdog impact record OI-2), so the zero margin of OI-7 is not yet shown.
 131  - TSR-706 releases the relay ≤ 0.1 s after the fault reaction. That fits the FRTI.
 132  - TSR-707 (forwarding latency ≤ 1 ms in normal operation) is far inside the budget.
 133  - No TSR detected that forwarding has stopped. TSR-711 is added in [WP-S-02](WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md) v0.3.
 134  
 135  ### 3.7 Summary of FTTIs and budgets
 136  
 137  | SG / fault class | FTTI | FDTI budget | FRTI budget | Margin |
 138  |---|---|---|---|---|
 139  | SG-01 continuous limits | per frame | 0 | ≤ 10 ms (+ TSR-109) | — |
 140  | SG-01 RX loss/corruption | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.2 s (TSR-401) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 141  | SG-01 SoC heartbeat loss | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-407) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 142  | SG-01 SoC command stall | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.05 s (TSR-408) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.35 s |
 143  | SG-01 MCU execution fault | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-501; set by SG-07; 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (reset + relay release) | ≥ 0.3 s |
 144  | SG-01 relay stuck | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-506) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 145  | SG-02/06 loss without warning | 1 s to warning | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.5 s | — (see §3.2). EPS-fault siren of the reference build ≤ 1.26 s worst case from fault onset exceeds this row (T-28, OI-8). Heartbeat-loss siren ≈ 0.56 s after the last heartbeat, within this row (T-07a, all builds since panda `c33343b`; 2026-10-13) |
 146  | SG-03/04 | 1 s | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 147  | SG-05 brake/cancel | 0.2 s | ≤ 30 ms | ≤ 10 ms + EPS/PCM | TBD |
 148  | SG-05 steering override | 0.2 s | ≤ 40 ms + t_ovr | ≤ 0.1 s | TBD |
 149  | SG-07 content (TX gate, block list) | per frame | 0 | — | — |
 150  | SG-07 forwarding loss | 0.2 s (preliminary, §3.6) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-711; MCU hang: TSR-501 ≤ 0.1 s, 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-706, incl. relay drop-out) | 0 (depends on LSI tolerance, OI-7) |
 151  
 152  ## 4. Current detection and reaction times (code-derived)
 153  
 154  | # | Mechanism | Code | Derivation | Worst-case time |
 155  |---|---|---|---|---|
 156  | T-01 | RX timeout | `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/safety.h:321-344`; called at 1 Hz from `panda/board/main.c:241-242` | threshold max(10 × period, 1 s); for the Corolla messages 10 × period is 120 ms (`0xAA` 83 Hz), 200 ms (`0x260` 50 Hz), 303 ms (`0x1D2` 33 Hz), 250 ms (`0x226` 40 Hz), so 1 s applies; evaluated once per second | **1–2 s** (at `8b8c6ae`). *Status 2026-10-12:* 5 nominal periods (`rx_timeout_periods`, opendbc `de80b63`), evaluated by the 100 Hz safety task (panda `171fcdf`): **≤ 0.16 s** for `0x1D2`; the reference build adds `0x262` (25 Hz, ≈ 0.21 s, slightly above TSR-401's 0.2 s if the real rate is 25 Hz) and `0x1D3` (33 Hz, ≈ 0.16 s) (opendbc `21d8dd8`). Not verified on hardware |
 157  | T-02 | RX checksum / quality | `safety.h:112-121, 164-197` | on receipt | 1 message period (12–30 ms) |
 158  | T-03 | RX counter | — | no Toyota counters (`modes/toyota.h:39-46`) | not detected |
 159  | T-04 | Brake / cruise revoke | `safety.h:350-356, 518-527` | on receipt | 25 ms (brake 40 Hz), 30 ms (cruise 33 Hz) |
 160  | T-05 | Heartbeat engaged mismatch | `panda/board/main.c:181-189` | 3 consecutive 1 Hz ticks | **2–3 s** |
 161  | T-06 | Heartbeat loss → SILENT | `main.c:101-103, 153-162, 191-213` | counter +1 per 1 Hz tick, reset by each `0xf3`; ≥ 5 with ignition, ≥ 2 without | **4–5 s** (ign on), 1–2 s (ign off) (at `8b8c6ae`; split into T-06a/T-06b below) |

Not verified on hardware |
 163  | T-06b | Heartbeat loss → SILENT (2026-10-12) | as T-06 | unchanged | 4–5 s (ign on), 1–2 s (ign off); TSR-510 (SILENT ≤ 2 s) not met |

T-07 (4–5 s) still sounds when it fires and alone covers authority that ended within the 5 s before the loss (OI-9). Not verified on hardware |

Hangs before the first relay drive not covered. Not verified on hardware (M-06) |

 177  | T-18 | Safety mode/param mismatch | `selfdrived.py:331-339` | only after 10 s from start | 10 s after start |

 220  Implementation note: the panda tick handler runs at 8 Hz and the safety checks are "decimated to 1 Hz" (`main.c:105-106, 142-143`). The required budgets need a periodic safety task of at least 50 Hz (TSR-401) on a timer independent of the comms interrupts, with its completion feeding the IWDG service point (TSR-501). The architectural change is recorded in [WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md).
 221  
 222  *Status 2026-10-12:* since panda `9f60e17` a 100 Hz safety task on TIM4 runs `safety_tick()`, the heartbeat monitor, the EPS-fault siren timer and the IWDG feed (`main.c` `safety_task_handler`). All interrupts are at one priority level (`irq_priorities.h`, panda `52cad44`), so the task can be delayed by other handlers; its latency under load is not measured ([WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md) §5.4.5). *2026-10-13:* the safety task also runs the heartbeat-loss siren timer (panda `c33343b`, T-07a). The 8 Hz / 1 Hz tick keeps the SILENT transition (T-06b), the engaged-mismatch check (T-05) and the relay grace counter (T-08).

A10 Curve-bound time to departure is shorter than assumed driver reaction

Documented discrepancy · HARA owner / dynamics lead · G2

WP-S-04:86 · subject 20569e916c93 · SHA256 0a85dd04490e185e…

82| Lateral drift when assistance is lost in a curve | Loss of the curvature-holding a_y, up to 2.5–3.0 m/s² at the ODD curve bound (R_ODD = v²/2.5 m/s²) | WP-C-02 §3.2 |
83| Time to 0.6 m at 2.5 m/s² step | ≈ 0.69 s | §3.1 kinematics |
84| Driver reaction to an acoustic take-over request (attentive, hands near wheel) | 0.75–1.5 s (to be confirmed in [WP-C-08](../02-concept/WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) §8) | assumption |
85
86The physical time to departure in a curve (≈ 0.7 s) is shorter than driver reaction, so SG-02 in curves at the ODD bound relies on the warning being given at once and on torque being ramped rather than cut (FSR-02.04). **Warning budget: ≤ 0.5 s detection + ≤ 0.5 s to warning (FSC §8) is not sufficient alone for curves at the ODD bound;** it is sufficient on straight roads and gentle curves. This is a controllability and ODD question (OI-2), recorded for the HARA owner. For SG-06 (stationary lead at 30 m/s, decel need 3.5 m/s²), the margin depends on distance; the 1 s budget is kept.
87
88### 3.3 SG-03 unintended acceleration
89
90Gap closure with ego acceleration error a relative to a lead at constant speed: Δs = ½·a·t².

Why it matters: The repository estimates roughly 0.7 s at the ODD bound and itself requests a controllability re-rating or tighter ODD. Driver supervision is not an automatic mitigation.

AI-author preparation: Prepare the curve-bound controllability/ODD decision with dynamics validation needs; do not silently tighten the ODD or lower ASIL.

Closure evidence: Measured validated model, updated ODD and accepted HARA/FTTI rationale.

Task: TASK-A10 · dependencies: TASK-A04

Original document at the assessed commit (read-only)
   1  # WP-S-04 Timing Analysis: FTTI, Fault Detection and Reaction Budget
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-S-04 Timing analysis: FTTI, fault detection and reaction budget |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-4:2018 §6 (fault tolerant time interval, fault detection and fault reaction time intervals, emergency operation); ISO 26262-3:2018 §7 (FTTI as FSC input); ISO 26262-1:2018 (definitions, by reference); ASPICE 4.0 SYS.3 |
   7  | Version | 0.5 (heartbeat-loss siren, panda `c33343b`; EPS-siren worst case 1.26 s; 2026-10-13). 0.4 (§5 Figures 5-1 and 5-2 added: timing budgets per safety goal against the code-derived times, and the EPS-fault warning chain of OI-8; generated from §3.7 and §4 by `figures/fig_s04_timing.py`; no number changed; 2026-10-13). 0.3 (status update 2026-10-12: §4 code-derived times for the reference build at `bf6ce8c` (T-01, T-06a/b, T-09a, T-26…T-29), §5 verdicts, OI-8 EPS-fault warning latency; nothing verified on hardware). History: 0.2 (§3.6 preliminary SG-07 FTTI and budget, RR-2026-005 F-02; 2026-10-12) |
   8  | Status | Draft — kinematic estimates and code-derived times only; no measurement has been performed; not reviewed independently |
   9  | ASIL / scope | SG-01 (C until re-rated, B‡), SG-02…SG-07 (B) |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1) |
  12  | Approver | Safety manager |
  13  | Baseline | `bf6ce8c` (status update 2026-10-12; opendbc `7993399`, panda `b790fca`). Original analysis at `8b8c6ae` (opendbc `229dc70`, panda `92eb565`) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose
  16  
  17  The HARA gives preliminary FTTIs ([WP-C-03 §6](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md)) and the FSC splits them into detection and reaction budgets ([WP-C-04 §8](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md)). This document:
  18  
  19  1. defines the method used to derive each FTTI from vehicle dynamics (§2, §3);
  20  2. gives first kinematic estimates, parameterised where vehicle data is missing (§3);
  21  3. lists the detection and reaction times of the baseline code, derived from the source (§4);
  22  4. compares them with the budgets and lists the required changes (§5);
  23  5. specifies how the times will be measured (§6).
  24  
  25  It closes GAP-06 at specification level. HARA OI-3 and FSC OI-4 depend on it.
  26  
  27  ## 2. Definitions and method
  28  
  29  | Term | Use in this document |
  30  |---|---|
  31  | FTTI | Time from the occurrence of a fault in the item to the earliest possible occurrence of the hazardous event, if no safety mechanism acts |
  32  | FDTI | Fault detection time interval: fault occurrence → detection |
  33  | FRTI | Fault reaction time interval: detection → safe state reached at the actuator (EPS torque 0, PCM inactive) |
  34  | Budget rule | FDTI + FRTI ≤ FTTI, with a margin of at least 20 % of the FTTI for scheduling jitter, CAN latency and EPS/PCM response |
  35  | Driver reaction t_r | Not part of the FTTI. Used separately for controllability (C class) and for warning budgets (SG-02, SG-06) |
  36  
  37  Method per safety goal:
  38  
  39  1. Take the worst credible malfunctioning behaviour that the fault class can produce **without** the mechanism under analysis (e.g. EPS at its own LKA authority limit T_EPS when the envelope is defeated; envelope-limited torque when only the detection is missing).
  40  2. Integrate the vehicle response (lateral deviation, gap closure) from fault onset.
  41  3. FTTI = time until the response reaches the hazard threshold (lateral margin to the adjacent lane, minimum safe gap).
  42  4. Split into FDTI and FRTI per mechanism; compare with code-derived and measured times.
  43  
  44  Faults whose effect stays inside the envelope limits (e.g. a wrong but limited command from the QM SoC) are handled by the limits acting every frame and by driver controllability; they do not need detection within the FTTI. The FTTI matters for faults that **defeat** a limit or gate: lost or wrong RX data, SoC command path stalls with the EPS holding torque, MCU faults, relay faults.
  45  
  46  ## 3. FTTI derivation per safety goal
  47  
  48  ### 3.1 SG-01 lateral (lateral deviation vs time)
  49  
  50  Model: lateral acceleration error a_y builds either (i) as a step, (ii) with jerk j = 5 m/s³ (controller jerk limit, `openpilot/selfdrive/controls/lib/drive_helpers.py:9-14`), or (iii) as a linear ramp over 1.0 s, which is the envelope torque rate limit (15 raw/frame → 1500 raw in 100 frames, `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/modes/toyota.h:174`; host comment "1.0s time to peak torque", `opendbc_repo/opendbc/car/toyota/values.py:46`). Lateral deviation y(t) is integrated from rest relative to the lane. Speed does not appear because a_y is the input; the speed dependence enters through how much a_y a given torque produces (TSR-102).
  51  
  52  Time (s) to reach lateral deviation d:
  53  
  54  | d | a_y = 1 m/s² | 2 m/s² | 3 m/s² | 4 m/s² | 5 m/s² | Profile |
  55  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  56  | 0.3 m | 0.77 | 0.55 | 0.45 | 0.39 | 0.35 | step |
  57  | 0.6 m | 1.10 | 0.77 | 0.63 | 0.55 | 0.49 | step |
  58  | 0.9 m | 1.34 | 0.95 | 0.77 | 0.67 | 0.60 | step |
  59  | 0.6 m | 1.19 | 0.97 | 0.91 | 0.90 | 0.90 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  60  | 0.9 m | 1.44 | 1.14 | 1.05 | 1.03 | 1.03 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  61  | 0.6 m | 1.56 | 1.22 | 1.06 | 0.97 | 0.90 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  62  | 0.9 m | 1.81 | 1.40 | 1.22 | 1.11 | 1.03 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  63  
  64  Hazard threshold: the lateral margin from lane centre to the adjacent lane boundary, (lane width − vehicle width)/2. With the Corolla width ≈ 1.78 m (to be confirmed from the reference vehicle record) and the ODD lane widths (2.9–3.9 m, [WP-C-02 §3.1](../02-concept/WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md)): 0.56–1.06 m. The design value is **0.6 m** (narrow arterial lane), which is conservative for highways. For comparison, `docs/SAFETY.md:32` cites 0.9 s to 1 m at maximum actuation.
  65  
  66  Estimates:
  67  
  68  | Fault class | Worst behaviour | a_y assumption | FTTI estimate | Note |
  69  |---|---|---|---|---|
  70  | Envelope defeated (MCU fault, wrong mode, relay fault letting camera/SoC frames through unchecked) | EPS at its own authority T_EPS, step onset | a_y,EPS unknown (AOU-01R); 3–5 m/s² assumed | **0.5–0.6 s** | Supports the preliminary ≤ 0.5 s of the HARA |
  71  | Gate defeated (stale `0x1D2`/`0x226`, authority not revoked), limits intact | envelope-limited torque, 1 s ramp | a_y at τ_max: today unknown; after TSR-102 ≤ target (e.g. 2 m/s²) | 1.2 s at 2 m/s²; 0.9–1.1 s at 3–5 m/s² | Applies to SG-05 too (driver override not honoured) |
  72  | SoC command path stalls with EPS holding last torque | constant a_y at last torque (≤ τ_max) | as above, step from steady state | 0.6–0.8 s at 2–3 m/s² | Only if the EPS holds torque during drop-out (NF-02) |
  73  
  74  Conclusion: **FTTI(SG-01) = 0.5 s** is kept as the design value for faults that defeat the envelope; the budget of FSC §8 (detect ≤ 0.3 s, react ≤ 0.1 s, margin 0.1 s) holds. The value must be confirmed once a_y,EPS and τ_max(v) are measured (§6).
  75  
  76  ### 3.2 SG-02 / SG-06 loss without warning
  77  
  78  Hazard: departure (SG-02) or failure to decelerate (SG-06) before the driver takes over. The relevant time is fault → take-over request, plus driver reaction.
  79  
  80  | Item | Value | Basis |
  81  |---|---|---|
  82  | Lateral drift when assistance is lost in a curve | Loss of the curvature-holding a_y, up to 2.5–3.0 m/s² at the ODD curve bound (R_ODD = v²/2.5 m/s²) | WP-C-02 §3.2 |
  83  | Time to 0.6 m at 2.5 m/s² step | ≈ 0.69 s | §3.1 kinematics |
  84  | Driver reaction to an acoustic take-over request (attentive, hands near wheel) | 0.75–1.5 s (to be confirmed in [WP-C-08](../02-concept/WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) §8) | assumption |
  85  
  86  The physical time to departure in a curve (≈ 0.7 s) is shorter than driver reaction, so SG-02 in curves at the ODD bound relies on the warning being given at once and on torque being ramped rather than cut (FSR-02.04). **Warning budget: ≤ 0.5 s detection + ≤ 0.5 s to warning (FSC §8) is not sufficient alone for curves at the ODD bound;** it is sufficient on straight roads and gentle curves. This is a controllability and ODD question (OI-2), recorded for the HARA owner. For SG-06 (stationary lead at 30 m/s, decel need 3.5 m/s²), the margin depends on distance; the 1 s budget is kept.
  87  
  88  ### 3.3 SG-03 unintended acceleration
  89  
  90  Gap closure with ego acceleration error a relative to a lead at constant speed: Δs = ½·a·t².
  91  
  92  | a | 0.5 s | 1.0 s | 1.5 s | 2.0 s |
  93  |---|---|---|---|---|
  94  | 1.0 m/s² | 0.12 m | 0.50 m | 1.12 m | 2.0 m |
  95  | 2.0 m/s² | 0.25 m | 1.0 m | 2.25 m | 4.0 m |
  96  
  97  At typical following gaps (time gap ≥ 1.0 s → ≥ 20 m at 20 m/s) the gap loss within 1 s is ≤ 1 m at the envelope bound (+2.0 m/s²). HE-03.2 (launch toward a pedestrian from standstill) is the governing case: at 2.0 m/s² the vehicle moves 1 m in 1 s. **FTTI(SG-03) = 1 s** is kept; the standstill-launch case should be checked with the actual pedestrian distance assumptions in WP-C-08 (OI-3).
  98  
  99  ### 3.4 SG-04 excessive deceleration
 100  
 101  Following vehicle at time gap h, same speed v, reacting after t_r with equal deceleration: gap reduction ≈ v·t_r (independent of the deceleration level once both brake equally); collision if v·t_r ≥ h·v, i.e. t_r ≥ h. The hazard depends on the follower, not on detection time, so SG-04 is governed by the **deceleration onset limit and magnitude** (TSR-202, TSR-205) rather than by an FTTI for detection. For faults that defeat the bound (e.g. inactive transition step), FTTI(SG-04) = 1 s is kept as preliminary.
 102  
 103  ### 3.5 SG-05 release on driver action
 104  
 105  Requirement-based: the driver expects release within the time of their own action. The HARA sets ≤ 0.2 s from driver input to release. Budget: brake/cruise message period (25–30 ms) + envelope reaction (1 frame) + EPS/PCM response (TBD, AOU-01R/03R). Steering override: driver torque message period (20 ms at 50 Hz) + t_ovr (TSR-304).
 106  
 107  ### 3.6 SG-07 PCS preservation
 108  
 109  SG-07 has two parts with different timing.
 110  
 111  - **Content.** The item must not originate or alter a PCS-relevant frame. This is a per-frame property of the TX gate (TSR-705) and the forwarding block list (TSR-704). It has no detection budget: a wrong frame is either never sent or the design is wrong.
 112  - **Forwarding loss or delay.** While the relay intercepts, the stock camera frames that carry the PCS request reach the car only through the safety MCU. If forwarding stops (MCU hang, camera-side bus fault, a QM configuration command, queue overflow; WP-A-04 §8), the PCS braking request is delayed until the fault is detected and the relay returns the stock path. This part needs an FTTI.
 113  
 114  Hazard: the stock PCS brakes later than it would without the item, so less speed is removed before impact. If braking starts d seconds late and would otherwise have continued until impact, the speed reduction lost is at most a_PCS · d.
 115  
 116  | Item | Value | Basis |
 117  |---|---|---|
 118  | PCS braking deceleration a_PCS | ≈ 6 m/s² (assumed full PCS braking on dry road) | assumption A-PCS-1, to be measured (§6) |
 119  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.2 s | 6 × 0.2 = 1.2 m/s ≈ 4.3 km/h | kinematics |
 120  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.5 s | 3.0 m/s ≈ 10.8 km/h | kinematics |
 121  | PCS message period on the camera-side bus | not known | to be measured from drive logs (§6, OI-4) |
 122  | Relay drop-out time after the coil is released | ≈ 10–20 ms for typical automotive relays | assumption A-PCS-2; datasheet (WP-H-02) |
 123  
 124  **Preliminary FTTI(SG-07, forwarding loss) = 0.2 s.** A delay of 0.2 s removes at most about 4 km/h of the speed reduction the stock PCS would achieve, which is small next to the reductions an AEB function is designed for. A delay of 0.5 s or more (10 km/h or more) is not. This is engineering judgement on assumed values, recorded so the budget can be checked. It is replaced once the PCS braking profile and message timing are measured on the reference vehicle and the acceptable loss is agreed in the HARA review.
 125  
 126  Budget: **FDTI ≤ 0.1 s** to detect that forwarding has stopped (forwarding supervision, TSR-711; MCU hang through the hardware watchdog, TSR-501) and **FRTI ≤ 0.1 s** to release the relay (TSR-706) including relay drop-out.
 127  
 128  Consequences for the TSRs:
 129  
 130  - TSR-501 set the watchdog timeout to ≤ 0.2 s, which fits SG-01 (0.5 s) but not the SG-07 FDTI of 0.1 s. WP-S-02 v0.3 tightens it to ≤ 0.1 s. The alternative, releasing the relay by another path on an MCU hang, is open in WP-H-01 (HWSR-502). *Status 2026-10-12:* the IWDG is implemented with a 90 ms nominal timeout (reload 719, panda `b790fca`, T-09a); whether it stays ≤ 0.1 s depends on the LSI tolerance (watchdog impact record OI-2), so the zero margin of OI-7 is not yet shown.
 131  - TSR-706 releases the relay ≤ 0.1 s after the fault reaction. That fits the FRTI.
 132  - TSR-707 (forwarding latency ≤ 1 ms in normal operation) is far inside the budget.
 133  - No TSR detected that forwarding has stopped. TSR-711 is added in [WP-S-02](WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md) v0.3.
 134  
 135  ### 3.7 Summary of FTTIs and budgets
 136  
 137  | SG / fault class | FTTI | FDTI budget | FRTI budget | Margin |
 138  |---|---|---|---|---|
 139  | SG-01 continuous limits | per frame | 0 | ≤ 10 ms (+ TSR-109) | — |
 140  | SG-01 RX loss/corruption | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.2 s (TSR-401) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 141  | SG-01 SoC heartbeat loss | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-407) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 142  | SG-01 SoC command stall | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.05 s (TSR-408) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.35 s |
 143  | SG-01 MCU execution fault | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-501; set by SG-07; 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (reset + relay release) | ≥ 0.3 s |
 144  | SG-01 relay stuck | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-506) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 145  | SG-02/06 loss without warning | 1 s to warning | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.5 s | — (see §3.2). EPS-fault siren of the reference build ≤ 1.26 s worst case from fault onset exceeds this row (T-28, OI-8). Heartbeat-loss siren ≈ 0.56 s after the last heartbeat, within this row (T-07a, all builds since panda `c33343b`; 2026-10-13) |
 146  | SG-03/04 | 1 s | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 147  | SG-05 brake/cancel | 0.2 s | ≤ 30 ms | ≤ 10 ms + EPS/PCM | TBD |
 148  | SG-05 steering override | 0.2 s | ≤ 40 ms + t_ovr | ≤ 0.1 s | TBD |
 149  | SG-07 content (TX gate, block list) | per frame | 0 | — | — |
 150  | SG-07 forwarding loss | 0.2 s (preliminary, §3.6) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-711; MCU hang: TSR-501 ≤ 0.1 s, 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-706, incl. relay drop-out) | 0 (depends on LSI tolerance, OI-7) |

 190  
 191  | Fault class | Budget | Baseline | Verdict | Required change | TSR |
 192  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 193  | RX loss | ≤ 0.2 s | 1–2 s (T-01) | **Not met** at `8b8c6ae`. *2026-10-12:* Met by design for the Toyota RX set (`0x262` marginal, ≈ 0.21 s), not verified (M-04) | 5 periods, evaluation ≥ 50 Hz (move timeout check out of the 1 Hz path) | TSR-401 |
 194  | RX stuck/inserted | ≤ 0.2 s | not detected (T-03) | **Not met** | rate, frozen-content, cross-checks | TSR-403…405 |
 195  | SoC heartbeat loss | ≤ 0.3 s | 4–5 s (T-06) | **Not met** at `8b8c6ae`. *2026-10-12:* Revoke met by design (T-06a, ≤ 0.31 s; M-05 pending); SILENT ≤ 2 s not met (T-06b, TSR-510) | evaluate at ≥ 10 Hz; revoke at 0.3 s; SILENT ≤ 2 s | TSR-407, 510 |
 196  | SoC heartbeat loss → warning (added 2026-10-13) | warning within SG-02 1 s (§3.7) | siren at T-06: 4–5 s (T-07) | **Not met** at `8b8c6ae`. *2026-10-13:* Met by design in all builds when authority was granted at the revocation (T-07a, ≤ 0.56 s); not verified (M-05).  200  | Relay stuck released | ≤ 0.3 s | 1–2 s window (T-08) | **Not met** | readback | TSR-506 |

 208  | Cruise active with main off (added 2026-10-12) | ≤ 0.2 s | refuse at once; revoke after 150 ms (T-26) | Met by design (reference build), not verified | — | TSR-405(c) |
 209  | SoC TX rate above budget (added 2026-10-12) | revoke | ≤ 0.1 s after first rejected frame, ≤ 0.2 s from onset at 2× (T-27) | Met by design (reference build), not verified; budgets to be confirmed on logs | — | TSR-518 |
 210  | EPS fault (added 2026-10-12) | LKA refused; warning within SG-02 1 s (§3.7) | LKA refused on receipt; siren ≤ 1.26 s worst case after onset (T-28) | Refusal met by design (reference build); **warning latency exceeds the 1 s budget** (OI-8); not verified | see OI-8 | TSR-110, 516 |

 219  
 220  Implementation note: the panda tick handler runs at 8 Hz and the safety checks are "decimated to 1 Hz" (`main.c:105-106, 142-143`). The required budgets need a periodic safety task of at least 50 Hz (TSR-401) on a timer independent of the comms interrupts, with its completion feeding the IWDG service point (TSR-501). The architectural change is recorded in [WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md).
 221  
 222  *Status 2026-10-12:* since panda `9f60e17` a 100 Hz safety task on TIM4 runs `safety_tick()`, the heartbeat monitor, the EPS-fault siren timer and the IWDG feed (`main.c` `safety_task_handler`). All interrupts are at one priority level (`irq_priorities.h`, panda `52cad44`), so the task can be delayed by other handlers; its latency under load is not measured ([WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md) §5.4.5). *2026-10-13:* the safety task also runs the heartbeat-loss siren timer (panda `c33343b`, T-07a). The 8 Hz / 1 Hz tick keeps the SILENT transition (T-06b), the engaged-mismatch check (T-05) and the relay grace counter (T-08).

 228  | [WP-C-11](../02-concept/WP-C-11-ai-system-definition-and-safety-requirements.md) AIR-17 | Model inference ≤ 50 ms per frame; output older than 100 ms not used for actuation (TBC from WP-S-04) | Consistent with TSR-602 (≤ 2 periods = 100 ms at 20 Hz). Inference time to be measured (§6, M-09) |
 229  | AIR-12 / AIR-26 | 2 s of valid outputs after (re)initialisation before actuation | Accepted; affects start-up only |

 232  ## 6. Measurement plan
 233  
 234  No measurement has been performed. Results tables are templates.
 235  
 236  | ID | What | Method | Setup | Pass criterion |
 237  |---|---|---|---|---|
 238  | M-01 | EPS torque authority and a_y vs raw request and speed | Steady-state circular / step-steer tests on a closed course; log `0x2E4`, `0x260`, IMU | Reference vehicle, [WP-V-07](../06-validation/WP-V-07-vehicle-test-operations.md) | Data set for TSR-102 table and §3.1 a_y,EPS |
 239  | M-02 | EPS behaviour on `0x2E4` drop-out and on zero-torque frame | Stop/zero frames at controlled torque; measure wheel torque decay | Vehicle on lift (wheels free) then closed course | t_EPS; decides TSR-109 option (a)/(b) |
 240  | M-03 | PCM response to inactive value and cancel | Inject step from −3.5 m/s² to inactive; cancel bit | Closed course | Jerk within TSR-205 bound (AOU-05R) |
 241  | M-04 | Envelope RX timeout detection | Remove each RX message on the HIL bench; measure time to `controls_allowed` false and to first rejected TX | HIL (D-04) | ≤ 0.2 s after change |
 242  | M-05 | Heartbeat loss and command stall | Stop `0xf3`; stop `sendcan` while heartbeat continues | HIL | ≤ 0.3 s / ≤ 0.05 s to SS-L; siren ≤ 0.56 s after the last heartbeat when engaged (T-07a; by ear, `panda/tests/hitl/test_15_heartbeat_siren.py` times the revocation) |
 243  | M-06 | MCU hang | Inject infinite loop in tick / main / ISR via debug build | HIL | reset ≤ 0.2 s; relay released |
 244  | M-07 | Brake/cancel → torque zero at EPS | Vehicle, brake press while engaged at constant torque | Closed course | ≤ 0.2 s to EPS torque zero |
 245  | M-08 | Driver override | Steering force gauge, opposing torque | Closed course | per TSR-304 |
 246  | M-09 | SoC loop timing | `procLog`, per-process loop timing, model execution time, `sendcan` age histogram over ≥ 10 h of driving | Logs | p99.9 within TSR-602/614 budgets |
 247  | M-10 | Take-over request latency | Fault injection on SoC, camera on display + audio capture | HIL/vehicle | ≤ 1 s |
 248  | M-11 | Forwarding latency camera → car | Timestamped capture on both buses | HIL with real camera | ≤ TSR-707 bound |

Why it matters: The stated sum of detection and reaction equals FTTI before measured PCS timing. A watchdog nominal setting is insufficient to prove the end-to-end budget.

AI-author preparation: Document all terms and tolerances in the forwarding-loss budget and the measurement needed to establish margin.

Closure evidence: Worst-case FDTI+FRTI substantiated with positive justified margin or an explicitly accepted alternative safety argument.

Original document at the assessed commit (read-only)
   1  # WP-S-04 Timing Analysis: FTTI, Fault Detection and Reaction Budget
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-S-04 Timing analysis: FTTI, fault detection and reaction budget |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-4:2018 §6 (fault tolerant time interval, fault detection and fault reaction time intervals, emergency operation); ISO 26262-3:2018 §7 (FTTI as FSC input); ISO 26262-1:2018 (definitions, by reference); ASPICE 4.0 SYS.3 |
   7  | Version | 0.5 (heartbeat-loss siren, panda `c33343b`; EPS-siren worst case 1.26 s; 2026-10-13). 0.4 (§5 Figures 5-1 and 5-2 added: timing budgets per safety goal against the code-derived times, and the EPS-fault warning chain of OI-8; generated from §3.7 and §4 by `figures/fig_s04_timing.py`; no number changed; 2026-10-13). 0.3 (status update 2026-10-12: §4 code-derived times for the reference build at `bf6ce8c` (T-01, T-06a/b, T-09a, T-26…T-29), §5 verdicts, OI-8 EPS-fault warning latency; nothing verified on hardware). History: 0.2 (§3.6 preliminary SG-07 FTTI and budget, RR-2026-005 F-02; 2026-10-12) |
   8  | Status | Draft — kinematic estimates and code-derived times only; no measurement has been performed; not reviewed independently |
   9  | ASIL / scope | SG-01 (C until re-rated, B‡), SG-02…SG-07 (B) |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1) |
  12  | Approver | Safety manager |
  13  | Baseline | `bf6ce8c` (status update 2026-10-12; opendbc `7993399`, panda `b790fca`). Original analysis at `8b8c6ae` (opendbc `229dc70`, panda `92eb565`) |
  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose
  16  
  17  The HARA gives preliminary FTTIs ([WP-C-03 §6](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md)) and the FSC splits them into detection and reaction budgets ([WP-C-04 §8](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md)). This document:
  18  
  19  1. defines the method used to derive each FTTI from vehicle dynamics (§2, §3);
  20  2. gives first kinematic estimates, parameterised where vehicle data is missing (§3);
  21  3. lists the detection and reaction times of the baseline code, derived from the source (§4);
  22  4. compares them with the budgets and lists the required changes (§5);
  23  5. specifies how the times will be measured (§6).
  24  
  25  It closes GAP-06 at specification level. HARA OI-3 and FSC OI-4 depend on it.
  26  
  27  ## 2. Definitions and method
  28  
  29  | Term | Use in this document |
  30  |---|---|
  31  | FTTI | Time from the occurrence of a fault in the item to the earliest possible occurrence of the hazardous event, if no safety mechanism acts |
  32  | FDTI | Fault detection time interval: fault occurrence → detection |
  33  | FRTI | Fault reaction time interval: detection → safe state reached at the actuator (EPS torque 0, PCM inactive) |
  34  | Budget rule | FDTI + FRTI ≤ FTTI, with a margin of at least 20 % of the FTTI for scheduling jitter, CAN latency and EPS/PCM response |
  35  | Driver reaction t_r | Not part of the FTTI. Used separately for controllability (C class) and for warning budgets (SG-02, SG-06) |
  36  
  37  Method per safety goal:
  38  
  39  1. Take the worst credible malfunctioning behaviour that the fault class can produce **without** the mechanism under analysis (e.g. EPS at its own LKA authority limit T_EPS when the envelope is defeated; envelope-limited torque when only the detection is missing).
  40  2. Integrate the vehicle response (lateral deviation, gap closure) from fault onset.
  41  3. FTTI = time until the response reaches the hazard threshold (lateral margin to the adjacent lane, minimum safe gap).
  42  4. Split into FDTI and FRTI per mechanism; compare with code-derived and measured times.
  43  
  44  Faults whose effect stays inside the envelope limits (e.g. a wrong but limited command from the QM SoC) are handled by the limits acting every frame and by driver controllability; they do not need detection within the FTTI. The FTTI matters for faults that **defeat** a limit or gate: lost or wrong RX data, SoC command path stalls with the EPS holding torque, MCU faults, relay faults.
  45  
  46  ## 3. FTTI derivation per safety goal
  47  
  48  ### 3.1 SG-01 lateral (lateral deviation vs time)
  49  
  50  Model: lateral acceleration error a_y builds either (i) as a step, (ii) with jerk j = 5 m/s³ (controller jerk limit, `openpilot/selfdrive/controls/lib/drive_helpers.py:9-14`), or (iii) as a linear ramp over 1.0 s, which is the envelope torque rate limit (15 raw/frame → 1500 raw in 100 frames, `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/modes/toyota.h:174`; host comment "1.0s time to peak torque", `opendbc_repo/opendbc/car/toyota/values.py:46`). Lateral deviation y(t) is integrated from rest relative to the lane. Speed does not appear because a_y is the input; the speed dependence enters through how much a_y a given torque produces (TSR-102).
  51  
  52  Time (s) to reach lateral deviation d:
  53  
  54  | d | a_y = 1 m/s² | 2 m/s² | 3 m/s² | 4 m/s² | 5 m/s² | Profile |
  55  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  56  | 0.3 m | 0.77 | 0.55 | 0.45 | 0.39 | 0.35 | step |
  57  | 0.6 m | 1.10 | 0.77 | 0.63 | 0.55 | 0.49 | step |
  58  | 0.9 m | 1.34 | 0.95 | 0.77 | 0.67 | 0.60 | step |
  59  | 0.6 m | 1.19 | 0.97 | 0.91 | 0.90 | 0.90 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  60  | 0.9 m | 1.44 | 1.14 | 1.05 | 1.03 | 1.03 | jerk 5 m/s³ |
  61  | 0.6 m | 1.56 | 1.22 | 1.06 | 0.97 | 0.90 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  62  | 0.9 m | 1.81 | 1.40 | 1.22 | 1.11 | 1.03 | 1 s ramp (envelope rate) |
  63  
  64  Hazard threshold: the lateral margin from lane centre to the adjacent lane boundary, (lane width − vehicle width)/2. With the Corolla width ≈ 1.78 m (to be confirmed from the reference vehicle record) and the ODD lane widths (2.9–3.9 m, [WP-C-02 §3.1](../02-concept/WP-C-02-odd-and-intended-functionality.md)): 0.56–1.06 m. The design value is **0.6 m** (narrow arterial lane), which is conservative for highways. For comparison, `docs/SAFETY.md:32` cites 0.9 s to 1 m at maximum actuation.
  65  
  66  Estimates:
  67  
  68  | Fault class | Worst behaviour | a_y assumption | FTTI estimate | Note |
  69  |---|---|---|---|---|
  70  | Envelope defeated (MCU fault, wrong mode, relay fault letting camera/SoC frames through unchecked) | EPS at its own authority T_EPS, step onset | a_y,EPS unknown (AOU-01R); 3–5 m/s² assumed | **0.5–0.6 s** | Supports the preliminary ≤ 0.5 s of the HARA |
  71  | Gate defeated (stale `0x1D2`/`0x226`, authority not revoked), limits intact | envelope-limited torque, 1 s ramp | a_y at τ_max: today unknown; after TSR-102 ≤ target (e.g. 2 m/s²) | 1.2 s at 2 m/s²; 0.9–1.1 s at 3–5 m/s² | Applies to SG-05 too (driver override not honoured) |
  72  | SoC command path stalls with EPS holding last torque | constant a_y at last torque (≤ τ_max) | as above, step from steady state | 0.6–0.8 s at 2–3 m/s² | Only if the EPS holds torque during drop-out (NF-02) |
  73  
  74  Conclusion: **FTTI(SG-01) = 0.5 s** is kept as the design value for faults that defeat the envelope; the budget of FSC §8 (detect ≤ 0.3 s, react ≤ 0.1 s, margin 0.1 s) holds. The value must be confirmed once a_y,EPS and τ_max(v) are measured (§6).
  75  
  76  ### 3.2 SG-02 / SG-06 loss without warning
  77  
  78  Hazard: departure (SG-02) or failure to decelerate (SG-06) before the driver takes over. The relevant time is fault → take-over request, plus driver reaction.
  79  
  80  | Item | Value | Basis |
  81  |---|---|---|
  82  | Lateral drift when assistance is lost in a curve | Loss of the curvature-holding a_y, up to 2.5–3.0 m/s² at the ODD curve bound (R_ODD = v²/2.5 m/s²) | WP-C-02 §3.2 |
  83  | Time to 0.6 m at 2.5 m/s² step | ≈ 0.69 s | §3.1 kinematics |
  84  | Driver reaction to an acoustic take-over request (attentive, hands near wheel) | 0.75–1.5 s (to be confirmed in [WP-C-08](../02-concept/WP-C-08-driver-hmi-misuse-analysis.md) §8) | assumption |
  85  
  86  The physical time to departure in a curve (≈ 0.7 s) is shorter than driver reaction, so SG-02 in curves at the ODD bound relies on the warning being given at once and on torque being ramped rather than cut (FSR-02.04). **Warning budget: ≤ 0.5 s detection + ≤ 0.5 s to warning (FSC §8) is not sufficient alone for curves at the ODD bound;** it is sufficient on straight roads and gentle curves. This is a controllability and ODD question (OI-2), recorded for the HARA owner. For SG-06 (stationary lead at 30 m/s, decel need 3.5 m/s²), the margin depends on distance; the 1 s budget is kept.
  87  
  88  ### 3.3 SG-03 unintended acceleration
  89  
  90  Gap closure with ego acceleration error a relative to a lead at constant speed: Δs = ½·a·t².
  91  
  92  | a | 0.5 s | 1.0 s | 1.5 s | 2.0 s |
  93  |---|---|---|---|---|
  94  | 1.0 m/s² | 0.12 m | 0.50 m | 1.12 m | 2.0 m |
  95  | 2.0 m/s² | 0.25 m | 1.0 m | 2.25 m | 4.0 m |
  96  
  97  At typical following gaps (time gap ≥ 1.0 s → ≥ 20 m at 20 m/s) the gap loss within 1 s is ≤ 1 m at the envelope bound (+2.0 m/s²). HE-03.2 (launch toward a pedestrian from standstill) is the governing case: at 2.0 m/s² the vehicle moves 1 m in 1 s. **FTTI(SG-03) = 1 s** is kept; the standstill-launch case should be checked with the actual pedestrian distance assumptions in WP-C-08 (OI-3).
  98  
  99  ### 3.4 SG-04 excessive deceleration
 100  
 101  Following vehicle at time gap h, same speed v, reacting after t_r with equal deceleration: gap reduction ≈ v·t_r (independent of the deceleration level once both brake equally); collision if v·t_r ≥ h·v, i.e. t_r ≥ h. The hazard depends on the follower, not on detection time, so SG-04 is governed by the **deceleration onset limit and magnitude** (TSR-202, TSR-205) rather than by an FTTI for detection. For faults that defeat the bound (e.g. inactive transition step), FTTI(SG-04) = 1 s is kept as preliminary.
 102  
 103  ### 3.5 SG-05 release on driver action
 104  
 105  Requirement-based: the driver expects release within the time of their own action. The HARA sets ≤ 0.2 s from driver input to release. Budget: brake/cruise message period (25–30 ms) + envelope reaction (1 frame) + EPS/PCM response (TBD, AOU-01R/03R). Steering override: driver torque message period (20 ms at 50 Hz) + t_ovr (TSR-304).
 106  
 107  ### 3.6 SG-07 PCS preservation
 108  
 109  SG-07 has two parts with different timing.
 110  
 111  - **Content.** The item must not originate or alter a PCS-relevant frame. This is a per-frame property of the TX gate (TSR-705) and the forwarding block list (TSR-704). It has no detection budget: a wrong frame is either never sent or the design is wrong.
 112  - **Forwarding loss or delay.** While the relay intercepts, the stock camera frames that carry the PCS request reach the car only through the safety MCU. If forwarding stops (MCU hang, camera-side bus fault, a QM configuration command, queue overflow; WP-A-04 §8), the PCS braking request is delayed until the fault is detected and the relay returns the stock path. This part needs an FTTI.
 113  
 114  Hazard: the stock PCS brakes later than it would without the item, so less speed is removed before impact. If braking starts d seconds late and would otherwise have continued until impact, the speed reduction lost is at most a_PCS · d.
 115  
 116  | Item | Value | Basis |
 117  |---|---|---|
 118  | PCS braking deceleration a_PCS | ≈ 6 m/s² (assumed full PCS braking on dry road) | assumption A-PCS-1, to be measured (§6) |
 119  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.2 s | 6 × 0.2 = 1.2 m/s ≈ 4.3 km/h | kinematics |
 120  | Speed reduction lost for d = 0.5 s | 3.0 m/s ≈ 10.8 km/h | kinematics |
 121  | PCS message period on the camera-side bus | not known | to be measured from drive logs (§6, OI-4) |
 122  | Relay drop-out time after the coil is released | ≈ 10–20 ms for typical automotive relays | assumption A-PCS-2; datasheet (WP-H-02) |
 123  
 124  **Preliminary FTTI(SG-07, forwarding loss) = 0.2 s.** A delay of 0.2 s removes at most about 4 km/h of the speed reduction the stock PCS would achieve, which is small next to the reductions an AEB function is designed for. A delay of 0.5 s or more (10 km/h or more) is not. This is engineering judgement on assumed values, recorded so the budget can be checked. It is replaced once the PCS braking profile and message timing are measured on the reference vehicle and the acceptable loss is agreed in the HARA review.
 125  
 126  Budget: **FDTI ≤ 0.1 s** to detect that forwarding has stopped (forwarding supervision, TSR-711; MCU hang through the hardware watchdog, TSR-501) and **FRTI ≤ 0.1 s** to release the relay (TSR-706) including relay drop-out.
 127  
 128  Consequences for the TSRs:
 129  
 130  - TSR-501 set the watchdog timeout to ≤ 0.2 s, which fits SG-01 (0.5 s) but not the SG-07 FDTI of 0.1 s. WP-S-02 v0.3 tightens it to ≤ 0.1 s. The alternative, releasing the relay by another path on an MCU hang, is open in WP-H-01 (HWSR-502). *Status 2026-10-12:* the IWDG is implemented with a 90 ms nominal timeout (reload 719, panda `b790fca`, T-09a); whether it stays ≤ 0.1 s depends on the LSI tolerance (watchdog impact record OI-2), so the zero margin of OI-7 is not yet shown.
 131  - TSR-706 releases the relay ≤ 0.1 s after the fault reaction. That fits the FRTI.
 132  - TSR-707 (forwarding latency ≤ 1 ms in normal operation) is far inside the budget.
 133  - No TSR detected that forwarding has stopped. TSR-711 is added in [WP-S-02](WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md) v0.3.
 134  
 135  ### 3.7 Summary of FTTIs and budgets
 136  
 137  | SG / fault class | FTTI | FDTI budget | FRTI budget | Margin |
 138  |---|---|---|---|---|
 139  | SG-01 continuous limits | per frame | 0 | ≤ 10 ms (+ TSR-109) | — |
 140  | SG-01 RX loss/corruption | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.2 s (TSR-401) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 141  | SG-01 SoC heartbeat loss | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-407) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 142  | SG-01 SoC command stall | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.05 s (TSR-408) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.35 s |
 143  | SG-01 MCU execution fault | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-501; set by SG-07; 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (reset + relay release) | ≥ 0.3 s |
 144  | SG-01 relay stuck | 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-506) | ≤ 0.1 s | ≥ 0.1 s |
 145  | SG-02/06 loss without warning | 1 s to warning | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.5 s | — (see §3.2). EPS-fault siren of the reference build ≤ 1.26 s worst case from fault onset exceeds this row (T-28, OI-8). Heartbeat-loss siren ≈ 0.56 s after the last heartbeat, within this row (T-07a, all builds since panda `c33343b`; 2026-10-13) |
 146  | SG-03/04 | 1 s | ≤ 0.5 s | ≤ 0.3 s | ≥ 0.2 s |
 147  | SG-05 brake/cancel | 0.2 s | ≤ 30 ms | ≤ 10 ms + EPS/PCM | TBD |
 148  | SG-05 steering override | 0.2 s | ≤ 40 ms + t_ovr | ≤ 0.1 s | TBD |
 149  | SG-07 content (TX gate, block list) | per frame | 0 | — | — |
 150  | SG-07 forwarding loss | 0.2 s (preliminary, §3.6) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-711; MCU hang: TSR-501 ≤ 0.1 s, 90 ms nominal implemented, T-09a) | ≤ 0.1 s (TSR-706, incl. relay drop-out) | 0 (depends on LSI tolerance, OI-7) |
 151  
 152  ## 4. Current detection and reaction times (code-derived)
 153  
 154  | # | Mechanism | Code | Derivation | Worst-case time |
 155  |---|---|---|---|---|
 156  | T-01 | RX timeout | `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/safety.h:321-344`; called at 1 Hz from `panda/board/main.c:241-242` | threshold max(10 × period, 1 s); for the Corolla messages 10 × period is 120 ms (`0xAA` 83 Hz), 200 ms (`0x260` 50 Hz), 303 ms (`0x1D2` 33 Hz), 250 ms (`0x226` 40 Hz), so 1 s applies; evaluated once per second | **1–2 s** (at `8b8c6ae`). *Status 2026-10-12:* 5 nominal periods (`rx_timeout_periods`, opendbc `de80b63`), evaluated by the 100 Hz safety task (panda `171fcdf`): **≤ 0.16 s** for `0x1D2`; the reference build adds `0x262` (25 Hz, ≈ 0.21 s, slightly above TSR-401's 0.2 s if the real rate is 25 Hz) and `0x1D3` (33 Hz, ≈ 0.16 s) (opendbc `21d8dd8`). Not verified on hardware |
 157  | T-02 | RX checksum / quality | `safety.h:112-121, 164-197` | on receipt | 1 message period (12–30 ms) |
 158  | T-03 | RX counter | — | no Toyota counters (`modes/toyota.h:39-46`) | not detected |
 159  | T-04 | Brake / cruise revoke | `safety.h:350-356, 518-527` | on receipt | 25 ms (brake 40 Hz), 30 ms (cruise 33 Hz) |
 160  | T-05 | Heartbeat engaged mismatch | `panda/board/main.c:181-189` | 3 consecutive 1 Hz ticks | **2–3 s** |

 194  | RX stuck/inserted | ≤ 0.2 s | not detected (T-03) | **Not met** | rate, frozen-content, cross-checks | TSR-403…405 |

 219  
 220  Implementation note: the panda tick handler runs at 8 Hz and the safety checks are "decimated to 1 Hz" (`main.c:105-106, 142-143`). The required budgets need a periodic safety task of at least 50 Hz (TSR-401) on a timer independent of the comms interrupts, with its completion feeding the IWDG service point (TSR-501). The architectural change is recorded in [WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md).
 221  
 222  *Status 2026-10-12:* since panda `9f60e17` a 100 Hz safety task on TIM4 runs `safety_tick()`, the heartbeat monitor, the EPS-fault siren timer and the IWDG feed (`main.c` `safety_task_handler`). All interrupts are at one priority level (`irq_priorities.h`, panda `52cad44`), so the task can be delayed by other handlers; its latency under load is not measured ([WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md) §5.4.5). *2026-10-13:* the safety task also runs the heartbeat-loss siren timer (panda `c33343b`, T-07a). The 8 Hz / 1 Hz tick keeps the SILENT transition (T-06b), the engaged-mismatch check (T-05) and the relay grace counter (T-08).

   1  # WP-A-02 Coexistence of Elements and Freedom from Interference
   2  
   3  | Field | Value |
   4  |---|---|
   5  | Work product | WP-A-02 Coexistence of elements and freedom from interference |
   6  | Standard reference | ISO 26262-9:2018 §6 (criteria for coexistence of elements); ISO 26262-6:2018 §7 and Annex D (freedom from interference between software elements: timing and execution, memory, exchange of information); ISO 26262-5:2018 Annex D (communication bus diagnostic measures, informative); ASPICE 4.0 SYS.3, SWE.2 |
   7  | Version | 0.6 (heartbeat-loss siren, panda `c33343b`; EPS-siren worst case 1.26 s; 2026-10-13). 0.5 (§3 Figure 3-1 interference map with the §8 verdicts added; no change to the analysis; 2026-10-13). 0.4 (status update 2026-10-12: dated status notes for the reference build at `bf6ce8c` in §4–§6, §8 verdicts, OI-5 partly closed; nothing verified on hardware or reviewed independently). History: 0.3 (TSR-626, TSR-627 for FFI-CM-17/19; 2026-10-12) |
   8  | Status | Draft |
   9  | ASIL / scope | ASIL C (SG-01, until re-rated) / ASIL B (SG-02…SG-07) elements coexisting with QM elements, on the panda MCU and on the comma device |
  10  | Author | Assurance team (initial draft) |
  11  | Reviewer(s) | TBD (I1); input to G2 confirmation review |
  12  | Approver | Safety manager |
