  14  
  15  ## 1. Purpose and scope
  16  
  17  The LionDriver safety argument ([WP-M-01 §4](../01-management/WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#4-safety-architecture-argument-the-central-strategy)) treats everything on the application SoC as QM and puts all ASIL requirements on the safety envelope (E-03). That argument holds only if no fault in a QM element can cause the envelope to violate a safety requirement. This document analyses that condition, as ISO 26262-9 §6 requires when elements of different ASIL (or QM) coexist inside one element or interact with it.
  18  
  19  Two coexistence levels are analysed:
  20  
  21  | Level | Container | ASIL-allocated part | QM (or lower-ASIL) parts |
  22  |---|---|---|---|
  23  | L-MCU | Panda STM32H7 firmware image (one binary, one CPU, one address space) | opendbc safety hooks (Toyota mode), safety tick, heartbeat supervision, relay drive, CAN TX/RX path, fault handling | Host command dispatcher (USB/SPI), CAN forwarding plumbing, fan, sound, LEDs, IR, ADC housekeeping, debug UART, power save, bootstub/softloader paths |
  24  | L-DEV | comma device (SoC + MCU + shared PCB, power, enclosure) and harness | E-03 envelope, E-05 harness relay | E-01 SoC software (pandad, card, controlsd, selfdrived, ...), E-02 ML models, AGNOS Linux, network services |
  25  
  26  Dependent failures that do not originate in a QM element (shared power, clock, thermal, toolchain, identical constants) are analysed in [WP-A-03](WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md). This document cross-references them where interference and dependent failure overlap.
  27  
  28  TSR references are to [WP-S-02](../03-system/WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md) (Draft v0.1). New findings use the NF-nn IDs of WP-S-02 §11 where WP-S-02 already raised them.
  29  
  30  ## 2. Coexistence decision for the panda firmware (L-MCU)
  31  
  32  ISO 26262-9 §6 gives two options for a QM or lower-ASIL sub-element that coexists with a higher-ASIL sub-element: develop it to the higher ASIL, or show freedom from interference. The table records the decision per sub-element.
  33  
  34  | Sub-element (panda firmware) | Source | Safety role | Decision | Rationale |
  35  |---|---|---|---|---|
  36  | opendbc safety core and Toyota mode (`safety_rx_hook`, `safety_tx_hook`, `safety_fwd_hook`, `safety_tick`, limit checks) | `opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/safety.h`, `lateral.h`, `longitudinal.h`, `modes/toyota.h` | Implements FSR-01.xx…07.xx | **ASIL C (B after re-rating)** | Core envelope |
  37  | Tick handler: heartbeat timeout, controls-allowed mismatch, siren, `safety_tick` call | `panda/board/main.c:106-249` | FSR-01.07, FSR-02.05 | **ASIL C** | Safety supervision |
  38  | CAN driver RX/TX path (FDCAN ISR, `can_send`, queues) | `panda/board/drivers/fdcan.h`, `drivers/can_common.h:161-176` | Carries every actuation frame; invokes TX/RX hooks | **ASIL C** | Safety-related data path; a fault here bypasses the hooks |
  39  | Relay and harness drivers | `panda/board/drivers/harness.h` | FSR-01.11, FSR-07.03 | **ASIL C/B** | Actuation of the safe state |
  40  | `set_safety_mode` and mode bookkeeping | `panda/board/main.c:31-80` | Configures envelope | **ASIL C** | Configuration of the safety element |
  41  | Fault handling, exception handlers, early init | `panda/board/sys/faults.h`, `early_init.h:66-80` | FSR-01.09 | **ASIL C** | |
  42  | Host command dispatcher (`comms_control_handler`) | `panda/board/main_comms.h` | Receives commands from the QM SoC; some commands change safety-relevant state | **ASIL C for the commands that touch safety state; others shown non-interfering** | It is the interface through which QM interference arrives (§5) |
  43  | SPI/USB transport (`spi_rx_done`, DMA, USB ISR) | `panda/board/drivers/spi.h`, `stm32h7/llspi.h`, `stm32h7/llusb.h` | Moves host frames into `comms_can_write` | **ASIL C (integrity checks) / FFI for the rest** | Corruption here reaches `can_send` |
  44  | Fan, sound, LED, IR, DTS/ADC housekeeping, debug print | `drivers/fan.h`, `stm32h7/sound.h`, `drivers/led.h`, `stm32h7/lldts.h` | None | **QM with FFI** | Must not block or corrupt the above |
  45  | Bootstub / softloader | `panda/board/bootstub.c`, `flasher.h` | Boot integrity (TSR-511) | **ASIL C for signature check and relay-safe start; cybersecurity scope** | GAP-24, GAP-38 |
  46  
  47  **Consequence:** because the panda firmware is one image without MPU partitioning (GAP-11), the "QM with FFI" decision for housekeeping code can only be supported by analysis plus the measures in §6. If those measures are not implemented, all code in the image has to be developed and verified to the highest ASIL (practically: ASIL B after re-rating). This is OI-2.
  48  
  49  ## 3. Interference model
  50  
  51  | Type | ISO 26262-6 Annex D fault classes considered | Applies to |
  52  |---|---|---|
  53  | Spatial (memory) | Corruption of content, read/write access to memory allocated to another element, stack overflow, DMA writes to wrong location | L-MCU |
  54  | Temporal (timing and execution) | Blocking of execution, deadlock, livelock, wrong allocation of execution time, incorrect synchronisation, interrupt overload | L-MCU, L-DEV |
  55  | Communication (exchange of information) | Repetition, loss, delay, insertion, masquerade/incorrect addressing, incorrect sequence, corruption, asymmetric information, information to only a subset, blocking of a communication channel; plus unintended **commands** that change the receiver's configuration | L-MCU ↔ SoC (IF-04), vehicle CAN (IF-01/02/03), boot/reset lines |
  56  
  57  ![Figure 3-1: coexistence and interference map — QM SoC, panda firmware partitions (SoC-facing code, ASIL C core, QM housekeeping), interference channels FFI-SP-01…05, FFI-TM-01…07 and FFI-CM-01…19, and the five FFI verdicts of §8](figures/fig-a02-3-1-interference-map.svg)
  58  
  59  *Figure 3-1.* The coexistence decisions of §2 drawn as partitions of the one firmware image, the interference channels of §4–§6 by type (spatial, temporal, communication), and the verdicts of §8 with the key measures of §7. Amber means implemented in part and not verified; no verdict is "shown". Drawn from §2, §4–§8. Source: [`figures/fig-a02-3-1-interference-map.svg`](figures/fig-a02-3-1-interference-map.svg); change it together with §2, §7 and §8.
  60  
  61  ## 4. Spatial interference (L-MCU)
  62  
  63  | ID | Interference | Path | Effect on safety | Existing measures (code) | Gap | Required measure |
  64  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  65  | FFI-SP-01 | QM code (comms handler, housekeeping) overwrites envelope state (`controls_allowed`, `current_safety_mode`, limits, `relay_malfunction`, heartbeat variables) through a pointer or index error | Single address space; `.data/.bss/stack` all in DTCM (`panda/board/stm32h7/stm32h7x5_flash.ld:160-188`) | SG-01/03/04/05: authority granted or limits altered | MISRA C:2012 checking of `board/main.c` (`panda/tests/misra/test_misra.sh`); `-Werror`; bounds-checked index for CAN bus number (`can_common.h:163`) | **No MPU configuration** (`mpu_armv7.h` vendored, unused); no RAM ECC handling (GAP-08, GAP-11) | MPU regions: envelope data read-only to non-safety code paths, stack guard, code non-writable, MemManage → safe state (TSR-507); RAM ECC enabled with double-error → safe state (TSR-503) |
  66  | FFI-SP-02 | **SPI DMA writes beyond `spi_buf_rx`.** The data length is taken from the 16-bit header field `spi_data_len_mosi` (`drivers/spi.h:115`) and used directly as DMA length (`drivers/spi.h:233`, `stm32h7/llspi.h:22`). No check against `SPI_BUF_SIZE` (4096, `drivers/drivers.h:216`) was found. The header is protected only by an 8-bit XOR (`spi.h:97-104, 122`) Note: the linker map suggests the overrun is confined to the SRAM1/2 region holding only the SPI buffers, with envelope state in DTCM (not DMA-reachable); confirm per WP-W-04 OI-1 before crediting or discounting this path. | SoC (QM) → SPI4 → DMA2 Stream2 | Overrun of the SPI buffers. By linker layout the buffers are in SRAM1/2 (`.sram12`, `stm32h7x5_flash.ld:72, 202-206`) and the envelope state is in DTCM, which the general-purpose DMA cannot reach on the STM32H7 bus matrix (to be confirmed against RM0468). The expected effect is therefore corruption of `spi_buf_tx` and unused SRAM1/2, not of envelope state | Buffers placed in a separate SRAM bank from `.data/.bss`; header XOR | Missing length bound: **NF-04 of WP-S-02** (no GAP ID yet; OI-3); reliance on bus-matrix property not documented | Reject any header with `spi_data_len_mosi > SPI_BUF_SIZE − SPI_HEADER_SIZE − 1` or `spi_data_len_miso > SPI_BUF_SIZE − 4` (TSR-412); document DTCM-not-DMA-reachable as a safety-relevant HW property in [WP-S-05 HSI](../03-system/WP-S-05-hsi-specification.md); fault-injection test VS-FI-07 ([WP-V-05](../06-validation/WP-V-05-fault-injection.md)) |
  67  | FFI-SP-03 | Reassembly buffer overflow in `comms_can_write`: `can_write_buffer.data[72]`, packet length from `dlc_to_len[data[pos] >> 4]` (`can_comms.h:113-124`) | SoC → SPI → `comms_can_write` | Corrupted CAN packet passed to `can_send` (then subject to `safety_tx_hook`) | Max packet length bounded by DLC table (≤ 64 + header ≤ 72); `safety_tx_hook` checks every frame (`can_common.h:162`) | Bound argument not documented | Unit test and review of the bound; SWSR under TSR-412 |
  68  | FFI-SP-04 | Stack overflow from nested ISRs | All IRQs on one stack (`_Min_Stack_Size = 0x400` in linker check only) | Corruption of DTCM data | All interrupts appear to run at the same (default) NVIC priority, since no `NVIC_SetPriority` call was found in `panda/board`, so ISR nesting is limited. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* explicit priority table `board/drivers/irq_priorities.h` (panda `65b75d3`, pin `52cad44`): every enabled interrupt set before enabling, all classes at level 8, so no ISR preempts another | No stack usage analysis; no guard | Static stack analysis (WCET/stack in [WP-W-03](../05-software/WP-W-03-software-architecture.md)); MPU stack guard (TSR-507) |
  69  | FFI-SP-05 | Flash write by QM path (softloader / flasher) while a car mode is active | `0xd1` param 1 enters softloader in release (`main_comms.h:165-186`); then flasher erases/programs application sectors | Envelope code replaced | Sector 0 (bootstub) protected from flasher erase (`stm32h7/llflash.h:12-14`); bootstub signature check at boot (`bootstub.c:47-72`) | Softloader entry allowed at any time, also in a car mode (GAP-24); RSA-1024/SHA-1 | Refuse `0xd1` while a car safety mode is active or controls are allowed (TSR-513, to be extended: `0xd1` param 1 is not in its list, OI-7); release-only firmware signature with modern algorithm (TSR-511) |
  70  
  71  ### 4.1 Spatial interference on the device (L-DEV)
  72  
  73  The SoC and MCU have physically separate memories. There is no shared RAM. Spatial interference between them is therefore only possible through communication (§6) or by **replacing the MCU memory content** through flashing (FFI-SP-05, FFI-CM-12). Spatial interference inside the SoC (between QM processes, e.g. UI corrupting controlsd) is QM-to-QM and does not need an FFI argument under ISO 26262. It is a SOTIF and cybersecurity concern (GAP-20, GAP-23) and is covered there.
  74  
  75  ## 5. Temporal interference
  76  
  77  ### 5.1 On the panda MCU (L-MCU)
  78  
  79  | ID | Interference | Effect on safety | Existing measures (code) | Gap | Required measure |
  80  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  81  | FFI-TM-01 | **Interrupt load.** CAN RX ISR (runs `safety_fwd_hook` and `safety_rx_hook`, `drivers/fdcan.h:199-221`), SPI/DMA ISRs (run the whole command dispatcher and `comms_can_write` → `can_send` → `safety_tx_hook` in interrupt context, `drivers/spi.h:108-221`), USB ISR, sound DMA, fan tach and the 8 Hz tick all share one core. A babbling SoC or bus can starve the tick | Tick delayed → heartbeat timeout and `safety_tick` RX-timeout detection late (FSR-01.06/01.07) | Per-IRQ call-rate limits with faults (`drivers/interrupts.h:33-35`; e.g. SPI `llspi.h:86-88`, CAN `fdcan.h:257-262`, USB 1.5 M/s `llusb.h:14`); interrupt load measured (`interrupts.h:60-62`) | Rate faults are **report-only** (`PERMANENT_FAULTS = 0U`, `sys/sys.h:50`) (GAP-08); no priority scheme; no WCET. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* priorities explicit and equal (`irq_priorities.h`, panda `52cad44`); the safety task runs on TIM4 at 100 Hz with an interrupt-rate limit of 110 Hz (`FAULT_INTERRUPT_RATE_TICK`, report-only); the distinct levels of WP-W-03 §5.4.2 are not adopted (critical-section and handler lengths not measured, §5.4.5); rate faults still report-only; no WCET | Interrupt-rate and load faults → safe state (TSR-502); NVIC priority scheme with tick/safety above comms; WCET of ISRs ([WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md)) |
  82  | FFI-TM-02 | **Hang in any ISR or the main loop** (e.g. busy-wait) | All supervision stops; relay stays in its last (intercepting) state; behaviour of frames already queued in the FDCAN TX FIFO to be analysed | Software watchdog `simple_watchdog_kick` (`main.c:119`) checked in the **same** tick ISR (`main.c:301`, `drivers/simple_watchdog.h:7-17`) | **IWDG never initialised** (`stm32h7/stm32h7_config.h:43`) (GAP-07). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* IWDG1 implemented (`drivers/hw_watchdog.h`): window mode (≈ 5 ms minimum feed interval), ≈ 90 ms nominal, fed from the 100 Hz safety task only after main-loop progress, started at the first relay drive; a watchdog reset is reported as `pandaState.watchdogReset`. Remaining: hangs before the first relay drive not covered by design (relay released then), LSI tolerance (watchdog impact record OI-2), HIL evidence | Hardware independent watchdog serviced only on completion of safety processing, window mode (TSR-501) |
  83  | FFI-TM-03 | Busy-waits in code reachable from QM-triggered paths: `while (harness.sbu_adc_lock) {}` in `set_intercept_relay` (`drivers/harness.h:20`), reachable via `0xc5`/`0xdc` in SPI ISR context; `while ((SPI4->SR & SPI_SR_RXP) != 0U)` drain (`llspi.h:12-15`) | Potential blocking; with equal ISR priorities, a wait for a flag that only another ISR clears would deadlock | — | Not analysed | Analyse every busy-wait for termination; bound by timeout; covered by IWDG (TSR-501) |
  84  | FFI-TM-04 | Host command that reinitialises CAN (`0xdc` → `can_init_all()`, `main.c:78`; `0xde` bitrate, `0xe5` loopback → `can_init_all()`) | CAN TX/RX interrupted during re-init; RX timeouts | Re-init resets safety state to not-allowed (`safety.h:458`) | No restriction while in a car mode | Reject these commands while a car safety mode is active (§6, FFI-CM-06…09) |
  85  | FFI-TM-05 | Power-save/stop mode entered while the envelope must run | Envelope stops | Stop mode only from SILENT, asserted (`main.c:353-356`); comment cites STM UM2331 conditions of use (`sys/power_saving.h:5-8`) | `0xe7` power save is accepted in car modes (see FFI-CM-08) | As FFI-CM-08 |
  86  
  87  ### 5.2 On the device (L-DEV): SoC timing vs envelope
  88  
  89  | ID | Interference | Effect | Existing measures | Gap | Required measure |
  90  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  91  | FFI-TM-06 | SoC overload, scheduling delay, GC pause or crash of pandad/card/controlsd | Commands late, frozen or missing; envelope keeps passing the last in-limit frames that arrive | Host: SCHED_FIFO and core affinity, GC disabled (`openpilot/common/realtime.py:41-46`), `Ratekeeper.lagging` average-based (`realtime.py:72-74`); pandad drops `sendcan` older than 1 s (`openpilot/selfdrive/pandad/pandad.cc:79`). MCU: heartbeat loss → SILENT after 5 s with ignition (`main.c:101-103, 193-213`) | No WCET (GAP-23); detection 3–5 s vs SG-01 FTTI ≤ 0.5 s (GAP-06); heartbeat shows pandad liveness, not control-loop liveness (GAP-10). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* authority revoked 0.3 s after the last heartbeat in a car mode (TSR-407, panda `c2e5d93`); SILENT still after 5 s; command-frame freshness (TSR-408) and control-loop evidence (TSR-409) missing | Envelope command-freshness supervision within ≤ 0.3 s (FSR-01.07; TSR-407, TSR-408); heartbeat carries control-loop evidence (TSR-409); pandad age filter 20 ms (TSR-614, QM); host-side freshness check in controlsd (TSR-602, QM) |
  92  | FFI-TM-07 | SoC stops sending but does not reset (frozen kernel) | As FFI-TM-06 | As FFI-TM-06; panda siren 3 s on heartbeat loss (`main.c:198-201`) | Timing (GAP-06). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* authority revoked after 0.3 s (TSR-407); SILENT and siren still after 5 s (TSR-510, TSR-516(a) open). *2026-10-13:* siren at the revocation when authority was granted, ≈ 0.56 s after the last heartbeat, in all builds (TSR-516(a)/(b), panda `c33343b`; WP-S-04 T-07a); not verified on hardware. SILENT still after 5 s (TSR-510) | As FFI-TM-06; FSR-02.05 timing (TSR-516) |
  93  
  94  ## 6. Communication interference
  95  
  96  ### 6.1 SoC → panda data channel (IF-04)
  97  
  98  | ID | Fault | Effect | Existing measures (code) | Gap | Required measure |
  99  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 100  | FFI-CM-01 | Corruption of CAN TX frames on SPI | Corrupted actuation frame | SPI header + payload 8-bit XOR, NACK on mismatch (`drivers/spi.h:97-104, 122-138`); per-packet XOR checked at FDCAN TX (`can_comms.h`, `fdcan.h:100-101`); every frame passes `safety_tx_hook` limits (`can_common.h:162`) | 8-bit XOR has weak residual-error rate; no Hamming-distance argument (GAP-10) | CRC-16 or stronger over header and payload (TSR-410, HWSR-401); residual-error analysis per ISO 26262-5 Annex D |
 101  | FFI-CM-02 | Repetition / stale frames (SoC replays an old buffer) | Last command held; stale steering bias (HE-02.3) | `safety_tx_hook` rate and RT limits bound the change but accept a repeated in-limit value; pandad 1 s age filter is host-side (QM) | **No sequence counter or freshness** (`can_comms.h:27`) (GAP-10) | Sequence counter + freshness window on the SoC→panda command stream, checked by the envelope (TSR-410, TSR-411); stale → SS-L/SS-G |
 102  | FFI-CM-03 | Loss / delay | Actuation stops or is late | Heartbeat (5 s). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* authority revoked 0.3 s after the last heartbeat (TSR-407) | GAP-06. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* TSR-408 (command-frame gap) and TSR-409 missing | As FFI-TM-06 (TSR-407, TSR-408) |
 103  | FFI-CM-04 | Insertion / babbling (SoC floods CAN TX) | Bus load, TX of in-limit but unintended frames | TX whitelist and limits per frame (`safety.h:230-253`); `test_spam_can_buses` (`opendbc_repo/opendbc/safety/tests/common.py:937`); TX queue overflow counted (`can_common.h:165`); SPI CAN NACK when not ready (`spi.h:159-167`) | No TX rate limit per message ID in the envelope; overflow report-only. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* reference build: per-ID sliding 250 ms window, frames beyond the budget rejected, 3 rejections while authority is granted revoke it, reported as `pandaState.txRateExceeded` (TSR-518, opendbc `951b459`); budgets to be confirmed on logs; the shared forwarding/TX queue remains (SDF-05) | TX rate supervision per actuation message ID (TSR-518, added in WP-S-02 after OI-7); verification WP-S-08 VS-SI-11 |
 104  | FFI-CM-05 | Masquerade (SoC sends `0x344` PRE_COLLISION / `0x411` PCS_HUD on bus 0) | Alters stock PCS (SG-07) | `0x283` content-checked (`modes/toyota.h:251-257`) | `0x344`/`0x411` allowed with no content check (**GAP-42**; WP-S-02 NF-07 adds DSU-only IDs). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* `0x344` and the DSU IDs removed from the whitelist (opendbc `e089e85`); `0x411` removed in the reference build only (decision D-1, opendbc `bf24453`); other builds keep `0x411` (TSR-705) | Remove from the reference-configuration whitelist or content-check (TSR-705) |
 105  
 106  ### 6.2 SoC → panda commands that change configuration or mode
 107  
 108  These are the main FFI concern: a QM element can change the configuration and state of the safety element. All are unauthenticated and accepted in any mode unless stated.
 109  
 110  | ID | Command | Effect if sent by a faulty or compromised SoC while a car mode is active | Existing restriction | Gap | Required measure |
 111  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 112  | FFI-CM-06 | `0xdc` set safety mode / param (`main_comms.h:223-225`) | Any mode or param (e.g. different EPS scale, different brand, `ALLOUTPUT` in debug builds) | Mode change resets `controls_allowed` (`safety.h:458`); `ALLOUTPUT` only with `ALLOW_DEBUG` (`safety.h:413-422`); host reads mode from unauthenticated CarParams (`openpilot/selfdrive/pandad/panda_safety.cc:56-70`); host cross-check after 10 s (onboard review, `selfdrived.py:330-339`, QM) | **GAP-09.** No lock; no cross-check in the envelope. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* reference build (`board/reference_lock.h`): only SILENT, NOOUTPUT, ELM327 and TOYOTA/73 accepted; in TOYOTA only NOOUTPUT or SILENT; refusals counted as `pandaState.requestsRejected`, no driver alert (TSR-512) | Lock mode and param for the reference configuration in firmware; accept only Toyota/param 73 or SILENT/NO_OUTPUT (TSR-512, FSR-01.10) |
 113  | FFI-CM-07 | `0xc5` DEBUG drive relay (`main_comms.h:144-147`) | Relay released while the envelope believes it intercepts (camera LKA/ACC reach car → relay-malfunction latch), or relay driven in SILENT (PCS path cut, SG-07) | None. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* refused in a car mode in the reference build (TSR-513, `reference_lock.h`), counted as `requestsRejected`; still accepted outside car modes | **GAP-09** (not gated by `ALLOW_DEBUG`) | Remove from release builds (TSR-513, TSR-506 / HWSR-506c) |
 114  | FFI-CM-08 | `0xe7` set power save (`main_comms.h:258-260` → `sys/power_saving.h:21-50`) | In normal harness orientation, disables CAN interrupts and transceivers for the non-main buses, including the camera-side bus, while the relay stays energised. **By code reading, this stops forwarding of camera messages (including PCS) to the car side** → SG-07 violation; also loss of camera-side RX | Power save is entered automatically only on heartbeat loss together with SILENT (relay released) (`main.c:193-216`) | No restriction in car modes: covered by **NF-05 of WP-S-02** (no GAP ID yet). The SG-07 effect (forwarding stops with relay energised) is not stated in NF-05 (OI-4). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* `0xe7` refused in a car mode in the reference build (TSR-513), counted as `requestsRejected` | Reject `0xe7` (enable) while a car safety mode is active (TSR-513); HIL test [WP-S-08](../03-system/WP-S-08-system-integration-test.md) VS-SI-08 to confirm the effect |
 115  | FFI-CM-09 | `0xe5` CAN loopback (`main_comms.h:249-252`), `0xde`/`0xf9` bitrate (`:234-240, 297+`), `0xdb` OBD CAN mux (`:219-221`), `0xe8` CAN-FD auto (`:262-264`), `0xf1` clear TX queue (`:266-276`) | Actuation frames stop reaching the vehicle, or bus mis-configured. Mostly fail-silent (EPS timeout), but without envelope-side warning: unannounced loss (SG-02/SG-06) | None in car modes. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* `0xe5`, `0xde`, `0xf9`, `0xdb`, `0xe8`, `0xf1` (and `0xfc`, `0xe6`) refused in a car mode in the reference build (TSR-513), counted as `requestsRejected`; no envelope-side warning | **NF-05 of WP-S-02** (no GAP ID yet; OI-4) | Reject while a car safety mode is active, or force the envelope to SS-S with siren on such a change (TSR-513) |
 116  | FFI-CM-10 | `0xd8` reset MCU, `0xd1` softloader / bootloader (`main_comms.h:165-218`) | MCU restarts; relay released during reset (expected, HWSR-502); softloader path enables reflash (FFI-SP-05) | `0xd1` param 0 only with `ALLOW_DEBUG`. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* `0xd8` and `0xd1` param 1 refused in a car mode in the reference build (TSR-513, panda `7e2d148`) | Softloader allowed in release (GAP-24); no warning to the driver from the envelope on SoC-commanded reset. *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* closed for car modes in the reference build; outside car modes and in other builds unchanged; `0xc0`, `0xb0`, `0xb1`, `0xf6` still to be confirmed (WP-W-02 OI-5) | Refuse while controls allowed; `0xd1`/1 and `0xd8` are not in the TSR-513 list (OI-7); reset path must release the relay (TSR-706) |
 117  | FFI-CM-11 | `0xf3` heartbeat with `engaged` flag (`main_comms.h:278-285`) | A QM fault can keep sending heartbeats while the control loop is dead; `heartbeat_engaged = true` keeps `controls_allowed` | Heartbeat engaged mismatch clears authority after 3 s (`main.c:182-189`) | Heartbeat proves pandad liveness only (GAP-10) | Heartbeat content bound to the control-loop output (control-loop evidence, TSR-409) |
 118  | FFI-CM-12 | **Boot-pin and reset control** (`STM_BOOT0`, `STM_RST_N`) driven by the SoC (`openpilot/common/hardware/comma/hardware.py:401-419`); pandad recovery flashes a development bootstub when firmware does not boot (`openpilot/selfdrive/pandad/pandad.py:34-39`) | MCU forced into ROM bootloader; any firmware can be written (no RDP/WRP, `stm32h7/llflash.h:14`). The envelope can be replaced by a QM element | During ROM bootloader the GPIOs are at reset state, so the relay is expected released (safe direction, HWSR-502c) | **GAP-38**, GAP-24 | RDP level and WRP on bootstub and application (TSR-511, TSR-514); release firmware signature with modern algorithm; no development bootstub in the recovery path of release builds; consider a hardware interlock that prevents BOOT0 assertion while ignition is on (WP-H-02) |
 119  | FFI-CM-13 | `0xf6` siren enable (`main_comms.h:287-289`) | Spurious alarm (startle); or SoC suppresses nothing (siren on heartbeat loss is set internally, `main.c:198-201`; since panda `c33343b` also `drivers/heartbeat_siren.h`) | — | Startle only (QM) | None required for ASIL; HMI review in WP-C-08 |
 120  | FFI-CM-14 | `0xdf` alternative experience, `0xf8` disable heartbeat (`main_comms.h:242-247, 291-295`) | Would alter brake/gas disengage behaviour or disable heartbeat | **Accepted only outside car safety modes** (`is_car_safety_mode` checks); heartbeat disable forced off in car modes (`main.c:165-167`). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* `0xdf` refused in every mode in the reference build, alternative experience stays 0 (SWSR-512a); assumption of use AOU-14 | — | Existing restriction to be verified by test (WP-S-08 VS-SI-04) |
 121  
 122  ### 6.3 Vehicle CAN → envelope (external interference on the RX side)
 123  
 124  Not QM-to-ASIL interference in the strict sense, but the same communication fault model applies and the envelope must detect it.
 125  
 126  | ID | Fault | Existing measure | Gap | Required measure |
 127  |---|---|---|---|---|
 128  | FFI-CM-15 | Repetition / stuck frames on `0x1D2`, `0x226`, `0x260`, `0xAA` | Checksum on `0x1D2`, `0x260` (8-bit additive, `modes/toyota.h:65-72`); timeouts ≤ ≈2 s (`safety.h:321-344`). *Status 2026-10-12 (`bf6ce8c`):* 5-period timeout evaluated at 100 Hz, ≤ 0.16 s (`0x262` ≈ 0.21 s in the reference build; WP-S-04 T-01); reference build adds `0x262`/`0x1D3` with checksum and the CRUISE_ACTIVE vs MAIN_ON cross-check (TSR-405(c)) | No counters; `0x226`/`0xAA` no checksum (GAP-01); slow timeout (GAP-06) | E2E strategy for each gating/limiting signal (plausibility cross-checks where the OEM provides no counter: e.g. wheel speed vs EPS, brake vs decel), timeout ≤ 0.3 s (TSR-401…TSR-406, FSR-01.06) |
 129  | FFI-CM-16 | **Forwarding decided before RX validation** of the same frame (`drivers/fdcan.h:199` before `:221`) | Static forwarding block list (`safety.h:270-281`) | GAP-11 | Acceptable for camera→car PCS frames (forwarding must not depend on validation, SG-07); confirm no blocked/intercepted message can be forwarded (TSR-704, TSR-707); test WP-S-08 VS-SI-20 |
 130  
 131  ### 6.4 Interference on the HMI and status path (from the FSC v0.2)
 132  
 133  [WP-C-04](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md) v0.2 added FSR-02.10 (envelope blocking while engaged) and FSR-02.11 (HMI path failure). Both depend on SoC processes that share the SoC with non-safety processes. Everything here is QM, so this is not QM-to-ASIL interference; it matters because the SoC side of these FSRs is B-supported by the envelope (TSR-516 siren, TSR-309 cluster state), and that support works only if the SoC failure is detected or the SoC stops.
 134  
 135  | ID | Fault | Effect | Existing measures (code) | Gap | Required measure |
 136  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
 137  | FFI-CM-17 | Non-safety SoC processes (logging, encoding, upload, streaming, `manage_athenad`) starve `soundd` or the UI render loop of CPU, GPU or audio buffers | Take-over request late or not perceived while control continues (FSR-02.11, H-07) | `ui` runs with real-time priority `Priority.CTRL_HIGH` on core 0 (`openpilot/selfdrive/ui/ui.py:19`); `selfdrived` and `controlsd` likewise (`selfdrived.py:591`, `controlsd.py:237`). `soundd` sets no real-time priority (`openpilot/selfdrive/ui/soundd.py`; started from `process_config.py:90`) | No liveness supervision of the render loop or audio stream; `soundd` scheduled as a normal process | HMI liveness heartbeats with immediate disable and siren take-over (TSR-622); `soundd` real-time priority or a worst-case load test (TSR-627) |
 138  | FFI-CM-18 | SoC software sets the alert volume too low (ambient-scaled volume; `MIN_VOLUME = 0.1`, `soundd.py:20, 159-160`) | Alert not audible (FSR-02.11) | Critical alerts ramp to maximum volume over 4 s (`ALERT_RAMP_TIME`, `soundd.py:21`) | No defined audible minimum; ramp time not tied to the warning budget | Audible minimum while the item can engage, else no-entry (TSR-623); ramp time within the SG-02/SG-06 warning budget |
 139  | FFI-CM-19 | `pandad` delays or freezes `pandaStates` while the panda keeps blocking authority | SoC does not see envelope blocking (FSR-02.10) | `pandaStates` alive limit 1 s (WP-S-03 §9); `controlsMismatch` ≈2 s | Detection depends on the envelope's own report ([WP-A-03](WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md) DFI-24) | Stale `pandaStates` treated as a mismatch, and a vehicle-response check (TSR-626) |

 163  |---|---|---|
 164  | L-MCU spatial | **Not shown** | No MPU; QM-triggered paths execute in the same address space; SPI length unbounded (mitigated by memory-bank separation, unconfirmed) |

 166  | L-DEV communication (data) | **Partially shown** | TX hook limits every frame; but no freshness/sequence and weak checksum |
 167  | L-DEV communication (commands) | **Not shown** at `8b8c6ae`. *2026-10-12:* **Partially shown in the reference build** | QM SoC can set any mode, drive the relay, disable camera-side forwarding, reset or reflash the MCU. *2026-10-12:* in the reference build the mode lock and car-mode request gating refuse these (TSR-512/513); boot control (FFI-CM-12) open; other builds unchanged; not verified on hardware |

Pins on 2026-10-14: opendbc `9a13f60`, panda `a55bdfc`; §3.2 was not re-inspected at those pins except the change-record count |
  15  
  16  > [!IMPORTANT]
  17  > This document describes **how** the software-level lifecycle will be implemented once its prerequisites are mature. It records no completed activity. Nothing here is evidence of ISO 26262-6 conformity, ASIL capability or safety. The drafts in [`05-software/`](../05-software/), and the safety-mechanism changes integrated under change records, are pre-entry engineering (§2.3), not Part 6 execution.
  18  
  19  ## 1. Purpose and scope
  20  
  21  LionDriver already has draft software work products WP-W-01…WP-W-11, written by back-filling from the inherited openpilot, opendbc and panda code ([WP-M-01 T-03](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring)). What it does not yet have is a controlled way to run the ISO 26262-6 lifecycle on them. That means records rather than prose, enforced trace links, evidence bound to exact builds and configurations, review controls, and a link into the Living Safety Case.
  22  
  23  This specification fixes that blueprint before work starts. It:
  24  
  25  1. sets the engineering principles that govern the software lifecycle (§2),
  26  2. defines entry criteria and records a preliminary readiness snapshot of the prerequisites (§3),
  27  3. specifies each Clause 5–11 activity against the existing repository (§4),
  28  4. defines interfaces to the supporting processes and other parts of the standard (§5),
  29  5. specifies reuse assessment (§6), configuration-specific assurance (§7), traceability (§8), Living Safety Case integration (§9), repository layout (§10), the register extension (§11), automation (§12) and the dashboard (§13),
  30  6. sets acceptance criteria (§14) and the phase sequence (§15),
  31  7. lists what may and may not be done before activation (§16).
  32  
  33  Out of scope: the content of software safety requirements, architecture, analyses or verification results. Those come from the activities this document plans. They are not produced by this document.
  34  
  35  The licensed text of ISO 26262 is authoritative. No standard text is reproduced here. Clause references are to the 2018 edition and must be checked against the licensed copy before use in an audit.
  36  
  37  ## 2. Engineering principles
  38  
  39  ### 2.1 Evidence, not documentation theatre
  40  
  41  | Rule | Consequence in this repository |
  42  |---|---|
  43  | A template is not evidence | Templates live in a template location (§10) and carry no status. A work product copied from a template starts at `Planned` or `Skeleton` |
  44  | A passing CI run is not approval | CI results are evidence inputs. They never change a `review_state` ([`case/README.md` §4](../case/README.md)) |
  45  | A test is not verification of a requirement | A requirement is verified only when a reviewed verification record links that requirement to a result on an identified build and configuration (§8) |
  46  | No invented numbers | Coverage, timing, resource figures and metrics appear only when measured, with the build, tool version and command recorded. Otherwise the record says `Not established` |
  47  | Drafts stay drafts | AI- or tool-generated content is marked as such and stays below `Approved` until a named human reviewer accepts it (§2.5) |
  48  
  49  ### 2.2 Status vocabulary
  50  
  51  The specification calls for more statuses than the current work product convention ([`assurance/README.md`](../README.md): Skeleton, Draft, In review, Approved, Baselined). The proposed mapping below is adopted in Phase B (§15). It is not applied to existing documents yet.
  52  
  53  | Specification status | Current convention | Requirement `status` ([WP-P-06](../07-supporting/WP-P-06-requirements-management-traceability.md)) | Case record ([schema](../case/schema/case-schema.json)) | Meaning |
  54  |---|---|---|---|---|
  55  | Planned | (none; propose adding) | — | — | Identified in the register; no content |
  56  | Draft | Skeleton / Draft | `proposed` | `draft` | Content exists; not reviewed |
  57  | In development | Draft | `agreed` → `implemented` | `in-progress` | Being worked against an approved input |
  58  | In review | In review | — | `review-required` / `in-review` | Verification review open |
  59  | Approved | Approved | `agreed` | `accepted` | Review passed at the independence level required by [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) |
  60  | Verified | (none; propose adding) | `verified` | evidence `available` + review `accepted` | Approved, and its verification records are accepted for a named baseline and configuration |
  61  | Superseded | (none; propose adding) | `withdrawn` (requirements) | baseline `superseded` | Replaced; kept for history |
  62  | Not applicable | N/A (tailored) | `withdrawn` | `withdrawn` | Tailored out, with rationale ([WP-M-01 §5](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring)) |
  63  
  64  Allowed transitions are checked by tooling (§12). Leaving out a state is not allowed. For example, a record cannot go from `Draft` to `Verified`.
  65  
  66  ### 2.3 Pre-entry engineering track
  67  
  68  Software-level engineering is already under way before activation. At `682f184`:
  69  
  70  - WP-W-01…WP-W-11 are written, and WP-W-02 holds 134 SWSRs that refine the WP-S-02 TSRs. They are mirrored in [`trace/items/swsr.yaml`](../trace/items/swsr.yaml) and checked in CI (K3, K4, X3; [`trace/README.md` §6](../trace/README.md#6-consistency-checks));
  71  - safety mechanisms derived from proposed TSRs and SWSRs (hardware watchdog, RX timeout, TX whitelist, safety-mode lock, heartbeat, IRQ priorities, EPS fault, cruise-main check, TX rate supervision) are integrated in the reference build. Each has a CT-1 change record in [`07-supporting/impact/`](../07-supporting/impact/README.md), and none is verified on hardware or independently reviewed;
  72  - a design note ([DN-01](../05-software/design-notes/DN-01-tx-rate-supervision.md)) and a HIL bench plan ([`hil-bench/`](../05-software/hil-bench/README.md), D-04) exist.
  73  
  74  This work is legitimate engineering, and it is what makes activation possible. It is governed by these rules until D-14. The project maintainer confirmed them as written on 2026-10-12 ([RR-2026-009](../05-software/reviews/RR-2026-009.md) POL-1, decided by delegation "go with your recommendation"):
  75  
  76  | Rule | Consequence |
  77  |---|---|
  78  | P-01 | Every SWSR keeps `status: proposed` until its parent TSR is approved (CR-05) **and** the SWSR has a recorded review. Being implemented in a build is shown in the `Baseline` column (`Impl`, `Partial`, `Not impl`), not by moving `status` to `implemented` |
  79  | P-02 | A code change in an SR-A or SR-Q path needs a CT-1 change record with an impact analysis ([WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md)), whether or not Part 6 is active |
  80  | P-03 | Text describing behaviour names the pins it was read from. When a change is integrated, the work products that describe the old behaviour are updated in the same pull request or carry an open item that names the change |
  81  | P-04 | Work products and change records do not describe pre-entry work as Part 6 completion, verification or approval |
  82  | P-05 | At Phase A, every pre-entry artifact is rechecked against the entry baseline and becomes an **input** to Clauses 6–11. Nothing carries over as completed evidence |
  83  
  84  ### 2.4 Upstream compatibility
  85  
  86  LionDriver keeps upstream openpilot behaviour and the upstream vehicle list unless a safety requirement forces a change. Part 6 work must not restructure application code for documentation convenience. After activation, code changes come only from approved software safety requirements, through [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) change requests. Before activation they follow the pre-entry rules of §2.3 (CT-1 change record with impact analysis). Each change records its effect on upstream functionality and on configurations outside the claimed scope. Supporting a vehicle is not the same as having assurance evidence for it (§7).
  87  
  88  ### 2.5 Human-controlled decisions
  89  
  90  | Activity | Automation may | Only an authorized human may |
  91  |---|---|---|
  92  | Requirements | Check syntax, attributes, uniqueness, links; propose wording | Accept, allocate an ASIL, approve |
  93  | Trace links | Propose candidate links, flagged `proposed-by: tool` | Record a link as approved |
  94  | Analyses (FMEA, FTA, DFA, FFI) | Generate worksheets, check completeness | Rate, conclude, approve |
  95  | Verification | Run tests, collect results, compute coverage | Judge pass/fail of a requirement, accept anomalies, approve reports |
  96  | Safety case | Compute status, flag stale evidence | Accept a claim, change a review state |
  97  
  98  Reviewers and independence levels are those of [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) and [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md).
  99  
 100  ## 3. Entry criteria and readiness
 101  
 102  ### 3.1 Entry criteria
 103  
 104  Part 6 implementation (Phases B–F, §15) starts only when the project maintainer has recorded, as decision D-14, approval of an **entry baseline**. The entry baseline is a git revision plus the approved versions of the work products below, with any exception justified in writing.
 105  
 106  | # | Prerequisite | Work product | Minimum state for entry | Confirmation measure |
 107  |---|---|---|---|---|
 108  | E-01 | Safety plan, tailoring | [WP-M-02](WP-M-02-safety-plan.md), [WP-M-01 §5](WP-M-01-assurance-strategy.md#5-tailoring) | Approved | CR-03 passed |
 109  | E-02 | Item definition | [WP-C-01](../02-concept/WP-C-01-item-definition.md) | Approved | — |
 110  | E-03 | HARA and safety goals | [WP-C-03](../02-concept/WP-C-03-hara.md) | Approved | CR-02 passed (I3) |
 111  | E-04 | Functional safety concept, FSRs | [WP-C-04](../02-concept/WP-C-04-functional-safety-concept.md) | Approved | CR-04 passed |
 112  | E-05 | TSRs, technical safety concept, system architecture | [WP-S-02](../03-system/WP-S-02-technical-safety-requirements.md), [WP-S-03](../03-system/WP-S-03-technical-safety-concept-architecture.md) | Approved for the software-allocated TSRs | CR-05 passed |
 113  | E-06 | Timing budget (FTTI) | [WP-S-04](../03-system/WP-S-04-timing-ftti-budget.md) | Approved, with measured values where software timing is credited | Part of CR-05 |
 114  | E-07 | HSI | [WP-S-05](../03-system/WP-S-05-hsi-specification.md) | Approved | Part of CR-05 |
 115  | E-08 | ASIL allocation and decomposition | [WP-A-01](../08-analyses/WP-A-01-asil-decomposition.md) | Approved | Part of CR-06 (system) |
 116  | E-09 | System FFI and DFA | [WP-A-02](../08-analyses/WP-A-02-coexistence-freedom-from-interference.md), [WP-A-03](../08-analyses/WP-A-03-dependent-failure-analysis.md) | Approved at system level | CR-06 (system) |
 117  | E-10 | Configuration management | [WP-P-01](../07-supporting/WP-P-01-configuration-management-plan.md) | Approved and in use | — |
 118  | E-11 | Change management | [WP-P-02](../07-supporting/WP-P-02-change-management.md) | Approved and in use | — |
 119  | E-12 | Requirements management and traceability | [WP-P-06](../07-supporting/WP-P-06-requirements-management-traceability.md), [`trace/`](../trace/README.md) | Approved; TSR records present in `trace/` | — |
 120  | E-13 | Verification and review procedure | [WP-P-05](../07-supporting/WP-P-05-verification-review-procedure.md) | Approved | — |
 121  | E-14 | Tool classification | [WP-P-07](../07-supporting/WP-P-07-tool-classification-qualification.md) | Classification done for the software toolchain (qualification may follow in Phase D) | — |
 122  | E-15 | Reviewer availability | [WP-M-04](WP-M-04-organization-competence-safety-culture.md), [WP-M-06](WP-M-06-confirmation-measures-plan.md) | At least one named reviewer other than the author for I1 software reviews | — |
 123  
 124  Missing upstream inputs are **not** to be filled in by the software lifecycle. If a TSR, ASIL or allocation is missing, the gap is recorded as an open action against the system-level owner. No SWSR is invented to cover it.
 125
